### [1] [On the Energy Minimizing Problem in Plasma Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33040)
*Maurice de Gosson*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper addresses the problem of finding minimum energy states of plasma by proposing a quantum-mechanical formulation—using the Wigner transform and symplectic geometry of Gaussian states—to overcome topological difficulties in Qin's classical 'Gromov ground state' approach.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve topological difficulties arising in Hong Qin's classical symplectic-rigidity-based 'Gromov ground states' for plasma minimum energy configurations, which remain constrained within the Hamiltonian framework and fail to fully capture non-classical or singular behavior.

Method: Introduces a quantum-mechanical reformulation of the plasma energy minimization problem using the Wigner quasiprobability distribution and exploits the symplectic geometry inherent in Gaussian quantum states to define physically admissible ground states.

Result: Provides a well-posed, topologically robust framework for minimum energy plasma states that transcends limitations of both Gardner’s volume-preserving and Qin’s classical symplectic approaches; yields a geometrically natural class of quantum ground states compatible with uncertainty and non-commutativity.

Conclusion: The quantum-symplectic approach via Wigner transforms and Gaussian state geometry resolves foundational obstructions in classical plasma energy minimization, offering a more complete and mathematically consistent foundation for fusion-relevant equilibrium theory.

Abstract: A fundamental question in plasma physics and its applications to fusion theory is to find minimum energy stares of plasma.. Hong Qin has suggested the use of what he calls "Gromov ground states" which take into account the phenomenon of symplectic rigidity, as opposed to Gardner's classical approach which only use volume-preserving diffeomorphisms. Howev4er Qin's approach leads to topological difficulties because his constructions remain in the classical (Hamiltonian) domain. We circumvent these difficulties by introducing a quantum-mechanical formulation of the problem, based on the Wigner transform and the symplectic geometry of Gaussian state

</details>


### [2] [Dynamics of two-dimensional Bose gases in the focusing case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33212)
*Lukas Reichmann*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves propagation of Bose-Einstein condensation and norm approximation for 2D Bose gas dynamics with singular, possibly weakly attractive interactions, under broad interaction scalings (polynomial or exponential) and general quadratic time-dependent external potentials.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend rigorous mean-field and condensation results to 2D Bose gases with highly singular and scalable interactions—including weak attraction—and time-dependent, physically realistic external potentials.

Method: Functional analytic and PDE techniques adapted to many-body quantum dynamics; use of coherent states, Fock space methods, and energy estimates tailored to singular two-dimensional interactions and nontrivial scaling regimes.

Result: Rigorous proof of propagation of Bose--Einstein condensation and a norm-approximation of the many-body Schrödinger evolution by a Bogoliubov-type dynamics, valid for polynomial scaling N^β (β > 0) and exponential scaling e^{N^κ} (0 < κ < 1/4), under general quadratic time-dependent external potentials bounded below.

Conclusion: The analysis establishes robustness of mean-field and condensation behavior in 2D Bose gases far beyond standard dilute limits, accommodating strong singularities, attraction, and flexible interaction scalings—significantly widening the scope of validity for effective dynamics in low-dimensional quantum many-body systems.

Abstract: We prove the propagation of Bose--Einstein condensation and a norm approximation for the dynamics of a Bose gas with localized initial data in two dimensions with singular interactions that may have a small attractive part. We allow the scaling of the interaction to be any polynomial scaling $N^\beta$ for $0<\beta<\infty$ and even exponential scaling $e^{N^\kappa}$ for $0<\kappa<1/4$. Our approach covers external potentials that are time-dependent, at most quadratic, and bounded below, including the situation without an external potential.

</details>


### [3] [Ground-state spaces of generalized stabilizer codes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33221)
*Cathy Cheng,Nigel Higson,Danilo Polo Ojito,Emil Prodan*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a C*-algebraic framework for generalized stabilizer quantum models on non-compact manifolds, linking ground-state spaces to Lagrangian extensions of isotropic subgroups in a symplectic Pauli module; it yields explicit extremal ground states, sector decompositions, and proves uniqueness and spectral gap for frustration-free ground states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously characterize ground-state spaces of generalized stabilizer models on non-compact manifolds of arbitrary dimension and topology—beyond standard lattice-based or compact settings—using operator-algebraic methods.

Method: Constructs a twisted group C*-algebra realization of the quantum spin algebra over an infinite discrete symplectic Pauli module; establishes a correspondence between stabilizer models and isotropic subgroups; uses Lagrangian extensions to define C*-diagonals and Weyl groupoids encoding dynamics via continuous 1-cocycles.

Result: Explicit family of extremal ground states with sector decomposition; complete ground-state space characterization when sector space is T₀; proof of unique frustration-free ground state with spectral gap for all Lagrangian stabilizer models.

Conclusion: The C*-algebraic framework unifies and generalizes analysis of stabilizer models—including higher abelian quantum doubles, homological codes, and broad Lagrangian models—providing rigorous, dimension-agnostic characterization of ground-state structure and stability.

Abstract: In this paper, we determine the complete ground-state spaces for a broad class of generalized stabilizer quantum models defined over CW-decompositions of non-compact manifolds of arbitrary dimensions and topological type. We introduce a $C^*$-algebraic framework in which the quantum spin algebra $\A$ is realized as the twisted group $C^*$-algebra of an infinite discrete Pauli module $P$ endowed with a symplectic structure. We establish a correspondence between stabilizer models and isotropic subgroups of $P$: Every Lagrangian extension of such a subgroup determines a $C^*$-diagonal of $\A$, whose Weyl groupoid encodes the dynamics through a continuous 1-cocycle. This realization yields an explicit family of extremal ground-states for every admissible Lagrangian model, together with their decomposition into sectors. When the sector space is $T_0$, this gives a complete characterization of the ground-state spaces. We also prove that every Lagrangian stabilizer model admits a unique frustration-free ground state with a spectral gap. Our framework applies in arbitrary dimension, including higher abelian quantum doubles, homological codes, and a broad class of stabilizer models.

</details>


### [4] [Partial Airy ideals and coloured blobbed topological recursion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33232)
*Gaëtan Borot,Vincent Bouchard,Swagata S. Datta*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces partial Airy ideals—underdetermined linear PDE systems—with unique exponential-type solutions (partition functions) up to initial conditions; constructs them from spectral curves with colour data via twisted free-field realizations of $\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{gl}_r)$-algebras at self-dual level; derives coloured loop equations for correlators; and develops coloured blobbed topological recursion—a generalized residue-based recursion encompassing Borot–Shadrin’s blobbed TR as a special case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize Airy structures and topological recursion beyond the standard (fully determined) setting, enabling treatment of broader classes of spectral curves enriched with 'colour' data, and to unify existing recursion formalisms—including blobbed topological recursion—within a coherent algebraic–differential framework rooted in $\mathcal{W}$-algebra representation theory.

Method: Constructs partial Airy ideals using twisted free-field representations of $\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{gl}_r)$-algebras at self-dual level; derives differential constraints on partition functions and translates them into correlator-level 'coloured loop equations'; establishes equivalence between these constraints and a recursive residue calculus—the 'coloured blobbed topological recursion'—via analytic continuation and local spectral curve geometry.

Result: Existence and uniqueness (up to initial data) of exponential partition functions for partial Airy ideals; explicit construction of such ideals from coloured spectral curves; derivation of coloured loop equations; formulation and proof of coloured blobbed topological recursion as a universal solution mechanism; recovery of Borot–Shadrin’s blobbed TR as a degenerate case.

Conclusion: Partial Airy ideals provide a robust algebraic foundation for generalizing topological recursion to underdetermined settings, with colour-enriched spectral curves and $\mathcal{W}$-algebraic structures playing essential roles; coloured blobbed topological recursion unifies and extends prior recursion schemes, offering new tools for enumerative geometry, integrable systems, and quantum field theory.

Abstract: We define and study the notion of partial Airy ideals, which are certain linear systems of PDEs that may contain less equations than variables. We show that they admit a unique solution of exponential type (the partition function) modulo a choice of initial condition. We construct a large class of examples of partial Airy ideals attached to spectral curves using twisted free-field representations of $\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{gl}_r)$-algebras at self-dual level. This generalizes the results of Bouchard- Borot-Chidambaram-Creutzig-Noshchenko by significantly enlarging the set of admissible spectral curves, provided they come with additional data that we call colour. We show that the differential constraints on the partition function are equivalent to a set of equations on the associated correlators, which we call coloured loop equations. We find a recursive residue formulation for the solutions of those equations, the coloured blobbed topological recursion}, which is a generalization of the currently known forms of topological recursion. It includes the blobbed topological recursion of Borot-Shadrin as a special case, giving it a natural interpretation within the formalism of partial Airy ideals.

</details>


### [5] [A non-equilibrium thermodynamics formulation of shear set-points in adaptively remodeling vascular networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33250)
*Enrique Hernández-Lemus,Daniel Pérez-Calixto*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper reformulates blood vessel remodeling as a non-equilibrium thermodynamic gradient flow, linking Onsager linear response, Rayleigh dissipation, structural mobility, and fluctuation theorems; it predicts anticorrelated radius fluctuations (testable Onsager reciprocity), detects housekeeping entropy from non-reciprocal signaling, and shows loop topology statistics are non-universal under varying detection thresholds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a rigorous non-equilibrium thermodynamic foundation for vascular adaptation—bridging metabolic constraints, shear-stress homeostasis, and stochastic structural dynamics—beyond prior equilibrium or purely mechanical models.

Method: Analytical thermodynamic modeling: (1) framing flow-remodeling as an Onsager linear system with Rayleigh dissipation functional; (2) deriving structural mobility from radius fluctuations and maintenance cost scaling; (3) enforcing exact metabolic budget via time-dependent Lagrange multiplier; (4) incorporating fluid-structural coupling via symmetric non-diagonal mobility; (5) applying stochastic thermodynamics to derive integral fluctuation theorem and housekeeping entropy; (6) constructing topology partition function with loop number as order parameter and testing numerically.

Result: Identifies structural mobility inversely proportional to vessel maintenance cost; obtains closed-form time-dependent metabolic multiplier; predicts anticorrelated radius fluctuations between neighboring vessels as testable signature of Onsager reciprocity; shows non-reciprocal signaling breaks time-reversal symmetry and generates measurable housekeeping entropy; demonstrates loop-count statistics are non-universal—dependent on detection threshold and not collapsing onto single effective temperature curve.

Conclusion: Vascular remodeling is a bona fide non-equilibrium thermodynamic process governed by gradient descent on a Rayleigh potential, with experimentally accessible signatures (fluctuation anticorrelations, housekeeping entropy) and fundamental limits to topological universality.

Abstract: Blood vessels remodel in response to wall shear stress while the network operates under a metabolic maintenance budget. For a power-law maintenance cost, minimizing dissipation at fixed budget yields a local shear set-point, and a purely local adaptation rule drives the network towards this optimum. Here we give this result a non-equilibrium thermodynamic formulation. Blood flow is an exactly linear Onsager system, and the slow remodeling of vessel radii is a gradient flow generated by a Rayleigh dissipation functional. This identifies the structural mobility, which scales inversely with the maintenance cost of each vessel and can be measured from radius fluctuations. Enforcing the budget exactly requires a time-dependent metabolic multiplier, which we obtain in closed form; with a fixed multiplier the budget is violated by up to one third during transients. The fluid displaced by remodeling vessels couples flow and structure through a symmetric, non-diagonal mobility, so that Onsager reciprocity becomes a testable statement: neighbouring vessels should show anticorrelated radius fluctuations. With noise, the structural sector obeys a second law and an integral fluctuation theorem. Non-reciprocal signaling between vessels, such as upstream conducted responses, then breaks the time-reversal symmetry of radius fluctuations and produces housekeeping entropy, detectable from time series of realistic length. Finally, we construct a partition function over network topologies with the number of loops as order parameter. Numerical tests show that loop counts do not collapse onto a single curve against an effective temperature, and that they depend strongly on the detection threshold.

</details>


### [6] [A Twisted Affine Interpolation of the $\mathbb{Z}_2^2$-graded Integrable Super-Liouville and super-Sinh-Gordon Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33485)
*Naruhiko Aizawa,Ren Ito,Luiza Miranda,Francesco Toppan*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs 2D, Z₂²-graded integrable Toda field theories using infinite twisted affine color Lie superalgebras; it unifies super-Liouville and super-Sinh-Gordon models via a Z₂²-graded extension of ŝoŝp(2|2)⁽²⁾ and extends key integrability frameworks (soldering, Lax pair, Bäcklund); Hamiltonian reduction yields three inequivalent Z₂²-graded superVirasoro algebras.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the construction of Z₂²-graded integrable Toda field theories from finite to infinite (twisted affine) color Lie superalgebras, enabling unified description of known supersymmetric integrable models and uncovering new graded symmetry structures.

Method: Systematic construction of Z₂²-graded integrable models based on twisted affine color Lie superalgebras; application of Polyakov's soldering, Leznov–Savel'ev Lax pair (zero-curvature) formalism, and Bäcklund transformations; Hamiltonian reduction of Z₂²-graded ŝoŝp(2|2)⁽²⁾ WZNW models.

Result: A Z₂²-graded extension of ŝoŝp(2|2)⁽²⁾ interpolates between super-Liouville and super-Sinh-Gordon theories; three distinct Z₂²-graded superVirasoro subalgebras—N=2, (1,3/2,3/2,2), and (3/2,3/2,2,2)—emerge from Hamiltonian reduction.

Conclusion: The framework successfully generalizes integrable Toda theory to infinite Z₂²-graded color Lie superalgebras, revealing rich algebraic structure and unifying previously disparate supersymmetric integrable models under a common graded symmetry principle.

Abstract: This paper is a third one in a series devoted to the construction of $2D$, $\mathbb{Z}_2^2$-graded, integrable Toda field theories. The first paper introduced parabosonic models obtained from Rittenberg-Wyler color Lie algebras; the second paper explained how to deal with parafermions (the $\mathbb{Z}_2^2$-graded super-Liouville theory) from finite Rittenberg-Wyler color Lie superalgebras. This new paper extends the construction to the infinite case of (twisted) affine color Lie superalgebras. A $\mathbb{Z}_2^2$-graded extension of ${\widehat{\mathfrak{osp}(2|2)^{(2)}}}$ allows to interpolate the super-Liouville and the super-Sinh-Gordon models, recovered as special limits. The $\mathbb{Z}_2^2$-graded extensions of three frameworks are here discussed: the Polyakov's soldering, the Leznov-Savel'ev Lax pair (zero-curvature) formulation and the Bäcklund transformations. As a byproduct of the construction, the Hamiltonian reduction from the $\mathbb{Z}_2^2$-graded ${\widehat{\mathfrak{osp(2|2)}^{(2)}}}$ WZNW models induces a $\mathbb{Z}_2^2$-graded color Lie superalgebra which admits three inequivalent superVirasoro subalgebras; they are the ${\cal N}=2$ superVirasoro, the $(1,\frac{3}{2},\frac{3}{2},2)$ $\mathbb{Z}_2^2$-graded superVirasoro and the $(\frac{3}{2},\frac{3}{2},2,2)$ $\mathbb{Z}_2^2$-graded superVirasoro.

</details>


### [7] [Admissible Subspaces for the Collective Haar Twirl: Schur-Weyl Classification and Heredity under Tensor-Factor Reduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33553)
*Sayantan Ganguly*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper classifies all subspaces of the t-fold tensor product space H_d^{⊗t} that support quantum private broadcasting (QPB) by leveraging Schur-Weyl duality and the Haar twirl; it characterizes necessary and sufficient conditions via fixed reduced states on multiplicity spaces, constructs explicit multi-block QPB schemes, and analyzes heredity properties (privacy, design, QPB) under recipient deletion—especially for symmetric/antisymmetric towers and canonical Schur-Weyl carriers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve an open question from Broadbent et al. on which subspaces beyond Sym^t(H_d) support quantum private broadcasting, and to unify and generalize the structural requirements for QPB using representation-theoretic tools.

Method: Applies Schur-Weyl duality to decompose H_d^{⊗t} into irreducible representations V_λ ⊗ W_λ; analyzes the collective Haar twirl T^{(t)} as a quantum channel; derives necessary/sufficient conditions for T^{(t)} to act as a replacement channel (i.e., output independent of input); reformulates conditions as Knill-Laflamme-type criteria for the complementary channel; introduces and studies heredity under deletion via branching rules on the Young graph and Peter-Weyl coefficient spaces.

Result: A complete classification: a subspace S ⊆ H_d^{⊗t} supports exact QPB iff (i) its projection onto each Schur-Weyl isotypic component V_λ ⊗ W_λ has constant weight (independent of encoded state), and (ii) within each active component, the reduced state on the multiplicity space W_λ is fixed. This yields explicit two- and multi-block constructions. Additionally, establishes precise heredity thresholds: symmetric tower is fully QPB-hereditary; antisymmetric tower is privacy-hereditary always and design-hereditary only when s ≤ (d+1)/2; canonical carriers obey multiplicity-free branching constraints.

Conclusion: Schur-Weyl duality provides a complete and operational framework for QPB: the Haar twirl’s replacement-channel behavior is equivalent to QPB feasibility, and heredity is governed by combinatorial and representation-theoretic constraints on Young diagrams and multiplicity spaces.

Abstract: Quantum private broadcasting (\textsf{QPB}) asks whether a quantum message can be encrypted once, broadcast to several recipients, and decrypted locally while the joint ciphertext reveals no plaintext information. Broadbent, González-Guillén, and Schuknecht showed that the full tensor-power space \(\mathcal H_d^{\otimes t}\) is generally unsuitable for \textsf{QPB}, identified \(\mathrm{Sym}^t(\mathcal H_d)\) as a natural plaintext space, introduced symmetric unitary \(t\)-designs, and asked which other subspaces support private broadcast. Using Schur-Weyl duality, we classify all subspaces \(\mathcal S\subseteq\mathcal H_d^{\otimes t}\) on which the collective Haar twirl \(\mathcal T^{(t)}\) is a replacement channel. Precisely, every Schur-Weyl block weight must be independent of the encoded state, and in each active block \(V_\lambda\otimes W_\lambda\), the reduced state on the multiplicity space \(W_\lambda\) must be fixed. This yields explicit two-block and general multiblock constructions and realizes the classification as the Schur-Weyl form of the Knill-Laflamme conditions for a complementary channel of \(\mathcal T^{(t)}\). We also introduce a canonical reduction under deletion of a recipient and characterize privacy heredity, design heredity, and \textsf{QPB}-heredity for exact restricted designs. The symmetric tower is fully \textsf{QPB}-hereditary, whereas the antisymmetric tower is fully privacy-hereditary and is design-hereditary from level \(s\) to \(s-1\) exactly when \(s\leq(d+1)/2\). For canonical carriers in arbitrary Schur-Weyl blocks, hereditary reduction is governed by multiplicity-free branching along the Young graph and inclusion of the associated Peter-Weyl coefficient spaces.

</details>


### [8] [Fractional reversible chemical kinetics: Time-Driven Monte Carlo method and application to enzyme conformational dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33652)
*Nelson H. T. Lemes,Higor V. M. Ferreira*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a fractional kinetic framework for reversible two-state chemical systems, derived microscopically from CTRW with Mittag-Leffler waiting times; it yields exact analytical solutions, proposes the first time-driven Monte Carlo algorithm for Caputo-based non-Markovian dynamics, and successfully models anomalous conformational relaxation of TbQSOX enzyme with α = 0.22.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a microscopic foundation for fractional-order kinetic equations in reversible two-state chemical systems exhibiting anomalous (power-law) relaxation, and to enable stochastic simulation of the underlying non-Markovian dynamics.

Method: Derivation of fractional kinetic equations from Continuous-Time Random Walk (CTRW) with Mittag-Leffler waiting-time distributions; analytical solution of the resulting Caputo-type fractional differential equations; development of a novel time-driven Monte Carlo algorithm for simulating the non-Markovian stochastic process.

Result: Exact closed-form analytical expressions for time evolution of state populations; first time-driven Monte Carlo method for Caputo fractional systems; quantitative agreement with experimental conformational kinetics of TbQSOX enzyme, yielding fractional order α = 0.22.

Conclusion: The CTRW-based fractional framework provides a physically grounded description of anomalous two-state kinetics; the derived Caputo equations and associated simulation algorithm constitute a robust tool for modeling and analyzing non-exponential relaxation in biochemical systems.

Abstract: In this work, we present a fractional framework for reversible two-state chemical kinetics derived from the Continuous-Time Random Walk (CTRW) formalism with Mittag-Leffler waiting-time distributions. This derivation provides a microscopic basis for the fractional kinetic equations governed by the Caputo derivative. An exact analytical solution is obtained, yielding closed-form expressions for the time evolution of the state populations. To the best of our knowledge, a time-driven Monte Carlo algorithm is developed for the first time to simulate the irreversible underlying non-Markovian stochastic dynamics governed by a system of Caputo fractional differential equations. The proposed framework is applied to the conformational dynamics of the TbQSOX enzyme, whose relaxation exhibits power-law behavior. The fractional model accurately reproduces the experimental kinetics, providing an estimate of the fractional order $\alpha = 0.22$, which is consistent with the observed anomalous dynamics.

</details>


### [9] [Linear statistics in the Coulomb gas: cumulants beyond rotational invariance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33659)
*Paul Bourgade,Pierre Le Doussal,Gregory Schehr*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives explicit formulas for the leading third and fourth cumulants of smooth linear statistics in large-N classical Coulomb gases in d ≥ 2, expressed as intrinsic boundary integrals over the equilibrium droplet—without requiring radial symmetry in potential or test function.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: While the leading variance of linear statistics in Coulomb gases is well understood, higher cumulants (especially third and fourth) remain largely unexplored beyond radially symmetric cases; this work closes that gap for general smooth droplets.

Method: Uses a tilted potential U_s = U + s f / β, analyzes the associated deformation of the equilibrium droplet via shape derivatives of the Dirichlet energy, interprets the deformation as infinitesimal free-boundary motion in the obstacle problem, and computes cumulants from asymptotics of the partition function's logarithmic derivatives.

Result: Explicit boundary-integral formulas: third cumulant ∝ ∫_{∂D} [∂_n h_f^ext]^3 dσ, fourth cumulant involves Dirichlet-to-Neumann operator, mean curvature, and normal derivatives of background density; verified in disk, half-plane, sphere, and non-radial cases (e.g., translated ellipses in d=2, spheroids in d=3).

Conclusion: Higher cumulants are governed entirely by geometric and harmonic properties of the droplet boundary—not bulk structure—establishing a universal boundary-localized mechanism for fluctuations beyond Gaussianity in Coulomb gases.

Abstract: We consider a classical Coulomb gas of $N$ particles in dimension $d\geq2$, confined by a general external potential $U$ in the large-$N$ limit. In that limit the density of particles has a compact support called the droplet. We study smooth linear statistics ${\mathcal L}_N=\sum_{i=1}^N f({\bf x}_i)$ at large $N$. While the leading variance of ${\mathcal L}_N$ is by now well understood in many settings, much less is known about higher cumulants. When $f({\bf x})=f(|{\bf x}|)$ and the potential is rotationally invariant, these cumulants were computed recently at leading order. We derive explicit formulas for the leading third and fourth cumulants for general smooth droplets, without assuming that either the potential or the test function is radial. The answers are expressed as intrinsic boundary integrals over the equilibrium droplet. The third cumulant depends on the cube of the jump of the normal derivative of the harmonic extension of $f$, while the fourth cumulant also involves the exterior Dirichlet-to-Neumann operator, the mean curvature of the boundary, and normal derivatives of the background density. Our derivation uses the tilted potential $U_s=U+s f/\beta$, the associated deformation of the droplet, and shape derivatives of the Dirichlet energy. This deformation has a natural interpretation as the infinitesimal motion of the free boundary in the obstacle-problem formulation of the equilibrium measure. Our formulas extend previous studies by Wiegmann and Zabrodin in two dimensions for $\beta=2$. In their setting, we provide a rigorous proof of the limiting third and fourth cumulants, supporting the proposed formulas for general Coulomb gases. We specialize the general formulas to the disk, the half-plane and the sphere, and check them against exactly solvable non-radial deformations, including translated elliptic droplets in $d=2$ and spheroidal deformations in $d=3$.

</details>


### [10] [V-Representability Beyond Singular Times in Time-Dependent Density Functional Theory: The Hubbard Dimer Case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33837)
*Éric Cancès,Théo Duez*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves global-in-time V-representability for the two-particle Hubbard dimer in the spin-singlet sector within both conventional and geometric TDDFT frameworks, and characterizes non-uniqueness of the effective potential at singular times.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the global V-representability problem in TDDFT for a minimal yet nontrivial interacting model—the two-particle Hubbard dimer in the spin-singlet sector—particularly beyond singular times where standard inversion fails.

Method: Analytical investigation of time-dependent density evolution under an external potential u(t), combined with rigorous proof techniques for V-representability; comparison between conventional (variational-principle-based) and geometric formulations of TDDFT.

Result: Global-in-time V-representability is established for both TDDFT formulations; singular times are shown to cause non-uniqueness of the time-dependent Kohn–Sham potential; the full set of admissible effective potentials at singular times is classified.

Conclusion: The two-particle Hubbard dimer admits globally V-representable densities in both conventional and geometric TDDFT, but singular times necessitate a refined uniqueness analysis—revealing a structured degeneracy in the effective potential space.

Abstract: We study the global $V$-representability problem in time-dependent density functional theory (TDDFT) for the two-particle Hubbard dimer model in the spin-singlet sector. In particular, we investigate whether the time-dependent density of the interacting system obtained for a given one-body external potential $u$ (the tunable control variable) can be reproduced by a non-interacting Kohn--Sham system beyond the singular times at which the usual inversion formulas break down. We deal with both the conventional formulation of TDDFT based on some time-dependent variational principle and the recently introduced geometric formulation of TDDFT (Phys. Rev. Lett. 136 (2026) 256401, Phys. Rev. A 113 (2026) 062222). For both principles, we prove global-in-time $V$-representability, show that singular times induce non-uniqueness of the time-dependent effective potential, and classify the set of solutions.

</details>


### [11] [Matrix representations of ${\rm BC}_n$ associative Yang--Baxter equations and related long-range spin chains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33913)
*M. Matushko,A. Mostovskii,A. Zotov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs matrix representations of the BC_n-type associative Yang-Baxter algebra using elliptic R- and K-matrices, corrects a unitarity condition, and applies them to build long-range quantum spin chains (e.g., Haldane–Shastry and Polychronakos–Frahm types) via R-matrix-valued Lax pairs for Calogero-type integrable systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend integrable spin chain constructions to BC_n-symmetric settings by leveraging algebraic structures from elliptic solutions of the associative Yang--Baxter equation, and to resolve inconsistencies (e.g., unitarity for odd N) in prior K-matrix derivations.

Method: Matrix representation of the BC_n associative Yang--Baxter algebra using the Baxter--Belavin R-matrix (GL_N fundamental) and Komori--Hikami elliptic K-matrix; revisited derivation via Felder--Pasquier procedure; construction of R-matrix-valued Lax pairs for classical Calogero--Inozemtsev and Calogero--Moser systems in external fields.

Result: Corrected unitarity relation for the Komori--Hikami K-matrix when N is odd; explicit R-matrix-valued Lax pairs for BC_n Calogero--Inozemtsev and Calogero--Moser-in-field models; new families of long-range spin chains with modified spin positions, including generalized Haldane--Shastry and Polychronakos--Frahm chains.

Conclusion: The BC_n associative Yang--Baxter algebra admits concrete elliptic matrix realizations that systematically generate novel integrable long-range spin models beyond the A_n case, unifying and extending previous constructions through R-matrix-valued Lax formalism.

Abstract: We construct a matrix representation of the algebra defined by the associative Yang--Baxter equations of ${\rm BC}_n$ type. In the elliptic case, two sets of generators are represented in terms of the Baxter--Belavin quantum $R$-matrix in the fundamental representation of ${\rm GL}_N$, while the remaining generators are represented by the elliptic $K$-matrix found by Komori and Hikami. We revisit its derivation using the Felder--Pasquier procedure and correct the unitarity relation for odd $N$. We then use these matrix representations to construct examples of long-range quantum spin chains from $R$-matrix-valued Lax pairs for the classical Calogero--Inozemtsev system associated with the ${\rm BC}_n$ root system. We also construct $R$-matrix-valued Lax pairs for Calogero--Moser models in an external field, leading to Haldane--Shastry and Polychronakos--Frahm chains with modified spin positions.

</details>


### [12] [The Genus-One B-Model Calabi-Yau/Landau-Ginzburg Correspondence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33984)
*Xinxing Tang,Junrong Yan*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves the Calabi-Yau/Landau-Ginzburg correspondence at genus one in the B-model by constructing an intrinsic Landau-Ginzburg invariant via the f-twisted Laplacian on C^n/Z_n and showing it matches the BCOV invariant of the Calabi-Yau hypersurface X_f up to a dimensional constant.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish the Calabi-Yau/Landau-Ginzburg correspondence for genus-one B-model invariants, predicted to relate orbifold LG invariants of (C^n/Z_n, f) to CY invariants of the hypersurface X_f.

Method: Constructs the genus-one B-model LG invariant intrinsically using the f-twisted Laplacian on the orbifold C^n/Z_n, and compares it with the BCOV torsion invariant of the Calabi-Yau hypersurface X_f.

Result: The constructed LG genus-one B-model invariant equals the BCOV invariant of X_f up to an additive constant depending only on n.

Conclusion: The genus-one B-model Calabi-Yau/Landau-Ginzburg correspondence holds for homogeneous degree-n polynomials with isolated singularity at 0, confirming the prediction via analytic comparison of twisted Laplacian and BCOV invariants.

Abstract: Let $f:\mathbb C^n\to\mathbb C$ be a homogeneous polynomial of degree $n$ with an isolated critical point at the origin. The Calabi--Yau/Landau--Ginzburg correspondence predicts a close relation between invariants of the Landau--Ginzburg orbifold $(\mathbb C^n/\mathbb Z_n,f)$ and those of the associated Calabi--Yau hypersurface $ X_f=\{[z_1:\cdots:z_n]\in\mathbb P^{n-1}:f(z_1,\ldots,z_n)=0\}. $ In this paper, we establish this correspondence for the genus-one B-model by constructing the Landau--Ginzburg genus-one B-model invariant intrinsically from the $f$-twisted Laplacian on $\mathbb{C}^n/\mathbb{Z}_n$ and proving that it agrees with the corresponding BCOV invariant of $X_f$ up to an additive constant depending only on the dimension.

</details>

*Azam Jahandideh*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This PhD thesis constructs the $P(\Phi)_2$ Euclidean quantum field theory on $\mathscr{S}'(\mathbb{R}^2)$ using finite- and infinite-volume measures, parabolic stochastic quantization, and energy methods, then verifies the full Osterwalder-Schrader axioms — including reflection positivity — for the first time in a stochastic quantization framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously construct a non-trivial local relativistic QFT in 2D by establishing the full Osterwalder-Schrader axioms — especially reflection positivity — for the $P(\Phi)_2$ model via stochastic quantization, which had been previously unachieved.
