Abstract: This PhD thesis constructs the $P(\Phi)_2$ Euclidean quantum field theory on $\mathscr{S}'(\mathbb{R}^2)$. We first construct the finite-volume measure of the $P(\Phi)_2$ model on $\mathscr{D}'(\mathbb{S}_R)$, where $\mathbb{S}_R$ is a two-dimensional sphere of radius $R\in\mathbb{N}_+$. To this end, we rely on Nelson's hypercontractivity estimate. We then use parabolic stochastic quantization and the energy method to construct the infinite-volume measure on $\mathscr{S}'(\mathbb{R}^2)$. This requires establishing the global-in-time existence of solutions to the corresponding stochastic partial differential equations with fixed cutoffs. The remainder of the thesis is devoted to verifying the Osterwalder-Schrader axioms: regularity, reflection positivity, and invariance under translations, rotations, and reflections. This combination of axioms, which is crucial for obtaining a non-trivial local relativistic quantum field theory on Minkowski spacetime, had not been established in earlier constructions based on stochastic quantization.

</details>


### [14] [The sharp leading asymptotics of the self-energy of the spinless Pauli-Fierz model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34122)
*Shinichiro Futakuchi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper determines the precise asymptotic behavior of the ground state energy $E_0(\alpha, \Lambda)$ of the Pauli-Fierz model for a non-relativistic electron coupled to the quantized radiation field with UV cutoff $\Lambda$, proving $E_0 = (1+o(1)) c_\alpha \alpha^{2/7} \Lambda^{12/7}$ as $\Lambda \to \infty$, with explicit $c_\alpha \approx 0.99338$. The leading-order energy is captured already by product (uncorrelated) electron-field trial states, showing electron-field correlations are subleading. The result holds uniformly under joint scaling constraints on $\alpha$ and $\Lambda$, and relies on a novel combination of coherent state bounds (upper bound) and a refined Born-Oppenheimer–type analysis with band-limited field estimates (lower bound).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the conjecture by Lieb and Loss that the upper bound $C_2 \alpha^{2/7} \Lambda^{12/7}$ on the ground state energy of the Pauli-Fierz model gives the correct asymptotic order, and to determine whether electron-field correlations contribute to the leading term.

Method: Upper bound: Coherent state representation of the field energy for a fixed electron density. Lower bound: Periodization of the field, block-wise Born-Oppenheimer approximation involving phase multiplication and a Hardy inequality in field space, and a spectral lower bound on the magnetic Neumann Laplacian for band-limited fields — crucially exploiting that weighted gradient constraints prevent vorticity concentration in such fields.

Result: For every fixed $\alpha > 0$, $E_0(\alpha, \Lambda) = (1+o(1)) c_\alpha \alpha^{2/7} \Lambda^{12/7}$ as $\Lambda \to \infty$, with explicit $c_\alpha = 0.99338\dots$. More generally, the asymptotics hold uniformly when $\Lambda/\alpha \to \infty$, $\alpha^{5/2} \Lambda \to \infty$, and $\log(2+\alpha) = o((\Lambda/\alpha)^{1/21})$. The ratio of $E_0$ to the infimum over product states tends to 1, proving electron-field correlations do not affect the leading order.

Conclusion: The Lieb–Loss conjecture is confirmed: the leading asymptotics of the ground state energy is indeed $\sim c_\alpha \alpha^{2/7} \Lambda^{12/7}$. Moreover, the energy is saturated by uncorrelated (product) trial states, meaning entanglement between electron and field is irrelevant at leading order. The scaling regime $\alpha^{5/2} \Lambda \to 0$ is shown to be qualitatively different, where the vacuum dominates.

Abstract: We consider the ground state energy E_0(alpha, Lambda) of a single spinless non-relativistic electron coupled to the quantized radiation field with a sharp ultraviolet cutoff Lambda (the Pauli-Fierz model), with fine structure constant alpha = e^2/(4 pi). Lieb and Loss proved that C_1 alpha^(1/2) Lambda^(3/2) <= E_0 <= C_2 alpha^(2/7) Lambda^(12/7) for large Lambda and suggested that the upper bound, which comes from product states, gives the correct order. We prove that, for every fixed alpha > 0, E_0(alpha, Lambda) = (1+o(1)) c_alpha alpha^(2/7) Lambda^(12/7) as Lambda -> infinity, with an explicit constant c_alpha = 0.99338...; more generally, this holds uniformly in the joint limit Lambda/alpha -> infinity, alpha^(5/2) Lambda -> infinity, provided log(2+alpha) = o((Lambda/alpha)^(1/21)). Since our upper bound holds already for the infimum over product states, it follows that the ratio of E_0 to this infimum tends to one: electron-field correlations do not contribute to the leading term. The condition alpha^(5/2) Lambda -> infinity cannot be dropped, since for alpha^(5/2) Lambda -> 0 the vacuum trial state gives a smaller energy. The upper bound follows from a coherent state bound for the field energy in a fixed electron density. The lower bound combines a periodization of the field, a Born-Oppenheimer bound in which phase multiplication and a Hardy inequality in field space are applied block by block, and a lower bound on the magnetic Neumann Laplacian on a cube for band-limited fields; the key point of the last is that a band-limited field obeying a weighted gradient condition cannot concentrate its vorticity.

</details>


### [15] [The effective mass of the Pauli-Fierz electron: a cutoff-independent lower bound and the breakdown of mass renormalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34125)
*Shinichiro Futakuchi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes two rigorous lower bounds on the effective-to-bare mass ratio h in the Pauli-Fierz model: (1) a uniform-in-cutoff bound recovering the perturbative coefficient for small alpha, and (2) a superlinear growth of h with UV cutoff lambda, implying a physical mass constraint on the bare mass and cutoff.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously justify the expected physical constraint that a finite physical electron mass requires a finite ultraviolet cutoff in the Pauli-Fierz model — a problem left open by Lieb and Loss (2002).

Method: For bound (1): uses coherent translation of the quantized field applied to the ground state as a trial state; exploits minimality to cancel the linear term. For bound (2): builds on sharp self-energy asymptotics from a companion paper; introduces a field-dependent phase trial state, defines an estimator for electron position via the local center of a shallow level set of the photon cloud, decouples a localized field part using a divergence-free external subspace, and proves rarity of shallow depressions near the localization window edge.

Result: First bound: h ≥ 1 + 0.3441α/(1 + 0.2985√α + 0.1252α) for all α > 0, λ ≥ 1 — cutoff-independent and matches Hiroshima–Spohn’s perturbative coefficient at O(α). Second bound: for each fixed α, h ≥ λ^(8/7−ε) for large λ, implying ι_PF(α) := inf_λ>0 h/λ > 0, hence m ≤ m_phys / ι_PF(α) enforces an upper bound on Λ for fixed physical mass m_phys.

Conclusion: The paper provides the first rigorous proof that the Pauli-Fierz effective mass diverges faster than linearly with the UV cutoff, thereby confirming the necessity of a finite cutoff to achieve a finite physical electron mass — resolving a long-standing open problem in non-relativistic QED.

Abstract: We consider the Pauli-Fierz model of one spinless non-relativistic electron coupled to the quantized transverse electromagnetic field, without external potential, with a sharp ultraviolet cutoff Lambda and bare mass m. Let h = m_eff/m be the ratio of the effective mass, defined by the curvature of the fiber ground state energy at total momentum zero, to the bare mass; by scaling, h depends only on the fine structure constant alpha = e^2/(4 pi) and on lambda = Lambda/m. We prove two lower bounds. First, h >= 1 + 0.3441 alpha/(1 + 0.2985 sqrt(alpha) + 0.1252 alpha) for all alpha > 0 and all lambda >= 1; the bound does not depend on the cutoff, and with the band extended to the cutoff its first order coefficient in alpha is the perturbative one of Hiroshima and Spohn. The proof uses a coherent translation of the field applied to the ground state as a trial state; the term linear in the translation vanishes by minimality of the ground state. Second, for every fixed alpha, h grows faster than linearly: for every epsilon > 0, h >= lambda^(8/7 - epsilon) for all large lambda (8/7 is the limit of the method). Consequently iota_PF(alpha) := inf_{lambda > 0} h/lambda > 0, and for a fixed physical mass m_phys a bare mass reproducing it exists only if Lambda <= m_phys/iota_PF(alpha). This was expected, but not proved, by Lieb and Loss (2002). The proof of the second bound starts from the sharp self-energy asymptotics of a companion paper and uses a trial state obtained from the ground state by a phase depending on the field configuration. The new ingredients are an estimator for the position of the electron given by the local centre of a shallow level set of the photon cloud, the decoupling of a localized part of the field from the electron through a divergence-free external subspace, and a proof that shallow depressions near the edge of the localization window are rare.

</details>


### [16] [Spectral localizer for unbounded topological Hamiltonians](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34138)
*Hermann Schulz-Baldes*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the spectral localizer framework—previously used for computing K-theoretic index pairings with Fredholm modules—to unbounded gapped Hamiltonians, specifically second-order differential operators like matrix-valued Schrödinger and regularized Dirac operators, using coercive phase-space localization and wavelet-based discretization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the spectral localizer method from bounded to unbounded gapped Hamiltonians, enabling topological index computations for physically relevant differential operators in quantum systems.

Method: Extends the spectral localizer construction via coercive phase-space localization and wavelet-based discretization to handle unbounded, gapped, second-order linear differential operators (e.g., matrix-valued Schrödinger and regularized Dirac operators).

Result: The half-signature of the (now infinite-dimensional but effectively truncated) spectral localizer correctly computes K-theoretic index pairings for unbounded gapped Hamiltonians, preserving topological robustness under the new framework.

Conclusion: The spectral localizer framework is successfully generalized to unbounded gapped Hamiltonians; coercive phase-space localization and wavelet discretization are essential technical enablers for maintaining finite-dimensionality and computability of the index.

Abstract: In a series of joint papers with Loring, it was shown how index parings of $K$-theory classes with Fredholm modules can be computed as the half-signature of a suitably constructed finite-dimensional matrix, called the spectral localizer. After offering a novel perspective on earlier results, it is shown how the framework can be extended to unbounded gapped Hamiltonians given as second-order linear differential operators (in particular, matrix-valued Schrödinger and regularized Dirac operators). Important insights are the coercive phase space localization and discretizations by means of wavelets.

</details>


### [17] [Random-walk scaling without Bernoulli statistics: transport, persistence and resonances in the triangle map](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34179)
*Mirko Degli Esposti*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the triangle map—a piecewise-parabolic, area-preserving toral map with zero Lyapunov exponent—via its symbolic dynamics (±1) and transport properties. It establishes spectral, cohomological, and statistical properties: absence of non-constant H¹ eigenfunctions/coboundaries, bounded run lengths, normal diffusion with highly parameter-dependent diffusion coefficient D (0.2–14× Bernoulli), first-passage exponent 1/2, anomalous behavior at β=0 (D=0 but slow variance growth, rare sign changes, n⁻⁰·² Fourier decay), and exact characterization of zero-sum words as resonances governing trapping and crossover scaling β⁻¹ᐟ². Conjectures are posed on unique ergodicity, mixing, CLT, and symbol cohomology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the statistical and dynamical properties of a zero-Lyapunov, area-preserving, piecewise-parabolic map—the triangle map—whose symbolic dynamics encode momentum transport, and to resolve how weak chaos (or lack thereof) manifests in diffusion, correlations, ergodic averages, and cohomological obstructions.

Method: Rigorous analysis of the Koopman operator on H¹, coboundary theory, symbolic dynamics (run-length bounds, zero-sum word classification), Green-Kubo formalism, exact computation on polygonal cylinder sets, asymptotic analysis of variance and first-passage statistics, and identification of resonant structures (pure twists at β=0) governing orbit trapping and scaling laws.

Result: The symbol is not an H¹ coboundary; Koopman has no non-constant H¹ eigenfunctions; symbol runs are uniformly bounded; diffusion is Gaussian with parameter-sensitive D ∈ [0.2,14]×Bernoulli D₀; at β=0, D=0 but Var(Sₙ) grows subdiffusively, sign changes are rare, Fourier mode correlations decay as n⁻⁰·²; zero-sum words correspond to resonances inducing 1/ℓ trapping law and β⁻¹ᐟ² crossover; asymmetric deformation yields exact logarithmic variance correction.

Conclusion: The triangle map exhibits rich intermediate dynamics between integrability and chaos: it is neither mixing nor hyperbolic, yet supports normal diffusion generically; its cohomological structure and resonance-driven trapping explain anomalous transport at parameter boundaries; the results suggest deep links between symbolic combinatorics, spectral theory, and transport in near-integrable systems, motivating conjectures on unique ergodicity, mixing, CLT validity, and full cohomology classification.

Abstract: The triangle map is an area-preserving, piecewise-parabolic map of the torus with vanishing Lyapunov exponent. We study the symbolic process $X_n=\pm1$ given by the two halves of the torus, whose partial sums $S_n$ are the transport of the lifted momentum, with an integrator free of accumulated rounding errors and exact computations on polygonal cylinder sets. We prove that the Koopman operator has no non-constant $H^1$ eigenfunctions, that the symbol is not an $H^1$ coboundary, and that runs of equal symbols are uniformly bounded. For generic parameters the walk is normally diffusive and Gaussian, but its diffusion coefficient is not that of a coin: away from the edges of the parameter square it varies between $0.2$ and $14$ times the Bernoulli value, and for a benchmark pair, with $D=3.09\pm0.01$, the excess is produced by the order of the bounded inter-event intervals, not by their distribution. The first-passage exponent is $1/2$ with a non-Bernoulli amplitude, and worst-case Birkhoff averages decay as for a uniquely ergodic system. For $\beta=0$ the Green-Kubo sum cancels, $D=0$, yet $\mathrm{Var}(S_n)$ keeps growing slowly, sign changes are rare and the correlations of Fourier modes decay as $n^{-0.2}$. We identify the zero-sum words, which become pure twists at $\beta=0$, as resonances that trap orbits with a computable $1/\ell$ law, set the crossover time $\beta^{-1/2}$ at small $\beta$, and, in an asymmetric deformation of the symbol, produce a logarithmic term in the variance whose coefficient we compute exactly. We formulate conjectures on unique ergodicity, mixing, the central limit theorem and the cohomology of the symbol.

</details>


### [18] [Semantic burstiness across principal directions of textual variation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34188)
*Mirko Degli Esposti,Marcelo A. Montemurro*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a method to quantify semantic burstiness in texts by projecting pre-trained word embeddings onto PCA-derived semantic directions, thresholding projections to define inter-event times, and analyzing their variability; it shows that real texts exhibit significantly higher burstiness than controls preserving either temporal order or frequency structure alone—indicating that long-range semantic organization arises from ordered recurrence of semantically related content, not just global rank correlations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how long-range correlations in written texts relate to semantic organization, moving beyond lexical burstiness to distributed semantic observables.

Method: Token-wise mapping of texts to pre-trained word embeddings; PCA on embedding trajectories to extract text-specific semantic directions; thresholding projected series (e.g., upper 5% tail) to define semantic events; computation of coefficient of variation of inter-event times yielding burstiness spectrum {B_k}; comparison against word-shuffled null (preserves embedding covariance, PCA eigensystem, marginal distributions) and fractional-Gaussian-noise surrogate (preserves Zipfian frequencies and DFA exponent of rank sequence).

Result: Original texts consistently show higher burstiness {B_k} than both controls across all 28 texts and first 20 PCA components; controls are statistically indistinguishable; autocorrelation and DFA of semantic projections also separate originals from controls.

Conclusion: Global rank-level long-range correlations are insufficient to explain observed semantic burstiness; instead, the results support an interpretation based on the temporally ordered recurrence of semantically related material.

Abstract: Long-range correlations in written texts extend over scales far beyond local syntax, but their relation to semantic organisation remains incompletely understood. Previous work has linked these correlations to the bursty recurrence of topically relevant words. Here we generalise that construction from individual lexical items to distributed semantic observables. We map a text token by token onto a trajectory of pre-trained word embeddings, use principal component analysis (PCA) to identify text-specific directions of semantic variation, and threshold the projected series to define inter-event times; their coefficients of variation form a component-resolved burstiness spectrum $\{B_k\}$. Across 28 literary and scientific texts and the first 20 components, the originals remain systematically above the geometric inter-event-time baseline and above two complementary controls: a word-shuffled null, which preserves the embedding covariance, the PCA eigensystem and the marginal projected distributions while destroying temporal order, and a fractional-Gaussian-noise surrogate, which preserves the Zipfian frequency structure and matches the detrended fluctuation analysis (DFA) exponent of the original rank sequence. With events defined by the upper 5% tail of each projection, the original exceeds both controls in every text-component pair, while the two controls are statistically indistinguishable from each other. Autocorrelation and detrended fluctuation analysis of the semantic projections separate the originals from both controls in the same way. Global rank-level long-range correlations are therefore insufficient to generate the bursty recurrence observed along semantic directions. The results instead support an interpretation in terms of the ordered recurrence of semantically related material.

</details>


### [19] [Boundary-condition independence of infinite-chain ground states in the $S=1$ $J$-$D$ antiferromagnetic chain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34330)
*Hal Tasaki*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper rigorously establishes the existence, uniqueness, and continuity (in anisotropy D) of a symmetric, gapped infinite-chain ground state for the S=1 J-D antiferromagnetic model near D=0, using analytic and inductive methods informed by OpenAI's Haldane gap proofs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide rigorous foundational input—specifically, existence, uniform convergence, symmetry, spectral gap, and continuous dependence on D—for a topological phase transition analysis in a companion paper, building on OpenAI's numerical and analytic work on the Haldane gap.

Method: Extends analytic machinery from OpenAI's Haldane gap proofs; employs inductive estimates (substantially aided by ChatGPT Astra) to control finite-volume ground states in two settings—periodic even-site chains and open odd-site chains with symmetric boundary magnetic fields—and proves full-sequence convergence (no subsequence needed) to a common infinite-volume limit, uniformly in D near zero.

Result: For sufficiently small |D|, both finite-volume constructions converge uniformly in D to the same infinite-chain ground state, which is symmetric, locally unique, gapped, and has local expectations continuously dependent on D.

Conclusion: The paper establishes a robust, D-continuous gapped ground state for the S=1 J-D chain near D=0, validating key assumptions for subsequent topological analysis; uniqueness is local (not global among all possible infinite-volume ground states).

Abstract: Extending the analytic machinery and using the numerical inputs from OpenAI's proofs of the Haldane gap, we study infinite-chain ground states of the $S=1$ $J$-$D$ antiferromagnetic model $H=\sum_j\{\boldsymbol S_j\cdot\boldsymbol S_{j+1}+D(S_j^{\mathrm z})^2\}$ for sufficiently small $|D|$. We consider two finite-volume settings: periodic chains with an even number of sites, and open chains with an odd number of sites and the same positive magnetic field in the $\mathrm z$ direction at both endpoints. We prove that the ground states in each setting have an infinite-chain limit along the full sequence, without passing to a subsequence, and that the two limiting states coincide. Convergence is uniform in $D$ on a fixed neighborhood of zero. The common limit is a symmetric locally-unique gapped ground state, and its local expectation values depend continuously on $D$. These conclusions concern the two specified constructions, not uniqueness among all infinite-chain ground states. They provide the input for the study of a topological phase transition in a forthcoming revision of our companion paper. The author formulated the problem and proposed the basic strategy. ChatGPT Astra made substantial contributions to the inductive estimates and to completing the proof.

</details>


### [20] [Rigorous Lower Bounds for Atomic and Ionic Ground-State Energies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34480)
*Michael Hofacker*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces a new iterative method to compute rigorous lower bounds for ground-state energies of atoms/ions, improving the best-known constant in the asymptotic lower bound from ~1.1447 to ~0.8710 and approaching the Thomas–Fermi limit (~0.7687); it also provides explicit bounds for atoms with 2 ≤ N ≤ 86 and proves uniqueness of expectation values of kinetic, potential, and interaction energies in the ground-state space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve rigorous lower bounds on atomic ground-state energies—particularly tightening the prefactor in the N^{1/3}Z^2 scaling law—and bridge the gap between existing bounds and the Thomas–Fermi asymptotic limit.

Method: A novel iterative scheme leveraging an initial energy estimate and a sharp upper bound on the maximum number of bound electrons N_c(Z); combines variational principles, spectral estimates, and careful control of electron correlation and kinetic energy.

Result: Derives explicit lower bound E(N,Z) ≥ −0.8710 N^{1/3} Z^2 for 0 < Z ≤ N; computes concrete lower bounds for E(N,N) across atoms Z = 2 to 86; outperforms prior bounds by Hertel–Lieb–Thirring and Seco; proves uniqueness of kinetic/potential/interaction energy expectation values in the ground-state eigenspace.

Conclusion: The method significantly advances rigorous quantum-mechanical bounds for atomic energies, brings theoretical constants closer to the Thomas–Fermi prediction, and provides both quantitative benchmarks and structural insight (uniqueness) into ground-state energetics.

Abstract: We establish a new iterative method for computing rigorous lower bounds for the ground-state energies of all atoms and ions. The accuracy of the resulting bounds depends on two ingredients: the quality of the initial energy estimate and the quality of the used upper bound for the maximum number of bound electrons, $N_c(Z)$, as a function of the nuclear charge $Z>0$. In particular, this new method allows us to establish the explicit lower bound $E(N,Z) \geq -0.8710\,N^{1/3}Z^2$ for the ground-state energy $E(N,Z)$ of the atom/ion with $N \in \mathbb{N}$ electrons and nuclear charge $Z \in (0,N]$, thereby considerably improving the best known constant $A=(3/2)^{1/3} \approx 1.1447$ in the estimate $E(N,Z) \geq -A\,N^{1/3}Z^2$ for $0 < Z \leq N$ and coming much closer to the best possible constant given by the Thomas-Fermi asymptotics $\lim_{N \to \infty} |E(N,N)|N^{-7/3}= -E_{\text{TF}}(1)\approx 0.7687$ derived in Adv. Math., 23(1) by Lieb and Simon. To demonstrate the potential and the limits of our method, we also compute explicit lower bounds for the ground-state energies $E(N,N)$ of all atoms with $2 \leq N \leq 86$ and we compare them with the bounds obtained in J. Chem. Phys., 62(8) by Hertel, Lieb, Thirring and the bounds obtained by Seco in his dissertation. Finally, we also provide a uniqueness result for the expectation values of the kinetic, potential and interaction energies on the ground-state space.

</details>


### [21] [Gaussian $β$-Markov Path Spaces without a Spectral Gap and the Araki--Woods Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34783)
*Yoshitsugu Sekine*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Extends the Gaussian β-Markov path space construction for free Bose fields to gapless one-particle Hamiltonians, preserving equivalence with the Araki–Woods representation; enables applications in constructive QFT at positive temperature and equilibrium statistical mechanics (e.g., BEC absence in non-number-conserving quasiparticle systems).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the Gaussian β-Markov path space framework—previously restricted to gapped one-particle Hamiltonians—to gapless cases, enabling its use in equilibrium statistical mechanics and constructive quantum field theory at positive temperature, particularly for systems like non-number-conserving quasiparticles where Bose–Einstein condensation may be absent.

Method: Mathematical extension of the Dereziński–Gérard construction by rigorously adapting the Gaussian β-Markov path space formalism and proving its unitary equivalence to the Araki–Woods representation for one-particle Hamiltonians with continuous spectrum (i.e., no spectral gap), using tools from operator algebras, functional integration, and nonequilibrium/statistical QFT.

Result: A well-defined Gaussian β-Markov path space for free Bose fields associated with gapless one-particle Hamiltonians, proven equivalent to the Araki–Woods representation; this provides a rigorous stochastic (path-space) framework for equilibrium thermal states in settings where particle number is not conserved.

Conclusion: The extension removes the spectral gap restriction, broadening applicability to physically relevant gapless systems in thermal quantum field theory and equilibrium statistical mechanics, especially for analyzing phase behavior (e.g., absence of BEC) in open or dissipative quasiparticle systems.

Abstract: Dereziński and Gérard constructed the Gaussian $\beta$-Markov path space of a free Bose field in a form equivalent to the Araki--Woods representation, which suits nonequilibrium statistical mechanics with a quantum-field environment, but only for one-particle Hamiltonians with a spectral gap. We extend this construction, together with the equivalence to the Araki--Woods representation, to one-particle Hamiltonians without a spectral gap. The extension is intended for constructive quantum field theory at positive temperature and equilibrium statistical mechanics, for instance in the problem of the absence of Bose--Einstein condensation of quasiparticles whose number is not conserved.

</details>


### [22] [On the types of quasiperiodic potentials and level surfaces of quasiperiodic functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34989)
*A.Ya. Maltsev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper compares two classes of quasiperiodic functions—those obtained by restricting smooth periodic functions to subspaces and those arising in quasicrystalline physical fields—and analyzes their similarities and differences using the Novikov problem on level-set geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand structural and geometric commonalities and distinctions between mathematically constructed quasiperiodic functions and physically relevant quasiperiodic potentials in quasicrystals.

Method: Comparative analysis grounded in the Novikov problem, focusing on the global topology and geometry of level sets of quasiperiodic functions.

Result: Both function classes share certain topological and analytical features (e.g., recurrence, spectral properties), but differ in rigidity, regularity constraints, and global level-set structure—particularly in higher codimensions and under physical realizability conditions.

Conclusion: The Novikov problem serves as a unifying framework for distinguishing mathematical idealizations from physically admissible quasiperiodic fields, highlighting the role of embedding dimension and smoothness in determining observable geometric behavior.

Abstract: We examine the similarities and differences between two types of quasiperiodic functions: those obtained by restricting smooth periodic functions in $\mathbb{R}^N$ to lower-dimensional subspaces $\mathbb{R}^n \subset \mathbb{R}^N$, and those that arise as physical fields or potentials in quasicrystalline structures. Our analysis reveals that functions in these two classes share a number of common features while also exhibiting significant differences. As a key tool for investigating both classes of functions, we employ the Novikov problem - specifically, the problem of describing the global geometry of the level sets of quasiperiodic functions.

</details>


### [23] [General Relativity for Mathematicians - Part I](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35194)
*Filippo Fila-Robattino,Manuel Tecchiolli*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Lecture notes covering foundational topics in mathematical physics, including classical mechanics, relativity, differential geometry, and general relativity, tailored for a mathematically rigorous audience.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a mathematically rigorous yet physically motivated introduction to core concepts in theoretical physics—particularly those underpinning general relativity—for advanced undergraduate or graduate students in mathematics or mathematical physics.

Method: Combines precise definitions, detailed proofs, explicit calculations, and physical interpretation; structured as lecture notes for a university course.

Result: A self-contained pedagogical exposition of variational principles, special relativity, smooth manifolds, tensor calculus, connections, curvature, geodesics, and the derivation of Einstein's equations from the Einstein-Hilbert action.

Conclusion: The notes successfully bridge mathematical rigor and physical insight, serving as a concise, proof-oriented foundation for studying general relativity and geometric mechanics.

Abstract: These notes cover the first part of a course of the same title taught by Filippo Fila-Robattino and Manuel Tecchiolli at the University of Zurich in the Winter Semester of 2025. Topics include variational principles in classical mechanics, special relativity, smooth manifolds and tensor calculus, connections and curvature, geodesic motion, and the derivation of Einstein's equations from the Einstein-Hilbert action. The treatment is aimed at a mathematical audience, combining physical motivation with precise definitions, detailed proofs, and explicit calculations.

</details>


### [24] [Measurement-dependent reduction of open dynamics in the quantum phase estimation algorithm: the visible subspace and the damped Fejér kernel](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35332)
*Marina O. Gordeichuk,Oleg M. Kiselev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the quantum phase estimation (QPE) algorithm under open quantum dynamics, determining the minimal observable space needed to predict the full output distribution; it leverages Krylov reduction theory, derives exact dimension bounds (M to M²−M+1), identifies spectral classes as a constructive basis for diagonal adjoint generators, and provides error-controlled approximate reductions via finite-time Gramians.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize the minimal subspace of observables sufficient to fully determine the QPE output distribution when the counting register evolves under generic open (non-unitary) dynamics—addressing limitations of standard closed-system QPE analysis.

Method: Applies Krylov subspace reduction theory to the Heisenberg picture; computes the 'visible space' of observables under Fourier measurement; derives cyclic-sum structure of outcome probabilities; constructs explicit bases using spectral classes modulo M for diagonal adjoint generators; introduces finite-time Gramian-based approximate reduction with a posteriori residual error estimation.

Result: Exact dimension formula and tight bounds (M ≤ dim ≤ M²−M+1) for the visible space; damped Fejér kernel solution for a solvable GKLS model; demonstration that higher harmonics in QPE distributions are distinguishable even for channels with identical Ramsey contrast; quadratic-order error bound for approximate reduction under compatible perturbations.

Conclusion: The output distribution of QPE under open dynamics is governed by a structured, often low-dimensional observable subspace—enabling efficient simulation and error-aware characterization; the framework unifies exact spectral analysis and practical approximate reduction with certified accuracy.

Abstract: For the quantum phase estimation (QPE) algorithm we study the minimal space of observables that is sufficient to predict the entire output distribution under open dynamics of the counting register. Building on the general Krylov theory of reduction, we compute the structure of this space explicitly for the full Fourier measurement. The outcome probabilities are determined by cyclic sums of the state matrix. If the adjoint generator is diagonal on the matrix units, the spectral classes inside the residue classes of the index difference modulo the register dimension $M$ give a constructive basis, an exact dimension formula, and attainable bounds: the dimension of the visible space lies between $M$ and $M^2-M+1$. The class includes independent qubit dephasing, and an analytically solvable reference model of Gorini--Kossakowski--Lindblad--Sudarshan (GKLS) type yields a damped Fejér kernel; two random-unitary channels with the same Ramsey contrast show that higher harmonics are distinguishable in the QPE distribution. For approximate reduction we use a finite-time Gramian and an a posteriori residual estimate, with a quadratic error order for a reducing unperturbed subspace and a compatible initial state. Numerical calculations check the consistency of the representations and the behaviour of the error under non-Toeplitz perturbations.

</details>


### [25] [Existence of a weak solution to the Navier-Stokes equations enjoying the energy equality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35517)
*Paolo Maremonti,Alfonsina Tartaglione*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves the existence of a weak solution to the Navier-Stokes equations that satisfies the energy equality—not just the standard energy inequality—by taking limits of mollified solutions (Leray's approach); however, it does not establish that all Leray–Hopf weak solutions satisfy energy equality due to lack of uniqueness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing open question of whether a weak solution to the Navier-Stokes equations can satisfy the energy equality (rather than only the weaker energy inequality) in the absence of regularity or uniqueness assumptions.

Method: Constructs a weak solution as the limit of smooth solutions to a mollified (regularized) version of the Navier-Stokes system, following Leray’s classical regularization approach; carefully controls convergence to ensure preservation of energy equality in the limit.

Result: Existence of at least one weak solution satisfying the energy equality is established; however, energy equality is not shown to hold for *all* Leray–Hopf weak solutions due to non-uniqueness.

Conclusion: Energy equality is attainable for some weak solutions constructed via mollification, but the question remains open for arbitrary Leray–Hopf solutions.

Abstract: To date, a Leray's weak solution to the Navier-Stokes equations enjoys an energy inequality. We prove the existence of a weak solution that enjoys the energy equality. The existence of the weak solution is achieved as limit of solutions to the mollified Navier-Stokes system (Leray's approach). Since no uniqueness result is known for weak solutions we are not able to claim that any weak solution in the sense of Leray-Hopf enjoys the energy equality.

</details>


### [26] [Finite-step projective dynamics of Schrödinger cocycles: non-degeneracy, regularity, and rigidity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35583)
*Mira Shamis*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes the 'no deterministic images' (NDI) condition for Schrödinger cocycles, proving it under broad classes of potentials—including nonconstant measurable, ergodic with infinite essential range, finite-state locally constant, bounded Markov/hidden Markov (under an L² gap), and nonconstant real trigonometric (e.g., almost Mathieu). It derives Hölder regularity for solution-direction distributions and stationary measures, proves non-atomicity of Oseledets directions for irrational frequencies and positive Lyapunov exponents, and provides a new zero-Lyapunov criterion via atomic subsequential limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand spectral and dynamical properties of Schrödinger operators via the 'no deterministic images' (NDI) condition—a key regularity property for projective cocycle dynamics that ensures absence of concentration in solution directions and supports positivity and continuity of Lyapunov exponents.

Method: Functional-analytic and probabilistic techniques: mixing/ergodic theory, Markov operator spectral gaps (L² norm), Hölder regularity analysis of transfer operators, classification of finite-state potentials via combinatorial dynamics, and extension to analytic settings using step-dependent regularity bounds.

Result: 1) Two-step NDI holds universally for nonconstant measurable potentials over mixing systems and ergodic potentials with infinite essential range; 2) Full classification of NDI persistence for finite-state locally constant potentials; 3) Hölder bounds for solution-direction distributions and conditional stationary measures under L² gap for bounded Markov/hidden Markov potentials; 4) NDI and uniform-in-step Hölder bounds for all k ≥ 2 for nonconstant real trigonometric potentials (including almost Mathieu); 5) Non-atomicity of Oseledets direction distributions and exponential convergence to unstable distribution for irrational frequencies and positive LE; 6) A new zero-LE criterion: existence of an atomic subsequential limit from a fixed initial direction implies LE = 0.

Conclusion: The NDI condition is generic across diverse potential classes, and its validity—paired with Hölder regularity and non-atomicity results—provides a unified framework for establishing continuity, positivity, and fine statistical properties of Lyapunov exponents and spectral measures for quasiperiodic and random Schrödinger operators.

Abstract: We study the no deterministic images condition (NDI) for Schrödinger cocycles, which excludes a common image of a fixed probability measure under almost all projective transfer transformations. We prove two-step NDI at every energy for nonconstant measurable potentials over mixing systems and ergodic potentials with infinite essential range, and classify persistence for finite-state locally constant potentials. For bounded Markov and hidden Markov potentials on general state spaces under a one-step $L^2$ norm gap condition on the Markov operator, we prove Hölder bounds for solution-direction distributions above an exponential cutoff and Hölder conditional stationary measures. Counterexamples show the essential sharpness of both assumptions. For nonconstant real trigonometric potentials, including the almost Mathieu operator, we prove NDI and Hölder bounds for distributions of solution directions at all steps $k\ge2$ and all frequencies (rational or irrational). The exponent depends on the step, while the constant is uniform in steps, frequency and energy on compact intervals. For every irrational frequency and every energy with positive Lyapunov exponent, we prove non-atomicity of both Oseledets direction distributions and convergence to the unstable distribution from every phase-independent initial probability. This gives a new criterion for a zero Lyapunov exponent: a subsequential limit with an atom for one fixed initial direction suffices. All these results extend to an analytic class, with step-dependent regularity bounds.

</details>


### [27] [Curvature-induced fracture mode coupling in doubly curved shells](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35597)
*Riccardo Brambilla,Siddhant Kumar,Bianca Giovanardi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a semi-analytical framework based on Reissner shallow-shell theory to analyze fracture behavior of arbitrarily oriented through-thickness cracks in doubly curved shells (spheres, ellipsoids, cylinders, hyperboloids, minimal surfaces) under general loading; it reveals curvature-induced coupling among opening (Mode I), sliding (Mode II), and tearing (Mode III) fracture modes—unlike flat plates—and identifies geometry-dependent coupling strength (strongest in hyperboloids, weakest in ellipsoids), including shear-induced crack closure in hyperboloids as a potential damage-resistance mechanism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Classical shell fracture theories are limited to cracks aligned with principal curvature directions and simple loadings (e.g., tension or pressure), leaving fracture of arbitrarily oriented cracks in doubly curved shells under general loading poorly understood.

Method: Semi-analytical framework built on Reissner shallow-shell theory; computes stress intensity factors (SIFs), energy release rates (G), and crack-lip displacements for through-thickness cracks in isotropic shells with positive (spheres, ellipsoids), zero (cylinders), and negative (hyperboloids, minimal surfaces) Gaussian curvature under arbitrary mechanical loading.

Result: Shell curvature couples Mode I, II, and III fracture responses—enabling single loading components to activate multiple modes; coupling strength depends on crack orientation and local curvature components (normal, parallel, torsional); ellipsoids show weak coupling, hyperboloids strongest, cylinders most orientation-sensitive; in-plane shear induces crack closure in hyperboloids.

Conclusion: Local Gaussian and directional curvatures fundamentally govern fracture-mode coupling in shells; this work provides a mechanistic basis for designing shellular (meta-)structures with tailored curvature distributions to control fracture behavior and enhance damage resistance.

Abstract: Classical shell fracture theories predominantly consider cracks aligned with principal curvature directions in cylindrical and spherical shells under tensile mode or pressure loading, leaving the fracture behavior of arbitrarily oriented cracks in doubly curved shells under general loading poorly understood. Here, we introduce a semi-analytical framework based on Reissner shallow-shell theory to investigate isotropic shells with positive, zero, and negative Gaussian curvature. The formulation computes stress intensity factors, energy release rates, and crack-lip displacements for arbitrarily inclined through-thickness cracks in spheres, ellipsoids, cylinders, hyperboloids, and minimal surfaces under general mechanical loading. We show that shell curvature couples opening, sliding, and tearing, allowing a single loading component to activate multiple fracture modes, unlike in flat plates. This coupling depends strongly on crack orientation and the normal, parallel, and torsional components of the shell curvature. Ellipsoids exhibit weak coupling, hyperboloids generally exhibit the strongest coupling, and cylinders display the most complex orientation-dependent responses. Moreover, in-plane shear can induce crack closure in hyperboloidal shells, suggesting a potential geometric mechanism for enhanced damage resistance. These findings elucidate the role of local curvature in fracture-mode coupling and provide a mechanistic foundation for the analysis of shellular (meta-)structures with engineered curvature distributions.

</details>


### [28] [Simplifying 2-Bundle Descriptions of Invariants and Local Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35669)
*Ferreira Bastien*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that surface amplitudes, quantum partition functions, and classical field dynamics for strings coupled to two-form gauge potentials can be recovered without invoking principal 2-bundles—using instead cocycles, bundles, operators, modules, Fourier transforms, and local substitutions—thereby eliminating redundant field variables and bypassing the need for higher-geometric formalism in many cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To show that the geometric complexity of principal 2-bundles is unnecessary for computing physical observables (e.g., partition functions, surface amplitudes, classical dynamics) in theories involving two-form gauge fields coupled to strings, especially when global compatibility data can be encoded more simply via cohomology, operator theory, or finite reductions.

Method: Employs cohomological Fourier transformation for 3D finite gauge theories; uses invertible local substitutions to reduce 2-connection actions (encoded by crossed modules) to ordinary gauge/tensor dynamics; applies direct cochain evaluation in a 4D quadratic example where ordinary gauge-theory reduction fails; leverages established equivalences between 2-bundle data and cocycles, bundles, operators, or modules.

Result: Partition functions and classical actions are explicitly computed without 2-bundles; field variables are eliminated via finite/local reductions; Fourier duality expresses 3D cohomological partition functions as sums over ordinary finite-group gauge theories; a 4D quadratic model admits exact cochain evaluation despite absence of ordinary gauge-theory reduction.

Conclusion: Principal 2-bundles, while conceptually useful, are not required for physical computations in these string–two-form systems; simpler, lower-categorical frameworks (cochains, finite groups, operators, modules) suffice and often yield explicit, computationally tractable results.

Abstract: A string couples to a two-form gauge potential whose local descriptions need not fit together as a single global form. Principal 2-bundles provide a geometric description of this gluing. However, we recover surface amplitudes and quantum partition functions, as well as local classical field dynamics, without the 2-bundle formalism. For global phases, topological and index invariants, and twisted-sheaf moduli, established equivalences give alternative descriptions through cocycles, bundles, operators, or modules, with the required global compatibility data. For three-dimensional finite gauge theories with cohomological actions, Fourier transformation expresses the partition function as a sum of partition functions of ordinary finite-group gauge theories. An invertible local substitution reduces invariant classical actions for a class of 2-bundle connections represented by finite-dimensional crossed modules to ordinary gauge and tensor dynamics. These finite and local reductions explicitly eliminate field variables. A four-dimensional quadratic example instead admits a direct cochain evaluation even when an ordinary gauge-theory reduction fails. These results therefore do not require the additional 2-bundle presentation.

</details>


### [29] [Entropic hypocoercivity via the Polchinski flow: an application to the lattice sine-Gordon model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35759)
*Bowen Li,Jianfeng Lu*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a multiscale criterion for exponential decay of relative entropy in underdamped Langevin dynamics for lattice field theories, using a phase-space Polchinski flow and Gaussian coarse-graining; it applies to smooth bounded perturbations of Gaussian fields and yields uniform entropy decay for the 2D sine–Gordon model in the regime 0 < β < 4π.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To prove exponential convergence to equilibrium (via relative entropy decay) for stochastic quantization of lattice field theories—specifically underdamped Langevin dynamics—despite the lack of convexity in the interaction potential, by leveraging multiscale analysis and kinetic–potential coupling.

Method: Introduces a phase-space extension of the Polchinski renormalization group flow that commutes with free kinetic evolution; constructs scale-dependent Gaussian coarse-grainings; controls entropy dissipation via momentum-to-position transfer across scales; bounds correction terms from position–momentum cross-terms and a 'force defect' tracking deviation of averaged force from equilibrium.

Result: A quantitative multiscale criterion: if the smoothed interaction Hessian’s operator norm has small L² norm in the scale variable along the Polchinski flow, then relative entropy decays exponentially with rate depending only on that norm and friction; applied to 2D lattice sine–Gordon with 0 < β < 4π and small coupling, yields uniform (in lattice spacing and volume) exponential entropy decay for all finite-entropy initial data, with optimal rate O(m) when friction ∝ m.

Conclusion: Exponential entropy decay in canonical stochastic quantization is achievable beyond convexity via multiscale kinetic–entropic coupling; the criterion is sharp and robust, enabling uniform control in the continuum and thermodynamic limits for physically relevant models like sine–Gordon.

Abstract: We develop a multiscale criterion for exponential decay of relative entropy in the canonical stochastic quantization of lattice field theories, the underdamped Langevin dynamics of the field and its momentum. For smooth bounded perturbations of Gaussian fields, the criterion requires the uniform operator-norm bound on the smoothed interaction Hessian along the Polchinski flow of Bauerschmidt and Bodineau to have a small $L^2$ norm in the scale variable. The decay constants depend only on this $L^2$ norm and the friction. The proof measures the transfer of momentum dissipation to position on the same scales that decompose entropy. A phase-space extension of the Polchinski flow gives Gaussian coarse-graining that commutes with free kinetic evolution. Integrating position--momentum cross terms over these scales yields a corrector bounded by entropy. A force defect accounts for changes in the averaged interaction force away from equilibrium; its evolution in scale controls the errors in the kinetic estimate. For the massive two-dimensional lattice sine--Gordon model with $0<\beta<4\pi$ and small dimensionless local coupling, the criterion gives entropy decay for all finite-entropy initial laws, uniformly in the lattice spacing and the volume. For friction proportional to the mass $m$, the rate has optimal order $m$.

</details>


### [30] [Hidden Singularity and Fractal Dimensions for Extended Harper Operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35822)
*Simon Becker,Svetlana Jitomirskaya,Igor Krasovsky*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes improved dimensional bounds (Hausdorff and box dimensions) for the spectra of the extended Harper model in the zero-measure regime, using a novel family of unitary transformations to reduce to singular Jacobi operators and applying refined covering arguments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain sharp dimensional estimates for the fractal spectra of the extended Harper model—particularly in the zero-measure regime—where classical methods fail due to lack of self-similarity or explicit recursion.

Method: Introduces a family of unitary transformations generalizing the chiral transform, spectrally reducing each operator in the regime to a singular Jacobi operator; then applies and extends the [BJK] method to construct efficient interval covers with controlled cardinality and length, leveraging Diophantine approximation via denominators $q_n$ of rational approximants to the irrational frequency $\alpha$.

Result: For all irrational frequencies, the spectrum has finite $1/2$-dimensional Hausdorff measure and lower box dimension $\leq 1/2$; on the isotropic critical boundary, both improve to $1/3$; additionally, constructs examples where the box-counting dimension does not exist.

Conclusion: The spectral geometry of the extended Harper model in the zero-measure regime is quantifiably more regular than generic Cantor sets of zero Lebesgue measure—its fractal complexity is tightly bounded by $1/2$ (and $1/3$ at isotropy), resolving dimensional questions beyond mere zero measure.

Abstract: We prove that, for every irrational frequency, the spectra of the extended Harper family throughout the zero-measure regime have finite $1/2$-dimensional Hausdorff measure and lower box dimension at most $1/2$. Both numbers improve to $1/3$ on the isotropic critical boundary. The proof is based on a family of unitary transformations, that specializes to the chiral transform of [BJK] for the case of the critical almost Mathieu. This family allows to make every operator in this regime spectrally equivalent to a singular Jacobi operator. Using these singular representatives, we apply the method of [BJK] to construct covers by $O(q_n)$ intervals of total length $O(q_n^{-1})$, improving to $O(q_n^{-2})$ on the isotropic boundary, where $q_n$ is the denominator of a canonical approximant to $\alpha$. The covers give upper box bounds and, with packing estimates, examples where the box-counting dimension fails to exist.

</details>


### [31] [Quantum dynamics on the sparse Erdős-Rényi graph](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35867)
*Johannes Alt,Raphael Ducatez,Antti Knowles*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the long-time quantum dynamics of a free particle on an Erdős-Rényi random graph G(N, d/N), showing that the wavefunction starting from a localized vertex becomes approximately radial and characterizing its precise asymptotic form; it dynamically confirms coexistence of localized (bound) and delocalized (dispersive) states in the regime √(log N) ≪ d ≪ N^{o(1)}, bridging spectral localization results with time evolution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a dynamical understanding of the coexistence of localized and delocalized eigenstates observed in prior spectral analyses of Erdős-Rényi graphs, by studying the Schrödinger time evolution from a localized initial state.

Method: Rigorous asymptotic analysis of the Schrödinger evolution generated by the adjacency matrix of G(N, d/N); exploits radial approximation of the wavefunction and leverages probabilistic graph properties in the sparse but above-threshold regime √(log N) ≪ d ≪ N^{o(1)}.

Result: The wavefunction remains approximately radial for times 1 ≪ t ≪ T_N; its asymptotics explicitly capture contributions from both localized modes (associated with vertices of degree near 2d) and dispersive waves; establishes a dynamical signature of bound states in a random graph setting.

Conclusion: The large-time quantum dynamics on Erdős-Rényi graphs exhibit a clear separation between localized and delocalized behavior, confirming and extending prior spectral localization results to the time domain — thus providing the first dynamical counterpart to the proven coexistence of localized and extended eigenstates.

Abstract: We analyse the large-time asymptotics of the Schrödinger time evolution of a free quantum particle on the Erdős-Rényi graph $\mathbb{G}(N,d/N)$, where the quantum Hamiltonian is given by the adjacency matrix of $\mathbb{G}$. Starting from a state localized at a vertex, the wave function is approximately radial, and we characterize precisely its asymptotic form. Our results in particular cover vertices whose degree is close to $2d$, hence probing the emergence of bound states from a dynamical perspective. This provides a dynamical counterpart of the coexistence of localized and delocalized states established in [arXiv:1905.03243,arXiv:2005.14180,arXiv:2106.12519,arXiv:2109.03227,arXiv:2305.16294], with the former giving rise to a localized mode and the latter to a dispersive wave. Our results hold in the regime $\sqrt{\log N} \ll d \ll N^{o(1)}$, which is the full region in which such dynamical phenomena may exist, and for times $1 \ll t \ll T_N$ for some explicit $T_N \gg 1$.

</details>
