### [32] [Dark Matter phase space distribution under a fully relativistic treatment. Eddington inversion according to general relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33150)
*Jaime Hoefken Zink,Pellegrino Piantadosi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a fully relativistic extension of Eddington inversion to reconstruct the dark matter phase-space distribution function from its density profile in strong gravitational fields, using the Tolman-Oppenheimer-Volkoff equations and a regularized Abel-type inversion; it demonstrates significant deviations from Newtonian predictions near Sgr A*, with implications for dark matter capture by neutron stars.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To accurately model dark matter phase-space distributions in strong-field regimes (e.g., near supermassive black holes) where Newtonian Eddington inversion fails, and to assess relativistic corrections to dark matter phenomenology such as capture rates.

Method: Formulates dark matter as an effective perfect fluid in a static, spherically symmetric spacetime; solves the Tolman-Oppenheimer-Volkoff equations for the gravitational background; derives a relativistic Abel-type integral inversion for the distribution function in terms of energy measured at infinity; regularizes finite-boundary divergence; applies the formalism to a Milky Way model with a generalized NFW halo and Gondolo-Silk spike around Sgr A*; computes relativistic vs. Newtonian velocity distributions and dark matter capture rates on neutron stars.

Result: The relativistic distribution recovers classical Eddington inversion in the weak-field limit; velocity distributions diverge significantly within ~10⁻⁵ pc of Sgr A*, with relativistic peak speed ≈2× Newtonian; relativistic capture factor near Sgr A* is ~10× smaller than Maxwell-Boltzmann prediction and Newtonian Eddington overestimates it by ~2×.

Conclusion: Relativistic phase-space reconstruction is essential for accurate dark matter phenomenology in strong gravitational fields—particularly for spike modeling, velocity statistics, and capture calculations near compact objects.

Abstract: We develop a fully relativistic extension of Eddington inversion to reconstruct the phase-space distribution function of dark matter from its density profile. Treating dark matter as an effective perfect fluid in a static, spherically symmetric spacetime, we use the Tolman-Oppenheimer-Volkoff equations to determine the gravitational background and derive an Abel-type inversion that yields the distribution function in terms of the conserved particle energy measured at infinity. We show that the resulting expression recovers the classical Eddington inversion in the weak-field, nonrelativistic limit. We also investigate the finite-boundary contribution, which can introduce an unphysical divergence, and present a regularization procedure that removes it. We apply our method to a Milky Way model consisting of a generalized Navarro-Frenk-White halo and a Gondolo-Silk dark-matter spike around Sagittarius A$^*$, neglecting baryonic contributions. The relativistic and Newtonian velocity distributions agree at radii far from the Galactic center but begin to differ at distances of order $10^{-5}\,\mathrm{pc}$. Closer to the black hole, the relativistic peak speed can reach approximately twice the Newtonian prediction. Finally, we derive a general expression for dark-matter capture by neutron stars that accommodates arbitrary ambient velocity distributions. Near Sagittarius A$^*$, the relativistic capture factor is nearly an order of magnitude smaller than the Maxwell-Boltzmann prediction, while the Newtonian Eddington result can overestimate it by a factor of approximately two. These results demonstrate the importance of relativistic phase-space reconstruction for dark-matter phenomenology in strong gravitational fields.

</details>


### [33] [Catastrophe Structure and Stability of Axion Stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33549)
*Fedor V Kusmartsev,Joudy J F Beek,Jack C M Hughes*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a unified stability analysis of axion stars—self-gravitating Bose–Einstein condensates of axions—using Schrödinger–Poisson dynamics and catastrophe theory, identifying stable dilute/dense branches separated by unstable ones via A₂–A₄ catastrophes; it incorporates repulsive sextic interactions and extends results to full cosine and QCD-corrected potentials, distinguishing collective-coordinate from full radial stability and delineating the validity regime of the non-relativistic approximation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether axion stars can persist as viable dark-matter structures and produce observable astrophysical signatures, a rigorous and unified understanding of their dynamical stability is required—especially across different axion potentials and interaction regimes.

Method: Combines non-relativistic Schrödinger–Poisson equations with catastrophe theory; derives effective equations and conserved quantities from the Einstein–Klein–Gordon system with periodic (cosine/QCD-corrected) axion potential; constructs one-parameter star solutions; employs Gaussian variational ansatz to obtain analytic energy functional E(R; N); identifies equilibrium bifurcations as Arnold A₂, A₃, A₄ catastrophes; distinguishes collective-coordinate stability from full radial perturbative stability; assesses domain of validity for non-relativistic approximation.

Result: Reveals two stable axion-star branches (dilute and dense) separated by an unstable branch due to repulsive sextic interaction; fold conditions for equilibria are analytically tractable in sextic approximation and numerically evaluable for full cosine and QCD-corrected potentials; identifies model-dependent critical masses governing collapse, bosenova emission, and black-hole formation.

Conclusion: Catastrophe theory provides a compact, robust classification of axion-star phases and stability transitions; the framework unifies treatment across axion potentials, clarifies limits of the non-relativistic approximation, and links structural instabilities to concrete astrophysical outcomes.

Abstract: Axion stars are self-gravitating Bose--Einstein condensates of QCD axions or axion-like particles. Their stability determines whether they can survive as dark-matter structures and what astrophysical signatures they may produce. Here we combine a non-relativistic Schrödinger--Poisson description with catastrophe theory to develop a unified picture of axion-star stability. Beginning with the Einstein--Klein--Gordon system for a periodic axion potential, we derive the effective equations of motion and conserved quantities and construct a one-parameter family of star-like solutions. A Gaussian variational ansatz reduces the problem to an analytic energy functional $E(R;N)$. Within this model, equilibria appear and disappear through Arnold $A_2$, $A_3$, and $A_4$ catastrophes as the particle number $N$ and interaction strengths vary. A repulsive sextic interaction produces two stable branches---dilute and dense axion stars---separated by an unstable branch. The same catastrophe criteria apply beyond the sextic truncation: for the full cosine and QCD-corrected potentials, the fold conditions follow from the complete variational energy and can be evaluated numerically. We also separate stability within the collective-coordinate ansatz from full radial perturbative stability and specify the range in which the non-relativistic treatment is reliable. This framework provides a compact classification of axion-star phases and identifies model-dependent critical masses associated with collapse, bosenova-like emission, and black-hole formation.

</details>


### [34] [A Low Scale for Quark-Lepton Unification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33152)
*Lawrence J. Hall,Claudio Andrea Manzari,Clara Murgui*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies near-coincidences in Yukawa coupling unification scales (e.g., y_τ = y_b and y_μ = 3y_s at ~10⁶ GeV; y_e ≈ y_d/3 at ~10⁸ GeV) as evidence for low-scale Pati–Salam unification (~10⁶–10⁷ GeV), proposes two concrete models with predictive flavor structures, and links them to testable leptoquark-mediated μ→e conversion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The observed fermion mass ratios (e.g., m_τ/m_b ≈ 1, m_μ/m_s ≈ 3, m_e/m_d ≈ 1/3) suggest underlying GUT-scale relations; the paper asks whether these arise from a common, testable unification framework at accessible energies rather than traditional high-scale GUTs.

Method: Renormalization-group evolution of SM Yukawa couplings including finite threshold corrections and flavor mixing; construction of minimal Pati-Salam models with hierarchical flavor textures and symmetric Yukawa matrices; computation of vector leptoquark couplings and μ→e conversion rates.

Result: Two viable low-scale Pati-Salam models: (i) full three-generation unification at 4.6⁺¹·²₋₀·₉×10⁶ GeV; (ii) symmetric Yukawa model at 1.5⁺⁰·⁷₋₀·₅×10⁷ GeV predicting |V_cb| = 0.042±0.002; both fix light-family texture and predict testable μ→e conversion rates.

Conclusion: Fermion mass patterns point to quark-lepton unification at ~10⁶–10⁷ GeV via Pati-Salam symmetry; the models are falsifiable via upcoming μ→e conversion experiments (e.g., Mu2e, COMET, PRISM/PRIME).

Abstract: The measured fermion masses may be pointing to the scale of quark-lepton unification. Studying the renormalization-group evolution of the Standard Model Yukawa couplings, we find that $y_\tau=y_b$ and $y_\mu=3y_s$ occur at nearly the same scale, around $10^6$ GeV, while the first generation reaches the Georgi-Jarlskog relation $y_e\simeq y_d/3$ at order $10^8$ GeV and can arise from the same Clebsch structure through a simple hierarchical flavor texture. This pattern naturally suggests low-scale Pati-Salam symmetry. We show that flavor mixing and finite threshold corrections preserve this picture. If the electron mass has an independent origin, the two heavier-generation relations can be realized in theories with a unification scale as low as $(5$-$7)\times10^5$ GeV. We identify two simple theories that accommodate all three relations and in which the measured masses fix the flavor structure. The first unifies all three generations at $4.6^{+1.2}_{-0.9}\times10^6$ GeV; the second, with symmetric Yukawa matrices, at $1.5^{+0.7}_{-0.5}\times10^7$ GeV, and predicts $|V_{cb}|=0.042\pm0.002$. Their shared light-family texture fixes the relevant vector-leptoquark couplings, making $\mu\to e$ conversion in nuclei a direct test of unification: upcoming searches will probe part of the region selected by the two theories, and proposed facilities could cover it completely.

</details>


### [35] [Nucleon polarizabilities revisited: insights from the Bernabéu-Ericson-Ferro Fontan-Tarrach sum rule](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34276)
*Xiong-Hui Cao,Ling-Yun Dai,Feng-Kun Guo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides a dispersive evaluation of the BEFT sum rule for nucleon dipole polarizabilities (α_E1 − β_M1), using hyperbolic dispersion relations to cleanly separate s- and t-channel contributions. It updates s-channel input with modern πN multipoles and non-πN estimates, and improves t-channel input with dispersive γγ→ππ/KK̄ data and Roy–Steiner solutions for ππ/KK̄→NN̄, including the isovector πη/KK̄ (I_t=1) channel. The result resolves prior inconsistencies and yields high-precision, model-independent values for proton and neutron electric and magnetic dipole polarizabilities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing discrepancy in earlier BEFT evaluations—particularly the overestimation driven by the scalar-isoscalar two-pion t-channel—and to provide a model-independent, dispersive benchmark for nucleon polarizabilities, especially for the neutron where experimental constraints are weak.

Method: Uses hyperbolic dispersion relations to isolate s- and t-channel contributions without double counting; incorporates modern single-pion photoproduction multipoles and non-πN inelastic estimates for the s-channel; employs contemporary dispersive γγ→ππ/KK̄ (I_t=0,1) amplitudes and Roy–Steiner equation solutions for ππ/KK̄→NN̄ in the t-channel; combines results with the Baldin sum rule to extract individual α_E1 and β_M1.

Result: Obtains precise values: α_E1^p = 11.17(52), β_M1^p = 2.84(52), α_E1^n = 12.18(59), β_M1^n = 3.03(59) ×10⁻⁴ fm³; resolves prior puzzle in BEFT evaluations; achieves proton precision comparable to state-of-the-art dispersive and χEFT extractions; provides first high-precision independent dispersive benchmark for neutron polarizabilities.

Conclusion: The improved dispersive BEFT analysis delivers robust, model-independent nucleon polarizabilities with controlled uncertainties, validates the consistency of dispersion theory across channels, and establishes a new benchmark—especially for the neutron—against which future experiments and theoretical calculations can be tested.

Abstract: We present a complete dispersive evaluation of the Bernabéu-Ericson-Ferro Fontan-Tarrach (BEFT) sum rule for the nucleon dipole polarizability difference $\alpha_{E1}-\beta_{M1}$. The analysis is based on hyperbolic dispersion relations, which separate the $s$- and $t$-channel contributions without double counting and are particularly suited to backward real Compton scattering. This framework makes it possible to precisely determine the polarizability difference from independent dispersive input rather than from fits to low-energy Compton scattering data. The $s$-channel part is updated with modern single-pion photoproduction multipoles and model estimates of non-$\pi N$ inelastic channels. For the $t$-channel, we use contemporary dispersive input for $\gamma\gamma\to\pi\pi/K\bar K_{I_t=0}$ together with Roy-Steiner equation solutions for $\pi\pi/K\bar K_{I_t=0}\to N\bar N$, and we extend the calculation to the isovector $\pi\eta/K\bar K_{I_t=1}$ channel associated with the $a_0(980)$. The resulting treatment resolves the long-standing puzzle of earlier BEFT evaluations driven by the scalar-isoscalar two-pion channel and gives a controlled estimate of the subleading isovector contribution. Combining the resulting BEFT values with the Baldin sum rule, we obtain, in units of $10^{-4}\,\mathrm{fm}^3$, \[ \alpha_{E1}^p=11.17(52),\quad \beta_{M1}^p=2.84(52),\quad \alpha_{E1}^n=12.18(59),\quad \beta_{M1}^n=3.03(59). \] For the proton, these values provide a benchmark with a precision comparable to state-of-the-art dispersive and chiral effective field theory extractions. For the neutron, whose polarizabilities are much less directly constrained experimentally, the result provides an independent dispersive benchmark with an uncertainty comparable to that of the proton.

</details>


### [36] [Nucleon tomography from a calibrated diquark spectator model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34602)
*Jakub Terlecki,Shohini Bhattacharya,Krzysztof Cichy*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates twist-2 vector generalized parton distributions (GPDs) H^q and E^q in a spectator diquark model (scalar + axial-vector), deriving for the first time an analytic expression for the axial-vector contribution to the helicity-flip GPD E^q; it introduces a momentum-transfer-dependent cutoff, calibrates parameters using proton-only vs. proton+neutron observables (reducing uncertainties and better constraining flavor dependence), and computes valence PDFs and impact-parameter distributions—revealing x- and flavor-dependent transverse quark structure in unpolarized and transversely polarized nucleons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified, phenomenologically constrained description of both longitudinal-momentum and transverse-spatial structure of valence quarks in the nucleon via generalized parton distributions (GPDs), particularly addressing the poorly constrained helicity-flip GPD E^q and its flavor decomposition.

Method: Spectator diquark model incorporating both scalar and axial-vector diquark configurations; analytic computation of twist-2 vector GPDs H^q and E^q; introduction of a momentum-transfer-dependent quasi-hard cutoff for transverse-momentum regularization; two-parameter calibration schemes—(i) using only proton electromagnetic moments/radii and gravitational form factor constraints, and (ii) jointly fitting proton and neutron observables—to determine quark/diquark masses, scalar/axial-vector mixing, and transverse-momentum scale.

Result: Explicit analytic expression for axial-vector-diquark contribution to E^q; compatible but tighter parameter ranges from joint proton–neutron calibration; zero-skewness GPDs H^{u,d}(x,0,t) and E^{u,d}(x,0,t); derived valence-quark PDFs and impact-parameter distributions showing x-dependent central localization and flavor-asymmetric distortion under transverse nucleon polarization.

Conclusion: The model delivers a consistent, unified phenomenological framework for nucleon structure, simultaneously describing longitudinal momentum distributions (via PDFs) and transverse spatial distributions (via impact-parameter densities), with improved flavor separation and reduced parameter uncertainty when neutron data are included.

Abstract: Generalized parton distributions (GPDs) provide a unified description of the longitudinal-momentum and transverse spatial structure of the nucleon. We calculate the twist-2 vector GPDs $H^{q}$ and $E^{q}$ in a spectator model containing both scalar and axial-vector diquark configurations. A central new result is the explicit analytic expression for the axial-vector-diquark contribution to the helicity-flip GPD $E^{q}$. To regulate the transverse-momentum integrations while retaining a physically sensible dependence on the momentum transfer, we introduce a momentum-transfer-dependent quasi-hard cutoff. The model parameters are determined through two complementary calibrations: one using proton observables only and another simultaneously including proton and neutron observables. Electromagnetic moments and radii, together with selected constraints on gravitational form factors, determine the quark and diquark masses, the relative scalar and axial-vector contributions, and the transverse-momentum scale. The two calibrations yield compatible parameter ranges, while the inclusion of neutron observables reduces the parameter uncertainties and more strongly constrains the flavor structure. From the resulting zero-skewness GPDs $H^{u,d}(x,0,t)$ and $E^{u,d}(x,0,t)$, we calculate the valence-quark PDFs and the impact-parameter distributions of unpolarized up and down quarks in both unpolarized and transversely polarized nucleons. The transverse distributions exhibit the expected localization of large-$x$ quarks near the transverse center of momentum and a flavor-dependent distortion when the nucleon is transversely polarized. Our results provide a unified phenomenological description of the longitudinal-momentum and transverse-position structure of valence quarks in the nucleon.

</details>


### [37] [Two-Pion Exchange Paradigm in Hadron-Hadron Interactions from Lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34655)
*Tetsuo Hatsuda*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses the HAL QCD method in (2+1)-flavor lattice QCD near the physical pion mass to study hadron-hadron interactions, finding attractive nucleon–flavor-singlet potentials and clear lattice evidence for two-pion exchange dominance in the long-range region where one-pion exchange is forbidden.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate nonperturbative hadron-hadron interactions—particularly two-pion exchange (TPE)—in QCD near the physical pion mass, and to assess the capability of the HAL QCD method to resolve universal long-distance dynamics.

Method: HAL QCD method applied to (2+1)-flavor lattice QCD simulations at nearly physical pion mass (m_π ≈ 146 MeV); extraction of nucleon–flavor-singlet hadron potentials (φ–N, J/ψ–N, η_c–N) and analysis of their distance dependence, especially in the long-range regime where OPE is forbidden.

Result: All studied nucleon–flavor-singlet potentials are attractive at all distances; unambiguous lattice signatures of TPE dominance observed in the long-range region where one-pion exchange is absent.

Conclusion: The HAL QCD method successfully captures universal long-distance phenomena—specifically TPE—directly from first-principles QCD, validating its efficacy for studying multi-hadron forces beyond perturbation theory.

Abstract: We study hadron-hadron interactions in (2+1)-flavor lattice QCD near the physical pion mass ($m_\pi \simeq 146\mathrm{MeV}$) using the HAL QCD method, with particular emphasis on two-pion exchange (TPE). The nucleon--flavor-singlet hadron potentials such as $\phi$-$N$, $J/\psi$-$N$, and $\eta_c$-$N$ are found to be attractive at all distances. Moreover, in the long-range region where one-pion exchange is forbidden, we observe lattice signatures consistent with TPE dominance. These results demonstrate that the HAL QCD method can extract universal long-distance phenomena, such as TPE, directly from nonperturbative QCD dynamics.

</details>


### [38] [LLMs Can Tackle High Energy Theoretical Physics, yet Gaps Remain: Part I, Quantum Field Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33134)
*Tong Liu,Bohan Li,Gongrui Cheng,Zixiong Yu,Yulan He,Volker Tresp*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces QFT-Bench, a 75-problem benchmark for evaluating LLMs on graduate- to research-level quantum field theory tasks; finds frontier models achieve >95% accuracy but reveal persistent errors in conventions, instruction following, and reasoning faithfulness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantitatively assess how far LLMs can go in high-energy theoretical physics—specifically Quantum Field Theory—where deep conceptual understanding, symbolic derivation, and convention-aware reasoning are essential, yet current evaluation benchmarks are lacking.

Method: Constructs QFT-Bench: a rigorously curated set of 75 closed-form, reference-answered problems spanning core QFT subareas (e.g., Feynman diagrams, renormalization, path integrals); evaluates 39 LLMs using hybrid assessment—symbolic verification for exact answers + LLM-based judge for partial credit and reasoning quality; analyzes chain-of-thought (CoT) faithfulness via logical consistency between reasoning steps and final answer.

Result: Frontier models achieve >95% accuracy on QFT-Bench; however, errors persist across all tiers—strong models err in conventions/instructions, weak models fail at conceptual or derivational levels; CoT analysis reveals widespread reasoning unfaithfulness: correct answers often stem from heuristic matching or luck, not logically sound derivations.

Conclusion: LLMs show promising but incomplete competence in QFT: high accuracy masks fragility in physical reasoning, symbolic fidelity, and procedural correctness; QFT-Bench establishes a rigorous, domain-specific evaluation framework and highlights the need for improved reasoning grounding, convention awareness, and faithful CoT generation in physics-AI systems.

Abstract: Beyond math and code, Large language models (LLMs) are beginning to tackle problems that have traditionally required substantial training for theoretical physicists, but it remains unclear how far this capability extends and where it might break. As a first step toward pushing LLMs in high-energy theoretical physics, we introduce QFT-Bench, a benchmark of 75 graduate- to research-level problems requiring extensive reasoning and derivation across core Quantum Field Theory (QFT) subareas. Each question has a well-defined target and closed-form reference answer. Evaluation combines symbolic verification with LLM-based judge. Across 39 proprietary and open-source models, frontier models already demonstrate substantial capabilities on nontrivial QFT calculations, with the strongest models exceeding 95% accuracy. At the same time, clear gaps remain. Even strong models still make errors in calculations, conventions, and instruction following, while weaker models often fail at the level of physical concepts or derivations. We further monitor models' CoTs and show that current models exhibit nontrivial reasoning unfaithfulness, i.e., they could reach correct answers but the reasoning itself does not justify and logically support them. These results provide a quantitative assessment of current LLM capabilities in QFT and a first step toward broader evaluation in high-energy theoretical physics.

</details>


### [39] [Muon Tridents at FASER and NA64$μ$: Motivation, Predictions, and Prospects for Precision Measurements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33153)
*Reinaldo Francener,Victor P. Goncalves,Felix Kling,Gabriel Rabelo-Soares*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies muon trident scattering in muon-nucleus interactions at the FASER experiment using LHC Run 3 data, achieving percent-level precision on the cross-section—the first measurement at TeV energies—and extends analysis to NA64μ to probe energy dependence across a broad range.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exploit the high-energy muon flux at the LHC's far-forward region for studying rare electromagnetic processes—specifically muon trident scattering—at unprecedented TeV-scale energies, where no prior measurements exist.

Result: Prediction of >10⁵ muon trident events in FASER Run 3; percent-level cross-section precision achievable; first observation of muon trident scattering at TeV energies projected; combined FASER + NA64μ coverage spans a broad, previously unexplored energy range.

Conclusion: Muon trident scattering is measurable at TeV energies with FASER’s spectrometer, enabling precision tests of QED in extreme kinematic regimes; synergy with NA64μ opens a new window to study the energy evolution of this fundamental process.

Abstract: The intense flux of high-energy muons produced in the far-forward region of $pp$ collisions at the LHC provides a unique opportunity to study rare electromagnetic interactions at TeV energies. In this work, we investigate the muon trident scattering in muon-nucleus interactions at the FASER experiment, which is expected to have recorded more than $10^5$ such events. The theoretical description of the process is reviewed and improved. Predictions for the total event rates and differential distributions expected during Run~3 of the LHC are presented, taking into account realistic detector acceptance and the main background contributions. The dependence of the predictions on the treatment of the nuclear form factors and the impact of higher-order QED effects such as Coulomb corrections are investigated. Our results indicate that the muon trident process can be measured for the first time at TeV energies using FASER's spectrometer, with a projected precision on the cross-section at the percent level. We also extended our analysis to the NA64$\mu$ experiment and showed that, together with FASER, these experiments can probe the energy dependence of the muon trident cross-section over a broad unexplored energy range.

Abstract: We calculate the relation between heavy-quark masses defined in the on-shell and $\overline{\rm MS}$ renormalization schemes up to four-loop order in Quantum Chromodynamics, accounting for a second, non-zero quark mass. We employ a semi-analytical method that yields precise results valid for all values of the mass ratio. In particular, our approach enables generalized expansions for small and large values of the second quark mass, incorporating all logarithmic contributions. For the limiting cases of a vanishing or equal second quark mass, we significantly improve upon the precision of known results for 18 of the 23 colour structures.

</details>


### [41] [Probing Orbital Angular Momentum Distributions in Elastic Vector Meson Production in Longitudinally Polarized Electron--Proton Scattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33255)

TL;DR: This paper calculates the longitudinal double-spin asymmetry (DSA) for elastic vector meson production in polarized e-p collisions at small x, identifying the cosφ_{kΔ} azimuthal harmonic as a sensitive probe of quark/gluon orbital angular momentum (OAM) distributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To access quark and gluon orbital angular momentum (OAM) distributions at small Bjorken-x, where conventional collinear factorization breaks down and small-x QCD dynamics dominate.

Result: The cosφ_{kΔ} azimuthal harmonic of the longitudinal DSA is shown to be directly sensitive to the OAM-dependent terms in the polarized Wilson line correlators—specifically, to the small-x limit of the generalized parton distribution (GPD)-like or TMD-like OAM-sensitive structures; results are process- and meson-species independent in leading approximation.

Conclusion: The cosφ_{kΔ} modulation in the longitudinal DSA for small-Δ⊥ elastic vector meson production constitutes a novel, theoretically clean, and experimentally viable observable to constrain quark and gluon orbital angular momentum at small x, complementary to exclusive deep inelastic scattering and other small-x spin probes.

Abstract: We consider elastic vector meson production in longitudinally polarized electron-proton collisions at small $x$. Inspired by previous pioneering proposals in the literature, we calculate the numerator of the longitudinal double-spin asymmetry (DSA) within the polarized small-$x$ formalism for both the transversely and longitudinally polarized vector mesons. Our calculation demonstrates that the $\cos\phi_{k\Delta}$ azimuthal harmonic of the DSA for the elastic vector meson production at small transverse momentum transfer (with $\phi_{k\Delta}$ the azimuthal angle between the transverse momenta of the outgoing electron and meson in the dipole/Trento frame) provides a promising probe of the orbital angular momentum (OAM) distributions at small $x$. Our results can be applied to several different vector meson species, thus opening up a new class of observables that can be used to extract OAM distributions from experimental data.

</details>


### [42] [A Tool-Grounded AI Agent for Gauge Coupling Unification and Proton Decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33593)
*Shaikh Saad*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: proton_agent is a tool-grounded AI agent for natural-language-driven computational analysis of grand unified theories, supporting gauge coupling unification and proton decay calculations with full local deployment support for open-source LLMs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between high-energy theoretical physics research and accessible, reproducible, and interactive computational tools—enabling physicists to explore grand unified theories (GUTs) via natural language without sacrificing numerical accuracy, scientific rigor, or deployment flexibility.

Method: Design and implementation of a deterministic, modular scientific backend integrated with an LLM-based agent; supports parsing natural-language queries, tool selection (e.g., beta-function computation, RG evolution, proton decay amplitude evaluation), execution of numerical/modeling tasks across multiple GUT frameworks (SU(5), SO(10), various intermediate symmetries), and visualization; compatible with both local open-source LLMs and commercial APIs.

Result: A fully functional, open, and reproducible framework that computes one- and two-loop gauge beta coefficients, performs multi-stage RG evolution, evaluates dimension-five and -six proton decay amplitudes in SUSY and non-SUSY GUTs, enables parameter scans (grid, random, MCMC), experimental comparisons, and generates publication-ready 2D/3D visualizations.

Conclusion: proton_agent establishes a new paradigm for domain-specific, tool-grounded AI agents in theoretical particle physics—demonstrating that rigorous, numerically exact scientific computation can be seamlessly integrated with LLM-driven interaction while preserving full local control and transparency.

Abstract: We present proton_agent, a tool-grounded AI agent that enables natural-language interaction with computational tools for gauge coupling unification and proton decay in grand unified theories. The agent translates user requests into computational tasks, selects and executes the appropriate tools, and interprets their outputs. All numerical calculations, model evaluations, experimental comparisons, parameter scans, and visualizations are handled by a deterministic scientific backend to ensure numerical accuracy and reproducibility. A central objective of this framework is to enable fully local deployment using free and open-source LLMs, while retaining compatibility with commercial LLM APIs. The framework computes one- and two-loop gauge beta coefficients for user-defined scalar and fermion multiplets and performs gauge coupling evolution through the Standard Model and various intermediate symmetry-breaking stages of $SO(10)$, including $SU(3)_C \times SU(2)_L \times SU(2)_R \times U(1)_{B-L}$, $SU(3)_C \times SU(2)_L \times U(1)_R \times U(1)_{B-L}$, $SU(4)_C \times SU(2)_L \times U(1)_R$, and $SU(4)_C \times SU(2)_L \times SU(2)_R$. For proton decay, the framework evaluates gauge-mediated dimension-six contributions in non-supersymmetric $SU(5)$ and $SO(10)$, as well as dressed dimension-five contributions in their supersymmetric counterparts. It supports benchmark calculations for user-specified parameter points, random and grid-based parameter scans, Markov-chain Monte Carlo exploration, comparisons with experimental limits, and publication-ready two- and three-dimensional visualizations. We describe the agent architecture, demonstrate its capabilities through representative benchmark calculations and regression tests, and discuss its current physics scope.

</details>


### [43] [Soft factor of quasi-TMDPDFs from the soft limit of the quasi beam function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33638)

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that the soft factor required for matching quasi-TMDPDFs to TMDPDFs is inherently contained within the quasi beam function, not separately constructed; using one-loop computation and region decomposition, it identifies hard, collinear, and soft contributions, with the soft region—after hemisphere folding—reproducing the square root of the lightcone soft function and reduced soft function; it provides an operator-level definition of the soft factor as a vacuum matrix element of soft Wilson lines, enabling all-order analysis and confirming all-order reproduction of the Collins-Soper kernel.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the ad hoc separation of the soft factor from the quasi beam function in large-momentum effective theory (LaMET) matching of quasi-TMDPDFs to physical TMDPDFs, and to establish a consistent, operator-based foundation for the soft subtraction.

Abstract: The quasi-TMDPDF in large-momentum effective theory is related to the TMDPDF through a soft factor, which has so far been constructed separately from the quasi beam function. We show that the soft factor is contained in the quasi beam function itself. Computing the one-loop quasi beam function and decomposing it by the method of regions, we find that the hard and collinear regions give the matching coefficient and the lightcone beam function. The soft region involves only one lightlike direction, yet, once the two hemispheres of the gluon momentum are folded onto each other, it reproduces the square root of the lightcone soft function together with the reduced soft function. The analysis leads to an operator definition of the soft factor, as the vacuum matrix element of the soft Wilson lines into which the quasi beam function turns. This definition provides the basis for an all-order analysis, and it shows that the Collins-Soper kernel is reproduced to all orders.

</details>

  <summary>Details</summary>

Method: Perturbative QCD calculation at leading order in α_s and up to O(q²) in the nonrelativistic expansion; includes quark-annihilation, two-gluon, and electromagnetic (photon emission from quark lines) diagrams; incorporates light-cone distribution amplitudes for η/η′, SU(3)-breaking light-quark mass effects, η–η′ mixing via two representative schemes (e.g., FKS vs. transition-form-factor–inferred), and heavy-pseudoscalar (η_c, η_b) mixing contributions treated as corrections to the light-meson wave functions.

Result: Quark annihilation dominates; EM interference is constructive; gluon contribution strongly suppressed; relativistic corrections reduce Υ(1S) rates by few %, increase Υ(2S) rates by 17–30%; B(Υ(1S)→γη′)/B(Υ(1S)→γη) differs by ~factor 5 between mixing schemes; at μ = m_Υ/2, central predictions exceed experimental upper limit by ~5× and ~7×; inclusion of η_b mixing induces destructive interference, reconciling theory with data when μ = m_Υ and mixing parameters are taken from transition form factors.

Conclusion: The Υ → γη(′) decays are theoretically robust probes of η–η′ mixing and heavy-quarkonium–light-meson couplings; agreement with experiment requires careful treatment of scale dependence, relativistic corrections, and η_b admixture—highlighting the importance of heavy-pseudoscalar mixing in precision QCD phenomenology of quarkonium radiative transitions.

Abstract: We calculate the radiative decays $\Upsilon(1S),\Upsilon(2S)\rightarrow\gamma\eta^{(\prime)}$ in perturbative quantum chromodynamics through order $q^{2}$ in the relativistic expansion, including the quark-content, two-gluon, and electromagnetic contributions. The annihilation rates depend weakly on the tested light-cone distribution amplitudes and light-quark masses. Relativistic corrections reduce the $\Upsilon(1S)$ rates by a few percent but enhance the $\Upsilon(2S)$ rates by approximately $17\%$--$30\%$. The ratio of the $\gamma\eta^{\prime}$ and $\gamma\eta$ branching fractions is sensitive to the light-meson mixing parameters, with predictions differing by about a factor of $5$ between two representative schemes. At a scale of half the quarkonium mass, however, the $\Upsilon(1S)\rightarrow\gamma\eta^{\prime}$ central predictions in the two mixing schemes exceed the experimental upper limit by factors of approximately $5$ and $7$. We also examine the heavy-pseudoscalar admixtures in the light mesons. The $\eta_c$ contribution is small, whereas $\eta_b$ mixing can substantially modify the rates through interference. Destructive interference alleviates the discrepancy. With the scale set to the quarkonium mass, the prediction in the mixing scheme inferred from transition form factors is compatible with the upper limit within the input uncertainty.

</details>

*Weiqiang Yang,Sibo Zhang,Supriya Pan,Luis A. Anchordoqui,Eleonora Di Valentino*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper tests three interacting dark matter–dark energy models using CMB, BAO (DESI DR2), and multiple SN Ia datasets; all models yield negative preferred effective neutrino masses—contradicting oscillation constraints—and fail to resolve the DESI neutrino-mass tension, even with ~3σ evidence for nonzero coupling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether interacting dark matter–dark energy models can resolve the tension between cosmological neutrino mass bounds (e.g., from DESI) and the minimum mass implied by neutrino oscillation experiments.

Method: Bayesian parameter inference using cosmic microwave background (Planck), DESI DR2 baryon acoustic oscillations, and Type Ia supernova data (PantheonPlus, Union3, DES-Dovekie); models include interacting vacuum energy and interacting dark energy with constant w in phantom/quintessence regimes; analyses performed with fixed vs. free effective neutrino mass (m_eff^nu), with and without positivity prior.

Result: All three interaction scenarios prefer negative m_eff^nu across all dataset combinations when the positivity prior is relaxed; no 95% CL interval includes the minimal oscillation-driven mass (~0.06 eV); nonzero coupling is favored at ~3σ when m_eff^nu is free, but best-fit masses remain negative.

Conclusion: The considered interacting DM–DE models—with either vacuum or constant-w DE—do not alleviate the DESI neutrino-mass tension; negative preferred m_eff^nu indicates a fundamental inconsistency between these models and established neutrino physics.

Abstract: We constrain three interacting dark matter--dark energy scenarios using cosmic microwave background measurements, DESI DR2 baryon acoustic oscillations, and the PantheonPlus, Union3, and DES-Dovekie Type Ia supernova compilations. We consider interacting vacuum energy and interacting dark energy with a constant equation of state in the phantom and quintessence regimes, analyzing each scenario with either a fixed total neutrino mass or a freely varying effective neutrino mass. When the positivity prior is relaxed, all three scenarios favor negative effective neutrino masses for every dataset combination. Although some combinations permit small positive masses, none of the 95\% confidence intervals reaches the minimum mass required by neutrino oscillation experiments. Allowing the effective neutrino mass to vary produces significances for a nonzero coupling reaching approximately $3\sigma$, but the preferred masses remain negative. These results demonstrate that the interaction and constant dark energy equations of state considered here fail to resolve the DESI neutrino-mass tension.

</details>


### [47] [Dissecting flavor tagging with graph neural networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33757)
*Ankita Budhraja,Sunny Kumar Singh,Balbeer Singh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how ParticleNet, a graph neural network for quark/gluon jet tagging, distributes its discriminating power between jet constituent multiplicity and substructure—finding that it jointly leverages multiplicity, jet scale (reconstructed from constituents), and radial spread, especially at low/intermediate multiplicities where multiplicity alone is ambiguous.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To disentangle how much of ParticleNet's quark/gluon discrimination arises from constituent multiplicity versus other structural features—differentially across jet transverse momentum and multiplicity—given that multiplicity itself is strongly correlated with parton type.

Method: Trains ParticleNet on matched Z+jet Pythia samples (80–400 GeV pT, rapidity-matched) and performs controlled ablation studies: (i) analyzing output behavior vs. multiplicity within pT bins; (ii) evaluating calibration and spread of gluon probabilities at fixed multiplicity; (iii) rescaling radial spread of constituents while holding multiplicity fixed to isolate geometric sensitivity.

Result: ParticleNet learns the pT-dependent shift in the multiplicity threshold where quark/gluon likelihoods cross; its output decomposes into a multiplicity log-likelihood ratio plus a residual substructure term; at fixed multiplicity, it correctly reclassifies 20–34% of jets whose multiplicity favors the wrong class; it assigns higher gluon probability to broader (larger radial spread) jets, most prominently at low/intermediate multiplicities.

Conclusion: ParticleNet’s discrimination is not driven solely by multiplicity but arises from a joint, calibrated use of multiplicity, reconstructed jet scale, and substructure—particularly radial spread—with substructure playing a decisive role where multiplicity is least informative.

Abstract: Graph neural networks such as ParticleNet are widely used for quark/gluon jet tagging. We study how the discriminating power of such a network is distributed between constituent multiplicity and the remaining structure of the jet, differentially in jet transverse momentum and multiplicity. To this end, we train ParticleNet on quark and gluon jets from Z+jet production in Pythia, with the two classes matched in transverse momentum and rapidity over the wide range $80\leq p_{T}^{\rm jet}\leq 400$ GeV. We find that the network learns how the fraction of gluon jets changes with the multiplicity in every $p_{T}^{\rm jet}$ bin, including the shift of the multiplicity at which quark and gluon jets are equally likely, which implies that it uses the jet scale (reconstructed from its constituents) jointly with its substructure. At fixed multiplicity, the gluon probabilities it assigns agree on average with the observed gluon fractions, but are widely spread about the mean. This allows us to interpret its output as the sum of the multiplicity log-likelihood ratio and a residual log-likelihood ratio at fixed multiplicity, which allows the network to correctly identify 20-34% of the jets whose multiplicity favors the opposite class. By rescaling the radial spread of the constituents at fixed multiplicity, we find that the network considers broader jets more gluon-like, and that this response is largest at low and intermediate multiplicity, where the multiplicity alone does not decide the class of a jet.

</details>


### [48] [Fermiophobic Z' Bosons in 3-4-1 Models: Classification and Collider Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33805)
*Eduard Suarez,Yithsbey Giraldo,Eduardo Rojas*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes fermion spectra and collider phenomenology in SU(3)_C × SU(4)_L × U(1)_X models, identifying minimal anomaly-free fermion sets for two charge operator realizations (b=c=1 and b=1,c=−2); it shows that only the b=c=1 case admits a fermiophobic limit where Z′ couplings vanish simultaneously (at ψ=nπ), evading standard dilepton constraints, while the b=1,c=−2 case does not; collider bounds thus depend critically on fermion embeddings and Z–Z′ mixing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically explore how fermion content and gauge-boson mixing in 3-4-1 models affect Z′ phenomenology and collider constraints—particularly whether light Z′ bosons can evade current dilepton searches via fermiophobic behavior.

Method: Classification of anomaly-free fermion representations under SU(3)_C ⊗ SU(4)_L ⊗ U(1)_X; derivation of chiral Z′ couplings to SM fermions; comparison of low-energy neutral-current interactions across distinct spectra; reinterpretation of ATLAS 13 TeV dilepton resonance search (139 fb⁻¹) under assumptions of negligible Z–Z′ mixing and decoupled exotic fermions.

Result: Five minimal, three-family, anomaly-free fermion spectra identified for each b,c configuration; identical low-energy Z′ couplings can arise from algebraically distinct spectra; b=c=1 model exhibits a universal fermiophobic limit (Z′ couplings ∝ sinψ → 0 at ψ=nπ), nullifying Drell-Yan constraints; b=1,c=−2 lacks such a limit; hence, light Z′ bosons remain viable only in specific embeddings.

Conclusion: Collider constraints on Z′ bosons in extended electroweak models are highly sensitive to fermion representation choices and neutral-boson mixing; the b=c=1 realization allows for light, fermiophobic Z′ states undetectable in standard dilepton channels, opening new avenues for BSM searches.

Abstract: We investigate the fermion spectra and collider phenomenology of models based on the \(SU(3)_C \otimes SU(4)_L \otimes U(1)_X\) gauge symmetry, focusing on two realizations of the electric charge operator characterized by \(b=c=1\) and \(b=1,\ c=-2\). For each configuration, we classify anomaly-free fermion sets and identify five minimal spectra with at least three SM-like quark and lepton families for each configuration, distinguishing algebraically irreducible structures from reducible spectra that remain minimal under the three-family requirement. We derive the chiral couplings of the additional neutral gauge boson \(Z'\) to Standard Model fermions and show that distinct anomaly-free fermion spectra can lead to identical low-energy neutral-current interactions. We then investigate the corresponding collider constraints using the ATLAS dilepton resonance search at \(\sqrt{s}=13\) TeV with an integrated luminosity of \(139\,\mathrm{fb}^{-1}\), assuming negligible \(Z-Z'\) mixing and kinematically inaccessible exotic fermions. A particularly interesting result emerges for the \(b=c=1\) configuration, where all \(Z'\) couplings to Standard Model fermions are proportional to \(\sin\psi\), with \(\psi\) denoting the additional neutral gauge-boson mixing angle. Consequently, these couplings vanish simultaneously at \(\psi=n\pi\), giving rise to a fermiophobic limit in which conventional Drell-Yan dilepton constraints disappear. In contrast, the \(b=1,\ c=-2\) configuration does not exhibit a universal fermiophobic limit. Our results demonstrate that collider constraints on additional neutral gauge bosons depend sensitively on the fermion embeddings and neutral gauge-boson mixing structure, and suggest the possibility of comparatively light \(Z'\) bosons in suitable realizations of extended electroweak gauge theories.

</details>


### [49] [Radiative Higgs decay to $ψ\left(4230\right)$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34117)
*Ahmed Ali,Muhammad Jamil Aslam*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates the SM and BSM radiative Higgs decay H → ψ(4230) + γ, treating ψ(4230) as a tetraquark candidate (I^G(J^{PC}) = 0⁻(1^{--})). It introduces a direct amplitude parametrization based on the ψ(4230) leptonic coupling and finds BR_SM ≈ 4.9 × 10⁻⁷ (dominated by experimental uncertainty in Γ_{ψ→ℓℓ}), rising to ~10⁻⁶ for anomalous charm Yukawa coupling y_c = −3.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe exotic hadron structure and possible BSM physics via rare radiative Higgs decays into a candidate tetraquark vector meson ψ(4230), leveraging its well-measured quantum numbers and decay properties.

Method: Computes H → ψ(4230)γ decay amplitude using a model-independent parametrization of the 'direct amplitude' anchored to the experimentally measured leptonic decay width Γ_{ψ→e⁺e⁻}; incorporates SM and BSM (charm-Yukawa-modified) Higgs couplings; evaluates branching ratio with error propagation dominated by Γ_{ψ(4230)} uncertainty.

Result: Predicted SM branching ratio: ℬ(H → ψ(4230)γ) = 4.88^{+4.71}_{−4.52} × 10⁻⁷; enhanced to ~10⁻⁶ for y_c = −3, indicating sensitivity to anomalous charm-Higgs coupling.

Conclusion: H → ψ(4230)γ is a theoretically clean and experimentally accessible channel to test both the tetraquark nature of ψ(4230) and potential BSM modifications to the charm Yukawa coupling, especially at high-luminosity LHC or future Higgs factories.

Abstract: We calculate the decay rate for the radiative Higgs decay $H \to \psi(4230) + \gamma$ within and beyond the SM. The vector state $\psi(4230)$ is a candidate exotic tetraquark state and has the quantum numbers $I^G(J^{PC})=0^-(1^{--})$, having a mass $m_{\psi(4230)}=4221.7 \pm 2.5$ MeV and a decay width $\Gamma_{\psi(4230)}=51 \pm 7$ MeV. We propose a practical parametrization of the so-called {\it direct amplitude}, using the leptonic coupling of the $\psi(4230)$ to estimate the short-distance overlap relevant to the Higgs decay amplitude. We find ${\cal B}_{\rm SM}( H \to \psi(4230) \gamma)=4.88^{+4.71}_{-4.52}\times 10^{-7}$, where the uncertainties are dominated by the experimental uncertainty in the leptonic decay width of the $\psi(4230)$. The branching ratio increases to about $10^{-6}$ for $y_c=-3 $, where $y_c$ is the Higgs-Yukawa coupling of the charm quark.

</details>


### [50] [Anomalous Soft Photons: A Brief Overview](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34216)
*Klaus Reygers*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper summarizes experimental efforts addressing the soft-photon puzzle—the persistent discrepancy between soft-photon data in hadronic reactions and predictions from Low's soft-photon theorem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify and consolidate the experimental status of the soft-photon puzzle, which challenges the validity or applicability of Low's soft-photon theorem in hadronic processes.

Method: Review and synthesis of representative experimental measurements, theoretical baseline (Low's theorem), and identification of key experimental challenges.

Result: A compact experimental perspective highlighting the unresolved discrepancy, major measurement efforts, and systematic limitations hindering definitive resolution.

Conclusion: The soft-photon puzzle remains open; current data continue to deviate from Low’s theorem predictions, underscoring the need for improved precision, control of systematics, and possibly extensions to the underlying theory.

Abstract: These conference proceedings provide a compact experimental perspective on the soft-photon puzzle. The puzzle refers to the long-standing discrepancy between soft-photon data in hadronic reactions and predictions based on Low's soft-photon theorem. We summarize the theoretical baseline, representative measurements, and the main experimental challenges. For a detailed account of the experimental history and theory, we refer to the recent review article Phys. Rept., 1097:1, 2024.

</details>


### [51] [High-order temperature fluctuations in hot and dense QCD matter from multi-charge holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34414)
*Yidian Chen,Peiyu Yan,Zhibin Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies temperature cumulants (2nd–6th order) across the QCD phase diagram using a lattice-calibrated multi-charge Einstein–Maxwell–dilaton model under heavy-ion charge constraints; it identifies critical signatures—e.g., variance suppression and sign reversals in higher cumulants—near the critical end point (~547,112 MeV), confirms mean-field critical scaling (δ=3) via cumulant ratios, and shows nonmonotonic freeze-out behavior in collision energy, offering complementary probes to conserved-charge fluctuations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the QCD critical end point and critical scaling behavior using temperature cumulants—which reflect thermal response—complementing conventional conserved-charge fluctuation observables.

Method: Lattice-calibrated multi-charge Einstein–Maxwell–dilaton (EMD) model constrained by heavy-ion net-charge data; computation of temperature cumulants up to sixth order; analysis of cumulant ratios, critical-isotherm scaling, and dynamical freeze-out trajectories across the phase diagram.

Result: Near the critical end point (μ_B,T)≈(547,112) MeV: (i) temperature variance is locally suppressed due to enhanced heat capacity; (ii) 3rd–6th-order cumulants exhibit sign reversals on the high-T side, with onset shifting to lower μ_B as order increases; (iii) cumulant ratios validate mean-field criticality (δ=3); (iv) freeze-out variance shows a local minimum near √s_NN≈6 GeV, path-dependent in position and depth.

Conclusion: Temperature cumulants are sensitive, complementary probes of thermal criticality in the QCD phase diagram, encoding distinct information from conserved-charge fluctuations and enabling robust tests of critical scaling and freeze-out dynamics.

Abstract: We investigate temperature cumulants from second to sixth order across the QCD phase diagram using a lattice-calibrated multi-charge Einstein--Maxwells--dilaton model under heavy-ion charge constraints. Near the model's critical end point at $(\mu_B,T)\simeq(547,112)$~MeV, the enhanced heat capacity produces a local suppression of the temperature variance. The higher-order cumulants develop sign reversals on the high-temperature side of the transition, with the onset moving to lower baryon chemical potentials as the order increases. Ratios of different cumulant orders provide a consistency check of the expected mean-field critical behavior. At fixed chemical potentials, their limits on both sides of the critical point agree with the predictions for the critical-isotherm exponent $\delta=3$. The freeze-out analysis reveals nonmonotonic collision-energy dependence that need not be accompanied by sign reversals. On a representative trajectory, the variance reaches a local minimum near $\sqrt{s_{NN}}\simeq6$~GeV, with its position and depth depending on the freeze-out path. Temperature cumulants probe the thermal response and its critical scaling, providing information complementary to conserved-charge fluctuations for exploring the QCD phase diagram.

</details>


### [52] [Axions from Betelgeuse: Probing Axion Couplings with $^{23}\rm{Na}$ and $^{55}\rm{Mn}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34540)
*Nicholas A. M. Revel,Baldeep Grewal,Anupam Ray,Aaron C. Vincent*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper searches for axion-like particles (ALPs) produced via nuclear de-excitation in Betelgeuse, using gamma-ray observations from INTEGRAL-ISGRI and NuSTAR; no signal is found, and stringent 95% CL limits are placed on the ALP-photon–nucleon coupling product |g_ag g_ap| for both magnetic conversion and decay channels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe ALPs — well-motivated dark matter and BSM physics candidates — using stellar interiors as natural high-luminosity sources, leveraging Betelgeuse’s hot, dense core and known nuclear transitions in ^23Na and ^55Mn.

Method: Model ALP production from thermally excited ^23Na and ^55Mn nuclear states in Betelgeuse; compute ALP fluxes, propagation, and photon signatures via galactic magnetic-field conversion or two-photon decay; analyze INTEGRAL-ISGRI (for ~1.37 MeV ^23Na line and ~0.834 MeV ^55Mn line) and NuSTAR (for higher-resolution ^55Mn line) data to set exclusion limits on |g_ag g_ap|.

Result: No significant excess observed; derived 95% CL upper limits: for magnetic conversion, |g_ag g_ap| < 4.1×10⁻²¹ GeV⁻¹ (^23Na, m_a < 10⁻¹⁰ eV); for decay, down to 3.0×10⁻²² GeV⁻¹ (^23Na, m_a ≈ 400 keV) and 2.9×10⁻¹⁹ GeV⁻¹ (^55Mn, m_a ≈ 83 keV), with mass-dependent scaling (m_a⁻³/² at low masses, m_a⁻² below ~1 keV for ^23Na). ^23Na gives strongest limits but is evolutionarily sensitive; ^55Mn limits are more robust.

Conclusion: Stellar nuclear transitions provide a powerful probe of ALP couplings; this work sets world-leading constraints on |g_ag g_ap| for keV–sub-eV ALPs, highlighting the complementarity between high-energy resolution (NuSTAR) and high-sensitivity all-sky monitoring (INTEGRAL) and the importance of nuclear astrophysics modeling uncertainties.

Abstract: We investigate axion-like particle (ALP) production in the interior of Betelgeuse via nuclear de-excitation of thermally populated excited states of $^{23}$Na and $^{55}$Mn. As these ALPs propagate to Earth, they can produce photons either through their conversion in the Galactic magnetic field or through their decay. Using INTEGRAL-ISGRI and NuSTAR observations, we search for these photon signals, find no significant excess, and derive 95% CL exclusion limits on the ALP couplings. For magnetic-field conversion of ALPs, the $^{23}\mathrm{Na}$ ($^{55}\mathrm{Mn}$) line observed with INTEGRAL-ISGRI constrains $|g_{a\gamma}g_{ap}| < 4.1\times10^{-21}$ ($6.9\times10^{-19}$) $\mathrm{GeV}^{-1}$ for $m_a < 10^{-10} \mathrm{eV}$. For ALP decays, the $^{23}\mathrm{Na}$ ($^{55}\mathrm{Mn}$) line observed with INTEGRAL-ISGRI (NuSTAR) constrains $|g_{a\gamma}g_{ap}|$ down to $3.0\times10^{-22}$ ($2.9\times10^{-19}$) $\mathrm{GeV}^{-1}$ at $m_a \simeq 400$ ($83$) keV, weakening in both cases as $m_a^{-3/2}$ for lower ALP masses, and as $m_a^{-2}$ for $^{23}$Na below $m_a \sim 1$ keV, with sensitivity extending to $m_a \simeq 440.2$ ($124$) keV. Although our $^{23}\mathrm{Na}$-based limits are the strongest for both channels, their larger transition energy makes them more sensitive to the precise evolutionary stage of Betelgeuse, whereas the $^{55}\mathrm{Mn}$-based limits are considerably more robust against such uncertainties.

</details>


### [53] ["Inevitable'' nonstrange tetraquark states and $ρρ$ interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34741)
*Yanyue Pan,Yuying Hao,Shirui Zhao,Hongxia Huang,Jialun Ping*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates ρρ interactions with quantum numbers IJ=00, 02, 11, 20, 22 using the quark delocalization color screening model; finds strong attraction and bound states—especially for IJ=02 and 00—with binding energies >100 MeV, supports a molecular interpretation, and proposes IJ=02 ρρ as the most promising nonstrange tetraquark candidate.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify viable nonstrange tetraquark candidates by systematically studying ρρ interactions across multiple isospin–spin channels, motivated by analogies with the d* dibaryon and the expectation of 'inevitable' exotic hadrons in QCD.

Method: Quark delocalization color screening model (QDCSM), a microscopic quark-model framework incorporating both quark exchange (delocalization) and inter-cluster color screening to describe multiquark systems and meson–meson interactions.

Result: Strong attractive potentials found for all studied ρρ channels; largest binding energies (>100 MeV) for IJ=00 and IJ=02; smallest (~few MeV) for IJ=20; intercluster distances consistent with loosely bound molecular states; ρρ interaction qualitatively resembles ΔΔ interaction.

Conclusion: The ρρ state with IJ=02 is identified as the most promising 'inevitable' nonstrange tetraquark candidate, warranting dedicated experimental searches.

Abstract: Investigation of the interaction between two $\rho$ mesons with $IJ=00, 02, 11, 20$ and $22$ is conducted systematically in the framework of the quark delocalization color screening model. The results show that there is a strong attractive potential between two $\rho$ mesons, which makes it easier for these two mesons to form bound states. The largest binding energies occur for $IJ=02$ and $IJ=00$ with values over a hundred MeV, while the $IJ=20$ state is the least bound and has a binding energy of only a few MeV. Moreover, the results of intercluster distances favor a molecular interpretation of these states. The interaction analysis reveals that the $\rho\rho$ and $\Delta\Delta$ interactions share certain similarities. Drawing inspiration from the $d^*$ dibaryon prediction and from decay-property analyses, we suggest that the $\rho\rho$ with $IJ=02$ may be the most promising candidate of the ``inevitable'' nonstrange tetraquark state, which deserves experimental search.

</details>


### [54] [The Pion Parton Distribution Puzzle in the High x Region](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34798)
*Misak Sargsian*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a spectral function-based theoretical approach to describe the partonic structure of hadrons, applying it to calculate the pion's valence-parton distribution function (PDF); results show that the pion’s x-weighted valence PDF peak and height constrain its analytic behavior near x→1, consistent with dominance of the Feynman mechanism and minimal residual mass.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend a previously developed spectral function approach—originally used for nucleon PDFs and revealing pion-cloud effects—to the pion itself, aiming to understand its soft partonic structure and explain empirical features of its valence PDF, especially near x→1.

Method: Spectral function representation of valence quarks in a mean-field residual hadron; modeling the pion as a valence q\bar{q} cluster embedded in a residual field; using phenomenological light-front wave functions for both components; fitting model parameters (residual mass, q\bar{q} virtuality) to empirical pion xPDF data at initial scale Q₀.

Result: The peak position and height of the x-weighted valence PDF determine its analytic form as x→1; best-fit parameters indicate very small residual mass and high virtuality of the q\bar{q} cluster, implying dominance of the Feynman mechanism and negligible hard component; the model successfully reproduces the observed ~ (1−x) behavior at x→1.

Conclusion: The spectral function approach, calibrated to pion PDF data, reveals that the pion’s valence structure is dominantly soft and kinematically driven by the Feynman mechanism; the x→1 asymptotics are non-trivially fixed by low-x and peak-region observables, suggesting a tight constraint between global PDF shape and endpoint behavior.

Abstract: We developed a new theoretical approach in the description of partonic structure of hadrons based on spectral function representation of valence quarks. In this approach massless valence quarks are considered in the mean field of residual hadron. This approach initially was applied to the calculation of parton distribution function~(PDF) of nucleons that resulted in an observation of pion-cloud structure of nucleon. In this report we present our calculation of pion PDF in the valence region within the same approach. In this approach we describe the soft partonic structure of pion through the valence $q\bar q$ cluster, embedded in the residual field of the pion. The pion PDF is calculated using phenomenological Light-Front wave functions for the $q\bar q$ cluster and the residual field. Our result indicates that the peak position of the x weighted valence PDF~(xPDF) depends on parameters characterizing the virtuality of the $q\bar q$ cluster and the mass of the residual system. These parameters are evaluated by fitting the calculated PDF to the height and the peak position of empirical pion xPDF at starting $Q_0$. They indicate that unlike the nucleon case, very little residual mass is needed in the pion to describe existing pion PDFs in the valence region. They also require the $q\bar q$-cluster to be highly virtual with the interacting quark carrying almost all of the momentum of the $q\bar q$ cluster. This picture is consistent with the dominance of the Feynman mechanism leaving little room for the hard component in the PDF. The calculation practically describes the $\sim (1-x)$ behavior observed recently at $x\to 1$ limit. Our non-trivial observation is that the height and the peak position of xPDF define the analytic behavior of valence PDF at $x\to 1$ limit.

</details>


### [55] [Fermion Mass and Mixing Patterns from Fusion Algebras](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34720)
*Jacques Distler,Can Kilic,Sanjay Mathai*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes using noninvertible symmetries—specifically fusion algebras—to generate hierarchical fermion mass textures in the Standard Model; it identifies FR⁸,⁶₈ as the best-fitting rank-8 fusion algebra for Yukawa matrices, but finds no fusion algebra simultaneously fits both fermion masses and CKM/PMNS mixing angles, while guaranteeing RG stability of textures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the large hierarchy of fermion masses in the Standard Model via symmetry principles—specifically moving beyond conventional (invertible) global or gauge symmetries to noninvertible symmetries encoded in fusion algebras, which can naturally suppress higher-dimension operators and yield structured Yukawa textures.

Method: Uses fusion-algebra labeling to assign suppression weights to composite operators contributing to Yukawa couplings; performs a Monte Carlo scan over Wilson coefficients of effective operators without committing to a specific UV completion; evaluates goodness-of-fit for fermion mass matrices (up, down, charged lepton) and derives predictions for CKM and PMNS matrices.

Result: The rank-8 fusion algebra FR⁸,⁶₈ yields the best simultaneous fit to the three Yukawa matrix textures; however, no studied fusion algebra reproduces both observed fermion mass ratios and quark/neutrino mixing angles simultaneously; all resulting textures are guaranteed stable under renormalization group evolution due to fusion-algebra constraints.

Conclusion: Noninvertible symmetries via fusion algebras provide a viable, RG-stable mechanism for generating fermion mass hierarchies, but current constructions fall short of explaining mixing data—suggesting either extensions of the fusion-algebra framework or additional low-energy dynamics are needed.

Abstract: The large hierarchy of fermion masses in the Standard Model has long posed a challenge to be explained. One approach is to try to invoke a symmetry principle to dictate the structure ("texture") of the matrices of fermion Yukawa couplings to the Higgs field. Here we invoke noninvertible symmetries, specifically, fusion algebras, to provide such textures. In particular, the structure of the fusion-algebra can be used to assign labels to each field, which determines how suppressed any given composite operator is as a term in the Lagrangian. In UV completions, this can be interpreted as specifying a hierarchy of operators that in the IR effective theory become the Yukawa couplings to the Higgs field. Remaining agnostic of a specific UV model, we perform a Monte Carlo numerical scan over unknown Wilson coefficients, and we identify the rank-8 fusion algebra $\rm{FR}^{8,6}_8$ as the one providing the best fit for the textures of the up-quark, down-quark and charged-lepton Yukawa matrices. We also study the predictions for the CKM and PMNS mixing matrices, with the conclusion that none of the fusion algebras we study provide an accurate fit for the masses and mixing angles simultaneously. The fusion algebra structure ensures that the textures that we find are stable under RG running and persist down to low energies.

</details>


### [56] [Analysis of $S$-wave $ωπ$ dynamics behavior in multi-body $B_{\ell4}$ and $B_{\ell6}$ decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34840)
*Yan-Ting Yang,Hai-Bing Fu,Xue Zheng,Jian-Qi Chen,Sheng-Bo Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the multi-body semileptonic decays B→b₁(1235)ℓν (Bℓ4) and B→b₁(1235)(→ωπ→3π)ℓν (Bℓ6) to address theoretical inconsistencies arising from treating the broad b₁(1235) resonance as a stable final state. Using QCD light-cone sum rules, it computes b₁→B transition form factors with a transverse twist-2 LCDA modeled via light-cone harmonic oscillator, obtains TFFs and ratios at q²=0, incorporates the ωπ resonant line shape, and predicts branching ratios—finding BR(Bℓ4) ≈ 1/3 of the naive stable-resonance estimate—and extends to BR(Bℓ6).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The sizable decay width of the b₁(1235) resonance causes systematic deviations when treated as a stable final state in theoretical predictions; this work aims to resolve that issue and probe the internal structure of b₁(1235) via multi-body semileptonic B decays.

Method: QCD light-cone sum rules are employed to compute b₁(1235)→B transition form factors (TFFs); a right-handed chiral current defines the correlation function; the transverse twist-2 light-cone distribution amplitude (LCDA) of b₁(1235) is constructed using the light-cone harmonic oscillator model; the b₁→ωπ resonant line shape is incorporated to obtain full Bℓ4 observables; ω→π⁺π⁻π⁰ decay is further included for Bℓ6 prediction.

Result: TFFs V₀,₁,₂(0) and A(0) are obtained at large recoil; TFF ratios r_V = 1.530₋₀.₀₁₆⁺⁰.₀₁₃ and r₂ = 0.553₋₀.₀₁₅⁺⁰.₀₁₂; BR(Bℓ4) is found to be ~1/3 of the value assuming b₁(1235) as a stable final state; BR(Bℓ6) is predicted including sequential b₁→ωπ→3π decay.

Conclusion: Treating b₁(1235) as a resonant intermediate state—not a stable final state—is essential for accurate theoretical predictions in Bℓ4 and Bℓ6 decays; the computed TFFs, line-shape–corrected branching ratios, and Bℓ6 prediction provide benchmarks for experiment and future theory.

Abstract: The $b_1(1235)$ state has a sizable decay width, which have longstanding puzzle in treating as a final state in theoretical calculations may lead to systematic deviations in the predicted observables. To address this issue and further explore the internal structure of this resonance, we study the multi-body semileptonic decays $B_{\ell4}$ and $B_{\ell6}$. Within the approximation where the $b_1(1235)$ resonance dominates, we study the $B_{\ell4}$ decay $B\to b_1(1235)(\to\omega\pi)\ell^+\nu_\ell$ in the QCD light-cone sum rules framework. The corresponding transition form factors (TFFs) are calculated by constructing a correlation function with a right-handed chiral current. As the main nonperturbative input for the TFFs calculation, the transverse twist-2 light-cone distribution amplitude (LCDA) of the $b_1(1235)$ resonance is constructed using the light-cone harmonic oscillator model, and the resulting LCDA exhibits a single-peak behavior. At the large-recoil region, we obtain the TFFs $V_{0, 1, 2}(0)$ and $A(0)$, together with the corresponding TFFs ratios $r_V = 1.530_{-0.016}^{+0.013}$ and $r_2 = 0.553_{-0.015}^{+0.012}$. We then further study the complete $B_{\ell4}$ decay by incorporating the $b_1(1235)\to\omega\pi$ resonant line shape and obtain the corresponding observables. The results show that the branching ratio of the $B_{\ell4}$ decay is about $1/3$ of that obtained when $b_1(1235)$ is treated as a final state. We also account for the subsequent decay $\omega \to \pi^+\pi^-\pi^0$ and further predicted the branching ratio of the $B_{\ell6}$ decay $B\to b_1(1235)(\to\omega(\to \pi^+\pi^-\pi^0)\pi)\ell^+\nu_\ell$. These results may provide useful references for future experimental measurements and further theoretical studies.

</details>


### [57] [Light top-flavoured dark matter in dark SMEFT: one-loop matching and phenomenology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34880)
*Meng-Chao Gao,Xin-Qiang Li,Ya-Dong Yang,Xing-Bo Yuan,Xin Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a unified effective field theory framework (DSMEFT → DLEFT → non-relativistic EFT) to analyze light top-flavoured dark matter (< EW scale) coupling preferentially to third-generation quarks, and confronts it with Belle II's B→Kνν excess, rare meson decays, direct/indirect detection, and thermal relic constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the Belle II excess in B⁺→K⁺νν̄ as DM production while maintaining consistency across multiple complementary probes—rare B/K decays, direct detection (CRESST-III, DarkSide-50), indirect detection (CMB, Fermi-LAT), and thermal relic abundance—using a consistent EFT hierarchy.

Method: One-loop matching of DSMEFT onto DLEFT at the EW scale for scalar/fermionic/vector DM and ALPs coupled exclusively to third-generation quarks; QCD evolution to low energies; subsequent matching onto non-relativistic DM-nucleus scattering EFT; global phenomenological analysis including branching ratio constraints, direct detection limits, CMB/γ-ray bounds, and thermal freeze-out calculation.

Result: Only tree-level DSMEFT operators can explain the B→Kνν̄ excess with O(1) couplings at Λ = 1 TeV; B⁰→K*⁰+inv is the dominant constraint over much of the favored parameter space; K⁺→π⁺+inv dominates at low DM mass (≲1 GeV); Bs→inv, B⁺→π⁺+inv, and Υ(1S)→inv gain relevance at high mass; direct detection excludes parts of the Belle II-favored region for m_DM ≳ 1–2 GeV for scalar-current operators.

Conclusion: A coherent EFT framework reveals strong complementarity among flavor, direct, and indirect probes; the Belle II excess severely constrains light top-flavoured DM models, with viable parameter space tightly bounded by combined constraints—particularly from B-meson invisible decays and sub-GeV direct detection.

Abstract: We study light top-flavoured dark matter (DM) below the electroweak (EW) scale within the dark Standard Model effective field theory (DSMEFT). We perform the one-loop matching of the DSMEFT onto the dark low-energy effective field theory (DLEFT) at the EW scale for all relevant operators that couple scalar, fermionic or vector DM, as well as axion-like particles, exclusively to third-generation quarks. After QCD evolution to the relevant low-energy scales, we match the DLEFT onto the non-relativistic EFT for DM-nucleus scattering. This EFT chain provides a unified framework for phenomenological studies of rare $B$- and $K$-meson decays with missing energy, direct and indirect detection, and the thermal relic abundance. Interpreting the Belle II excess in $B^+\to K^+\nu\bar\nu$ as a decay into DM, we find that only the DSMEFT operators contributing at tree level can accommodate it with $O(1)$ couplings for a new-physics scale of $1\,\mathrm{TeV}$. The decay $B^0\to K^{*0}+\mathrm{inv}$ provides the strongest constraint over large portions of the Belle II-favoured parameter space, while $K^+\to\pi^++\mathrm{inv}$ can dominate at low and $B_s\to\mathrm{inv}$, $B^+\to\pi^++\mathrm{inv}$ and $\Upsilon(1S)\to\mathrm{inv}$ become important at high DM masses, respectively. Direct-detection searches by CRESST-III and DarkSide-50 are competitive for operators involving scalar quark currents and, depending on the operator, exclude the Belle~II-favoured band for $m_{\mathrm{DM}}\gtrsim1$-$2\,\mathrm{GeV}$. We also perform detailed analyses of the thermal relic abundance and the indirect-detection constraints from the CMB and Fermi-LAT.

</details>


### [58] [Explaining the universal Hagedorn temperature in string models of hadrons with fixed endpoints or baryon junctions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34882)
*Yuki Fujimoto*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives mass formulas and asymptotic density-of-states expressions for string models of hadrons with one fixed heavy endpoint or a baryon junction; it shows that all such configurations share the same Hagedorn temperature as ordinary open strings—providing a microscopic basis for its phenomenological universality—and that the baryon-junction and quark-diquark models yield identical asymptotic density-of-states scaling, differing only in normalization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the recently observed phenomenological universality of the Hagedorn temperature across different hadronic systems (e.g., heavy-light mesons, baryons) by constructing and comparing underlying string-theoretic models.

Method: Analytical derivation of mass spectra and asymptotic densities of states for relativistic string models: (i) open strings with one fixed heavy endpoint (modeling heavy-light mesons), (ii) Y-shaped strings with a baryon junction (modeling baryons), and (iii) quark-diquark strings; comparison of Regge slopes, Hagedorn temperatures, and asymptotic state densities using saddle-point and thermodynamic methods.

Result: All considered string configurations—fixed-endpoint, baryon-junction, and quark-diquark—exhibit identical Hagedorn temperatures and asymptotic density-of-states scaling ∼ exp(β_H M), differing only in prefactors; however, the baryon-junction model predicts steeper (less realistic) Regge slopes than observed.

Conclusion: The Hagedorn temperature’s universality arises from shared string tension and transverse degrees of freedom, not dynamics of endpoints or topology; thus, thermodynamic limits of hadronic spectra are robust across distinct string realizations, though spectroscopic details (e.g., Regge slopes) discriminate between models.

Abstract: We derive the mass formulas and more accurate asymptotic expressions for the densities of states for string models of hadrons with one fixed heavy endpoint or a baryon junction. We show that strings with one fixed endpoint have the same Hagedorn temperature as ordinary open strings at the same tension and number of polarizations, despite the different Regge slopes of heavy-light and light mesons. This provides a microscopic explanation for the universality of the Hagedorn temperature recently found phenomenologically.
