We also find that the baryon-junction and quark-diquark pictures share this Hagedorn temperature and yield the same asymptotic mass dependence of the density of states, differing only by an overall normalization. The observed baryon mass spectrum provides little discrimination between the two pictures, although the baryon-junction model has a larger discrepancy with the observed Regge slopes.

</details>


### [59] [Probing the Collins effect at photon-photon colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35285)
*Simone Anedda,Carlo Flore,Francesco Murgia,Cristian Pisano*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes studying the Collins effect via azimuthal distributions of back-to-back unpolarized hadrons (e.g., pions) in γγ → h₁h₂X collisions, using quasi-real photons from lepton or heavy-ion ultra-peripheral collisions; predicts sizable (10–15%) asymmetries at FCC and LHC-UPC, enabling tests of quark fragmentation models and discrimination among Collins function parameterizations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the Collins effect—a spin-dependent hadronization mechanism—in an unpolarized γγ collision environment, offering a clean test of transverse-momentum-dependent fragmentation functions and model discrimination where traditional polarized experiments are challenging.

Method: Theoretical calculation of azimuthal asymmetries in the production of almost back-to-back unpolarized hadrons (mainly π±) in γγ → h₁h₂X, using collinear factorization with transverse-momentum-dependent (TMD) Collins fragmentation functions; includes numerical estimates for FCC-ee and LHC ultra-peripheral collision kinematics.

Result: Predicted Collins asymmetries reach 10–15% for suitable kinematic cuts (e.g., moderate Q², high hadron transverse momentum relative to thrust axis), with sufficient statistical precision expected at FCC and LHC-UPC experiments.

Conclusion: Measuring these asymmetries in γγ collisions provides a novel, unpolarized avenue to constrain the Collins function and distinguish between competing TMD fragmentation models, complementing existing SIDIS and e⁺e⁻ data.

Abstract: We propose to study the Collins effect in the production of almost back-to-back unpolarized hadrons (mainly charged pions) by looking at their azimuthal distributions around the jet thrust-axis in the process $\gamma\gamma \to h_1h_2X$. The quasi-real photons in the initial state can be emitted either by two scattering leptons or two charged heavy nuclei in ultra-peripheral collisions. We provide numerical estimates for kinematic configurations in principle accessible at the Future Circular Collider and at the planned experiments on ultra-peripheral collisions at the LHC. We find that the corresponding asymmetries can be sizable, even up to around 10-15%. The proposed measurements would thus shed light on the fragmentation mechanism of quarks into hadrons and, in principle, would allow one to distinguish among different existing models for the Collins functions.

</details>


### [60] [The neutrino fog for inelastic dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35789)
*C. S. Amarasinghe*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how inelastic dark matter scattering alters the neutrino fog (neutrino floor) in xenon-based direct detection experiments, revealing parameter-dependent fog suppression or reappearance, discontinuous fog onset due to background uncertainties, and counterintuitive elevation of the neutrino floor with improved neutrino rate precision.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how inelastic dark matter scattering—characterized by mass splitting and altered kinematics—affects the neutrino fog (the irreducible background from coherent neutrino-nucleus scattering) in xenon detectors, especially for future high-exposure experiments.

Method: Analytical and numerical characterization of recoil spectrum modifications induced by inelastic scattering kinematics; systematic study of fog onset, disappearance, and reappearance across the inelastic parameter space (DM mass, mass splitting); assessment of how uncertain backgrounds affect fog morphology and discovery limits; investigation of the impact of neutrino flux uncertainty reduction on fog opacity and derived floors.

Result: Inelastic scattering can eliminate the neutrino fog entirely in certain parameter regions; elsewhere, fog disappears at intermediate exposures but reappears at larger exposures; background normalization uncertainties induce discontinuous jumps in fog onset as a function of DM mass and splitting, while discovery limits remain smooth; reducing neutrino rate uncertainties can paradoxically raise the neutrino floor due to increased sensitivity to fog opacity.

Conclusion: The neutrino fog for inelastic dark matter is highly nontrivial and parameter-sensitive; standard assumptions about monotonic fog behavior and floor lowering with improved neutrino knowledge do not hold, necessitating tailored fog analyses for inelastic models in next-generation xenon experiments.

Abstract: We characterize the behavior of the neutrino fog for inelastic dark matter scattering on xenon. The inelastic scattering kinematics reshape the recoil spectrum of the signal and produce a qualitatively different neutrino fog landscape. In some regions of the inelastic parameter space the fog is completely absent for future xenon experiments at any scale, while in other regions the fog is dispelled only to reappear at larger exposures. We find that multiple background sources with uncertain normalizations can make the onset of the fog jump discontinuously with the dark matter mass and mass splitting, while the discovery limit varies smoothly. We also describe a mechanism by which reduced uncertainties on neutrino rates may have the unintuitive effect of raising neutrino floors derived from the fog opacity.

</details>


### [61] [Exact flavor conservation at the quark level in causal clusters: strange particles, protons and light nuclei in small collision systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34947)
*Paolo Castorina,Helmut Satz*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends a strangeness conservation model to light flavors (u, d) in pp and p--Pb collisions, using exact quark flavor conservation within causally connected clusters before hadronization; with strangeness sector fixed, a single parameter — the light-to-strange quark abundance ratio r = 3.76 ± 0.38 — successfully describes yields of protons, deuterons, helium-4, and hypertritons, consistent with string fragmentation expectations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain observed suppression patterns of light-flavor baryons and light nuclei in small collision systems (pp, p--Pb) beyond strangeness, by generalizing the exact flavor conservation framework from strange to up and down quarks within causally connected pre-hadronization clusters.

Method: Imposes exact conservation of u, d, and s quark numbers (and corresponding antiquarks) within finite-sized, causally connected clusters; incorporates nuclear size effects via cluster volume scaling; fits proton, deuteron, He, and hypertriton yields across multiplicity classes using one free parameter r = (u+d)/(2s) abundance ratio, with s-sector constrained from prior φ-meson analysis.

Result: Achieves excellent simultaneous description of multiplicity-dependent yields for p, d, ^4He, and ^3_ΛH in pp and p--Pb collisions at LHC energies; extracted r = 3.76 ± 0.38 implies s-quark abundance ≈ 0.27 per light flavor, matching expectations from string fragmentation models.

Conclusion: Light-flavor hadron and light-nuclei production in small systems is governed by exact quark flavor conservation in finite clusters, unifying strangeness and light-quark sectors under a common statistical hadronization framework without invoking QGP formation.

Abstract: The suppression of strange hadrons in pp and p--Pb collisions at the LHC, including the $\phi$ meson, has recently been described by the exact conservation of strangeness imposed on the quarks of a causally connected cluster before hadronization. Here we extend this approach to the light flavors. The numbers of up and down quarks and antiquarks are conserved in the same cluster, and the suppression of a baryon or a nucleus depends only on how many light quarks of each flavor it contains. With the strangeness sector fixed by the previous analysis, a single constant, the ratio $r$ of light to strange quark abundances, determines the multiplicity dependence of protons, deuterons, He and hypertriton in pp and p--Pb collisions. Including the finite size of the nuclei, one obtains a very good fit of the data for $r=3.76\pm0.38$, i.e.\ strange quarks about 0.27 times as abundant as light quarks per flavor, close to the value known from string fragmentation

</details>


### [62] [Dilepton azimuthal anisotropy from coherent $J/ψ$ photoproduction in hadronic Pb-Pb collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35149)
*Xueli Hua,Xin Wu,Shuai Yang,Wangmei Zha*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes using coherent J/ψ photoproduction as a geometry reference to mitigate nonflow and autocorrelation biases in event-plane estimation in heavy-ion collisions, calculating the dilepton anisotropy coefficient A₂ relative to the reaction plane in Pb–Pb collisions at 5.36 TeV using an equivalent-photon + Glauber + vector-meson-dominance framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Hadron-based event-plane estimators suffer from nonflow and autocorrelation biases—especially at low multiplicity—limiting precision in flow measurements; coherent J/ψ photoproduction provides a clean, geometry-defined reaction plane via quasireal-photon polarization correlation.

Method: The authors compute the second-order dilepton coefficient A₂ relative to the reaction plane using an equivalent-photon approximation coupled with generalized vector-meson dominance and a Glauber framework, incorporating nuclear diffraction and two-source interference; they compare two treatments of coherent scattering in the nuclear overlap region (with vs. without suppression).

Result: A₂ exhibits strong transverse-momentum dependence due to nuclear diffraction and interference, weak rapidity dependence; suppressing coherent contributions from the nuclear overlap region reduces total yield but enhances integrated A₂ (i.e., stronger polarization–reaction-plane correlation), especially in central/semicentral collisions; joint A₂ and coherent-yield measurements constrain coherence loss from hadronic interactions.

Conclusion: Coherent J/ψ photoproduction offers a robust, bias-resistant geometry reference for event-plane determination; azimuthal correlations of decay leptons can complement collectivity studies—even in small systems—provided sufficient tagged yields are achieved.

Abstract: Hadron-based event-plane estimators can be affected by nonflow and autocorrelation biases, especially at low multiplicity. Coherent $J/\psi$ photoproduction offers a complementary geometry reference through the correlation between quasireal-photon polarization and the reaction plane. We calculate the second-order dilepton coefficient $A_2$ relative to the reaction plane in hadronic Pb--Pb collisions at $\sqrt{s_{\mathrm{NN}}}=5.36$ TeV within an equivalent-photon and generalized vector-meson-dominance Glauber framework. Nuclear diffraction and two-source interference produce a pronounced transverse-momentum dependence, while the rapidity dependence is weak. We also compare two treatments of coherent scattering in the overlap region of the two colliding nuclei. Suppressing the contribution from this region reduces the yield and strengthens the integrated polarization--reaction-plane correlation, with the largest differences in central and semicentral collisions and smaller differences toward peripheral collisions. Joint measurements of the coherent yield and $A_2$ can constrain how hadronic interactions disrupt nuclear coherence and clarify the photoproduction mechanism. With sufficient tagged yields, azimuthal correlations involving decay leptons from photoproduced $J/\psi$ mesons could complement studies of collectivity, with potential future applications to small systems.

</details>


### [63] [Revisiting supernova constraints on dark gauge bosons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35379)
*Zun Wang,Yongchao Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper revisits supernova constraints on three light gauge bosons—dark photon, U(1)_{B-L}, and U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ}—using improved nucleon bremsstrahlung calculations (one-pion exchange with full momentum/mass dependence) and realistic geometrical treatment of decay/absorption in the supernova core; it derives updated limits from SN1987A cooling and low-energy supernovae (LESNe), yielding stronger mass and coupling bounds than prior work.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve upon previous supernova constraints by incorporating more accurate nuclear physics (full momentum/mass dependence in nucleon bremsstrahlung) and realistic geometrical modeling of gauge boson propagation and interaction in dense supernova environments.

Method: Re-evaluation of nucleon bremsstrahlung production via one-pion exchange approximation with full kinematic dependence; inclusion of geometrical effects on decay and absorption inside the supernova core; application of SN1987A neutrino-cooling constraint and LESNe energy-deposition limits using the SFHo-18.8 18.8 M⊙ progenitor profile.

Result: SN1987A cooling excludes dark photon masses up to ~230 MeV, U(1)_{B-L} up to ~280 MeV, and U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ} up to ~300 MeV; LESNe extend exclusions to ~450 MeV, ~440 MeV, and ~600 MeV respectively; corresponding coupling limits: ε < 5.8×10⁻¹², g_BL < 2.9×10⁻¹⁶, g_μτ < 6.4×10⁻¹².

Conclusion: Updated supernova analyses significantly strengthen constraints on light gauge bosons, especially for U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ}, where LESNe provide the most stringent mass reach; the refined treatment of nuclear production and transport is essential for robust exclusion limits.

Abstract: We revisit the supernova constraints on three well-motivated light gauge bosons: the dark photon, the $U(1)_{B-L}$ gauge boson, and the $U(1)_{L_\mu-L_\tau}$ gauge boson. For the dark photon and the $U(1)_{B-L}$ gauge boson, we re-evaluate the nucleon bremsstrahlung process within the one-pion exchange approximation, retaining the full momentum and mass dependence. The geometrical dependence of the decay and absorption of these gauge bosons inside the supernova core is also taken into account. Updated bounds on these models are derived based on the cooling of SN1987A and energy deposition constraints from low-energy supernovae (LESNe). Taking the SFHo-18.8 $18.8\,M_\odot$ profile, the SN1987A cooling constraints reach masses of about $230$ MeV, $280$ MeV, and $300$ MeV for the dark photon, the $U(1)_{B-L}$ boson, and the $U(1)_{L_\mu-L_\tau}$ boson, while the corresponding LESN limits extend to about $450$ MeV, $440$ MeV, and $600$ MeV, respectively. The kinetic mixing parameter $\epsilon$ for dark photon, the gauge coupling $g_{BL}$ in the $U(1)_{B-L}$ model, and the gauge coupling $g_{\mu\tau}$ for the $U(1)_{L_\mu-L_\tau}$ model are constrained down to $5.8\times10^{-12}$, $2.9\times10^{-16}$, and $6.4\times10^{-12}$, respectively.

</details>


### [64] [Probing the singlet-triplet scotogenic model with lepton flavor violation, gravitational waves and dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35444)
*Shi-Yu Li,Jun-Nan Lu,Yang Zhang,Gui-Jun Ding*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the singlet-triplet scotogenic model, linking neutrino masses, lepton-flavor violation (LFV), scalar dark matter, and gravitational-wave (GW) signatures from a first-order electroweak phase transition. It derives general one-loop Wilson coefficients for LFV processes, computes constraints from μ→eγ, μ→eee, and μ–e conversion, identifies λ₅ as pivotal for connecting neutrino mass, LFV, and DM detection, studies finite-temperature effective potential and GW spectra, and finds a benchmark point compatible with current experiments and detectable by future GW observatories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify neutrino mass generation, lepton flavor violation, scalar dark matter, and cosmological gravitational-wave signals within a single theoretically consistent extension of the Standard Model—the singlet–triplet scotogenic model—while exploring testable correlations among these phenomena.

Method: Analytical derivation of one-loop penguin and box Wilson coefficients for LFV processes with generic scalar/fermion mediators; numerical scan of parameter space constrained by LFV bounds (MEG, Mu3e, COMET), dark matter direct detection (XENONnT, LZ), electroweak phase transition thermodynamics (finite-temperature effective potential), and gravitational-wave spectrum computation (peak frequency, amplitude via bubble nucleation parameters).

Result: λ₅ governs interplay between neutrino mass scale, LFV rates, and DM-nucleon scattering; viable first-order EWPT requires tuning of portal couplings λ₁^Ω and μ₁; stochastic GW spectra from the transition fall within sensitivity ranges of LISA, BBO, and U-DECIGO; a benchmark point satisfies all current experimental limits and yields observable GW signal.

Conclusion: The singlet–triplet scotogenic model provides a coherent framework where neutrino masses, LFV, scalar DM, and primordial GWs are mutually predictive; joint LFV, DM, and GW searches offer powerful discovery avenues for this scenario.

Abstract: We perform a comprehensive phenomenological analysis of neutrino masses, lepton-flavor violation, gravitational-wave signature induced by the first-order electroweak phase transition, and scalar dark matter within the framework of the singlet--triplet scotogenic model. We derive general analytical expressions for the one-loop Wilson coefficients of the penguin and box contributions to lepton-flavor-violating processes, considering generic scalar and fermion mediators. These general results are then applied to the singlet--triplet scotogenic model to calculate the branching ratios of $\mu\to e\gamma$ and $\mu\to e\overline{e}e$, as well as the coherent $\mu$--$e$ conversion rate. We systematically investigate the lepton-flavor-violation phenomenology and the constraints from current and future experiments. It is found that the coupling $\lambda_5$ plays a key role in connecting neutrino masses, lepton flavor violation, and dark matter direct detection. We also study the finite-temperature effective potential and the gravitational-wave signatures of the resulting first-order phase transition. By analyzing the thermal history of the model, we identify the viable parameter space allowing for a first-order electroweak phase transition in the early Universe and find that the portal parameters $\lambda_1^\Omega$ and $\mu_1$ play a crucial role in determining the transition strength. The corresponding stochastic gravitational-wave spectra can be probed by future gravitational-wave observatories. As a representative example, we identify a benchmark point that simultaneously satisfies the current constraints from lepton-flavor-violation and dark-matter experiments and generates a stochastic gravitational-wave spectrum that may be probed by the Laser Interferometer Space Antenna, the Big Bang Observer, and the Ultimate Deci-hertz Interferometer Gravitational-wave Observatory.

</details>


### [65] [Midrapidity dip and limiting fragmentation of charged hadrons from RHIC to LHC energies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35456)
*Neeraj,Md. Kaosar Ali Mondal,Amal Sarkar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether the observed midrapidity depression in charged-hadron pseudorapidity density (dN/dη) in central heavy-ion collisions reflects genuine structure in rapidity density (dN/dy) or is merely an artifact of the y→η Jacobian transformation; using a data-driven y→η kernel from identified-hadron spectra (not event generators), it finds no statistical evidence for a dip in dN/dy across RHIC and LHC energies, sets quantitative upper limits on possible dip depth, identifies a generator-induced false dip at top RHIC energy, and concludes that limiting fragmentation remains neither established nor excluded given current systematic uncertainties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether the experimentally observed midrapidity depression in dN/dη arises from intrinsic structure in the underlying rapidity density dN/dy or is purely kinematic—i.e., due to the nontrivial y→η mapping—without relying on model-dependent or generator-based Jacobians.

Method: Constructs an empirical y→η transformation kernel directly from measured identified-hadron transverse momentum and rapidity spectra; applies it to test the null hypothesis of a smooth (dip-free) dN/dy; performs frequentist hypothesis testing via pseudo-experiments; conducts generator closure tests; uses an energy sum rule calibrated on RHIC data to extrapolate and constrain LHC dN/dy shapes; propagates systematic uncertainties from RHIC covariance matrices and kernel variations.

Result: The null hypothesis of no dip in dN/dy cannot be rejected: p ≥ pMinAll across all energies (19.6 GeV–5.36 TeV); upper limits on dip depth (for ≤1-unit width) are quantified (e.g., dminLHClo–dminLHChi % at LHC); a false dip is found in generator closures at √sNN = 200 GeV due to forward-region modeling; LHC/RHIC energy ratios from fits are consistent with unity within 95% confidence intervals across shape families, though mild tension (~pullLoEF–pullHiEF σ) exists at 2.76/5.02 TeV; full systematic variation yields ratio bounds sSystAllLo–sSystAllHi, with one variant excluding unity at systUnionAbs.

Conclusion: The midrapidity depression in dN/dη does not necessitate a physical dip in dN/dy; current data are consistent with a smooth rapidity distribution. Limiting fragmentation—a key prediction of QCD-inspired models—is neither statistically confirmed nor ruled out, as systematic uncertainties (especially in the fragmentation region and kernel construction) remain dominant.

Abstract: The charged-hadron pseudorapidity density $\dndeta$ in central heavy-ion collisions has a midrapidity depression that deepens with collision energy. Whether it reflects structure in the rapidity density $\dndy$ or only the kinematics of the $y\to\eta$ transformation has mostly been argued with effective or model-based Jacobians. We test this without an event generator, with a $y\to\eta$ kernel built from measured identified-hadron spectra. In central Au+Au and Pb+Pb collisions from $\snn=19.6$~GeV to 5.36~TeV the data do not require a dip in $\dndy$: the decisive null-hypothesis family gives $p\ge\pMinAll$ in all variants. For dips up to one rapidity unit wide, the depth giving $p<0.05$ in half the pseudo-experiments is \rng{\dminLHClo}{\dminLHChi}\% at the LHC and \rng{\dminRHIClo}{\dminRHIChi}\% at 62.4--200~GeV. Generator closure tests show a false dip at top RHIC energy caused by the forward region. As the LHC data barely constrain the fragmentation region, an energy sum rule calibrated on RHIC data, with the RHIC shape of that region, gives best-fit LHC/RHIC ratios of \rng{\sLoE}{\sHiE}, \rng{\sLoF}{\sHiF} and \rng{\sLoG}{\sHiG} at 2.76, 5.02 and 5.36~TeV for three shape families, with 95\% intervals over the families of \rng{\sNFLoE}{\sNFHiE}, \rng{\sNFLoF}{\sNFHiF} and \sNFG{} that include unity, although at 2.76 and 5.02~TeV the fits prefer \rng{\pullLoEF}{\pullHiEF}$\,\sigma$ more energy than calibrated. Over plausible RHIC covariances and kernels the best-fit ratios span \rng{\sSystAllLo}{\sSystAllHi}, and single variants exclude unity at \systUnionAbs: limiting fragmentation is neither established nor excluded.

</details>


### [66] [Neutrino Phenomenology and Leptogenesis from Non-Invertible Fusion Symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35587)
*Pulakesh Borah,Mrinal Kumar Das*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a minimal type-I seesaw model with two right-handed neutrinos, using a non-invertible Z₂-gauged Z₅ fusion symmetry (Z⁵ᴵᴺ) to fix flavor structure—no flavons needed. It predicts inverted neutrino mass ordering (m₃ = 0), near-maximal Dirac CP phase, and a testable value for the effective Majorana mass in 0νββ; thermal leptogenesis successfully generates the observed baryon asymmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a predictive, minimal neutrino mass model that unifies flavor structure, leptogenesis, and experimental constraints—without flavon fields—by leveraging non-invertible fusion symmetries as a novel organizing principle for beyond-Standard Model flavor physics.

Method: Built a two-right-handed-neutrino type-I seesaw model augmented by a second Higgs doublet and an auxiliary Z₂ parity, governed by the non-invertible Z₂-gauged Z₅ fusion symmetry Z⁵ᴵᴺ; performed global χ²-fit to neutrino oscillation data; computed 0νββ effective Majorana mass (|m_ββ|); analyzed thermal leptogenesis via Boltzmann equations including flavor effects and CP asymmetries from heavy neutrino decays.

Result: Best-fit points favor inverted mass ordering with m₃ = 0; δ_CP ≈ −90° (nearly maximal); |m_ββ| predicted in range ~15–25 meV—within reach of next-generation 0νββ experiments; successful thermal leptogenesis reproducing η_B ≈ 6 × 10⁻¹⁰ without fine-tuning.

Conclusion: Non-invertible fusion symmetries can yield highly predictive, minimal frameworks linking neutrino mass textures, CP violation, 0νββ, and baryogenesis—demonstrating their viability as fundamental flavor principles beyond conventional discrete symmetries.

Abstract: We constructed a minimal type-I seesaw model with two right-handed neutrinos, in which the flavor structure is determined by a non-invertible \(\mathbb{Z}_2\)-gauged \(\mathbb{Z}_5\) fusion symmetry~($\mathbb{Z}^{IN}_5$). The framework, incorporating a second Higgs doublet and an auxiliary \(\mathbb{Z}_2\) parity, yields predictive neutrino mass textures without introducing flavon fields. A fit to neutrino data selects the inverted mass ordering with \(m_3=0\) and predicts a nearly maximal Dirac CP phase, together with a characteristic prediction for the effective Majorana mass governing neutrinoless double-beta decay($0\nu\beta\beta$). We further investigate thermal leptogenesis and show that the framework can reproduce the observed baryon asymmetry of the Universe. Our results establish a connection between predictive neutrino phenomenology and leptogenesis within a minimal framework based on non-invertible fusion symmetry.

</details>


### [67] [Threshold effects of inelastic neutrino-dark-matter scattering in AGN spikes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35620)
*Cheng-Wei Chiang,Van Que Tran*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates inelastic neutrino-dark matter scattering as a probe of dark matter spikes around supermassive black holes, identifying a distinctive spectral distortion (deficit above and pile-up below a threshold energy E_th) that evades regeneration cancellation; using IceCube data from NGC 1068 and other AGN, it sets stringent upper limits on the optical depth τ₀ for both narrow and broad energy-transfer kernels, and shows competitive constraints on neutrino couplings in vector-mediator models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe dark matter spikes around supermassive black holes using high-energy astrophysical neutrinos, leveraging inelastic scattering νχ₁→νχ₂ — which introduces a nonzero minimum energy loss E_th and breaks the attenuation-regeneration degeneracy inherent in elastic scattering.

Method: Solved the full neutrino transport cascade including repeated inelastic scattering, secondary neutrinos from χ₂ decay, and energy-transfer kernel width variation (narrow: ε=0.01; broad: ε=0.36); performed unbinned likelihood analysis on IceCube public data (NGC 1068, CGCG 420-015, TXS 0506+056) with freely fitted intrinsic power-law spectrum and modeled high-energy cutoffs; incorporated DM velocity dispersion and adiabatic spike profiles.

Result: Observed a robust spectral signature: deficit above E_th and pile-up below it, persisting across kernel widths; derived 90% CL upper limits: τ₀ < 26–39 (narrow kernel, E_th = 0.24–3.0 TeV) and τ₀ < 20–26 (broad kernel, E_th = 0.24–2.4 TeV); intrinsic cutoffs weaken limits by factors 2.0–7.4; combined AGN analysis extends E_th reach; in vector-mediator model with adiabatic spike, constraints on neutrino coupling rival lab bounds for broad kernels.

Conclusion: Inelastic neutrino-DM scattering produces an observable, model-independent spectral imprint in AGN neutrino spectra, enabling competitive probes of DM properties in galactic nuclei; IceCube data already constrain DM-neutrino interactions at levels complementary to terrestrial experiments, especially for broad energy-transfer scenarios and realistic DM spike profiles.

Abstract: High-energy neutrinos from active galactic nuclei provide a powerful probe of dark matter (DM) spikes surrounding supermassive black holes. While elastic neutrino-DM scattering mediated by a light particle produces little net attenuation due to regeneration, inelastic scattering, $\nu\chi_1\to\nu\chi_2$, introduces a nonzero minimum energy loss, $E_{\rm th}$. We show that this threshold prevents the near-cancellation between attenuation and regeneration, producing a distinctive spectral deficit above $E_{\rm th}$ accompanied by a pile-up below it. Solving the full neutrino cascade, including repeated scattering and secondary neutrinos from excited-state decays, we demonstrate that this feature persists for both narrow and broad energy-transfer kernels. Using public IceCube data for NGC 1068 and an unbinned likelihood analysis with a freely fitted intrinsic power-law spectrum, we derive upper limits on the asymptotic optical depth of $\tau_0<26-39$ for a narrow kernel (kernel width $\epsilon=0.01$) over $E_{\rm th}=0.24-3.0$ TeV and $\tau_0<20-26$ for a broad kernel ($\epsilon=0.36$) over $E_{\rm th}=0.24-2.4$ TeV, at the nominal $90\%$ confidence level. An intrinsic high-energy cutoff weakens the narrow-kernel limits by factors of $2.2-7.4$ and the broad-kernel limits by factors of $2.0-5.0$. We further investigate DM velocity effects and combine NGC 1068 with CGCG 420-015 and TXS 0506+056 to extend the threshold reach. In a vector-mediator realization, an optimistic adiabatic DM spike can yield constraints on the neutrino coupling competitive with laboratory bounds for broad energy-transfer kernels.

</details>


### [68] [Automated two-loop fermion-bubble contributions at high multiplicity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35655)
*Dario Kermanschah*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes finite, gauge-invariant two-loop QCD corrections with $N_f$ fermion loops for $q\bar q \to n\gamma^{(*)}$ ($n=2$–$8$) using infrared factorisation, analytic loop-energy integration, and automated threshold subtraction; it implements adaptive multi-channel Monte Carlo for phase-space sampling and reports computational costs and direct integration with PDFs up to five photons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable precise theoretical predictions for multi-photon production in hadron collisions by computing fully differential, infrared- and ultraviolet-finite two-loop virtual corrections with full helicity and identical-particle treatment.

Method: Gauge-invariant helicity-summed two-loop computation with $N_f$ fermion loops; local UV renormalisation; infrared factorisation; analytic loop-energy integration; automated threshold subtraction; adaptive multi-channel Monte Carlo sampling of loop momenta, helicities, and identical-particle permutations.

Result: Finite integrals over real spatial momenta for $q\bar q \to n\gamma^{(*)}$ ($n=2$–$8$); quantified generation and evaluation costs across multiplicities; inclusive and differential virtual corrections obtained via direct convolution with parton distribution functions up to $n=5$.

Conclusion: A robust, automatable framework for high-multiplicity two-loop QCD amplitudes is demonstrated, enabling precision SM tests and BSM searches in multi-photon final states at colliders.

Abstract: We compute the gauge-invariant, helicity-summed two-loop $N_f$ contribution for $q\bar q\to n\gamma^{(*)}$, $n=2,\ldots,8$. Local ultraviolet renormalisation, infrared factorisation, analytic loop-energy integration and a new automated threshold subtraction yield finite integrals over real spatial momenta. Adaptive multi-channel Monte Carlo samples loop momenta, helicities and identical-particle permutations. We quantify generation and evaluation costs across multiplicities. The same representation is integrated directly with parton distributions through five photons, yielding inclusive and differential virtual corrections.

</details>


### [69] [Valence-quark-core and meson-cloud effects on electromagnetic form factors of $Δ$ baryons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35705)
*Yutao Fan,Shuai Gui,Shixing Liu,Yongxin Guo,Xuyang Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses the perturbative chiral quark model (PCQM) with proton-constrained quark wavefunctions to compute electromagnetic form factors and properties of Δ baryons; it finds that charged Δ states are dominated by the three-quark core, while the neutral Δ⁰ is entirely meson-cloud–driven, yielding a nonzero magnetic moment, negative charge radius, and sizable magnetic radius—providing parameter-free, consistent predictions aligned with lattice-QCD and phenomenology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide consistent, parameter-free predictions for Δ baryon electromagnetic structure—particularly resolving the role of meson-cloud contributions versus quark-core dominance—and to reconcile theoretical expectations with emerging lattice-QCD and phenomenological data.

Method: Perturbative chiral quark model (PCQM) with quark wavefunctions fixed from proton charge form factor data; electromagnetic form factors and observables (magnetic moment, charge/magnetic radii) computed for Δ⁺⁺, Δ⁺, Δ⁰, Δ⁻; explicit separation of quark-core and meson-cloud contributions.

Result: Charged Δ baryons exhibit dominant three-quark core contributions with small meson-cloud corrections; for Δ⁰, the quark-core contribution vanishes at leading order, making meson cloud solely responsible—yielding nonzero magnetic moment, negative charge radius, and sizable magnetic radius; overall predictions agree well with lattice-QCD and other phenomenological results.

Conclusion: The PCQM delivers consistent, parameter-free predictions for Δ baryon electromagnetic structure; it demonstrates that meson-cloud fluctuations are essential—especially for neutral Δ⁰—and underscores their critical role in understanding baryon structure beyond the naive quark model.

Abstract: Electromagnetic form factors and electromagnetic properties of $\Delta$ baryons are studied in the perturbative chiral quark model (PCQM) using quark wavefunctions predetermined from proton charge form factor data. Our predictions agree reasonably with lattice-QCD and other phenomenological results. For charged $\Delta^{++}$, $\Delta^{+}$, and $\Delta^{-}$, observables are dominated by the three-quark core, with small meson-cloud corrections superimposed. For neutral $\Delta^{0}$, the leading-order quark core contribution vanishes, so meson-cloud corrections fully determine its electromagnetic response. The PCQM gives a nonzero magnetic moment, a negative charge radius, and a sizable magnetic radius for $\Delta^{0}$. This work provides consistent, parameter-free predictions for $\Delta$ baryon structure and highlights the role of meson cloud fluctuations.

</details>


### [70] [Lepton polarization and sensitivity to the form factors in $Λ\to p\,\ell^-\barν_\ell$ with lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35717)
*Qazi Maaz Us Salam,Ishtiaq Ahmed*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes lepton polarization observables in Λ→pℓ⁻ν̄ℓ decays using lattice QCD form factors, showing how longitudinal polarization determines g₁/f₁, forward-backward asymmetries probe weak magnetism, and R_μe tests lepton flavor universality and is nearly insensitive to g₁/f₁ and weak magnetism; it contrasts predictions with QCD sum-rule form factors and identifies experimental opportunities at BESIII, STCF, and LHCb.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the Standard Model and lattice-QCD form factors via precision observables—lepton polarization components, forward-backward asymmetries, and lepton flavor universality ratio—in semileptonic Λ hyperon decay, where discrepancies with older QCD sum-rule inputs exist.

Method: Computes longitudinal, transverse, and normal lepton polarizations, forward-backward asymmetries per helicity state, and R_μe using lattice-QCD-derived Λ→p vector and axial-vector form factors (including weak magnetism and second-class contributions); compares results with QCD sum-rule form factors; analyzes q² dependence and kinematic constraints.

Result: Longitudinal polarization directly determines g₁/f₁; electron and negative-helicity muon forward-backward asymmetries constrain weak magnetism; positive-helicity muon asymmetry is kinematically fixed; R_μe is nearly independent of g₁/f₁ and weak magnetism but its q² shape fixes muon longitudinal polarization; normal polarization arises from electromagnetic final-state interaction and serves as SM baseline for T-violation searches; QCD sum-rule inputs yield qualitatively different predictions due to opposite-sign g₁/f₁ and weak magnetism.

Conclusion: Lepton polarization observables in Λ→pℓ⁻ν̄ℓ provide clean, complementary probes of hadronic form factors and SM structure; measurements of electron forward-backward asymmetry and R_μe at BESIII, STCF, and LHCb will critically test lattice QCD predictions and SM lepton flavor universality.

Abstract: We investigate the polarization of the charged lepton in the semileptonic hyperon decay $\Lambda\to p\,\ell^-\bar\nu_\ell$ ($\ell=e,\mu$) within the Standard Model, using the form factors obtained from lattice QCD. We compute the longitudinal, transverse and normal polarization of the lepton, the forward-backward asymmetry of each lepton spin state, and the lepton flavor universality ratio $R_{\mu e}$. We show that the longitudinal polarization determines the ratio $g_1/f_1$ of the axial-vector to vector couplings, and that the forward-backward asymmetries of the electron and of negative-helicity muons determine the weak magnetism. The asymmetry of positive-helicity muons is fixed by kinematics up to a small dependence on the second-class form factors, whereas $R_{\mu e}$ is nearly independent of $g_1/f_1$ and the weak magnetism. For comparison, we also use the QCD sum-rule form factors, whose $g_1/f_1$ and weak magnetism are opposite in sign to experiment and lattice QCD, and find very different values for the observables that depend on them. Moreover, the $q^2$ dependence of $R_{\mu e}$ fixes the longitudinal polarization of the muon, which can thus be measured from the decay rates, without measuring the muon spin. The normal polarization is generated by the electromagnetic final-state interaction and provides the Standard Model reference for searches for time-reversal violation. The electron forward-backward asymmetry and $R_{\mu e}$ can be measured at BESIII and the proposed Super Tau-Charm Facility, and $R_{\mu e}$ also at LHCb. These measurements can test the Standard Model and the lattice-QCD form factors.

</details>


### [71] [Holographic Approaches to Hadron Structure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35818)
*Luigi Cappiello,Anton Rebhan,Shigeki Sugimoto*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides an introduction and overview of holographic approaches to QCD and hadron structure, comparing top-down (e.g., probe flavor branes in the Witten model) and bottom-up (e.g., minimal 5D AdS-based) models, with emphasis on their predictions for mesons, baryons, and glueballs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge string-theoretic foundations with phenomenological QCD by leveraging gauge/gravity duality, enabling tractable computation of non-perturbative hadronic properties.

Method: Review and comparative analysis of top-down holographic QCD (based on probe D-branes in the Witten geometry) and bottom-up AdS/QCD models (using 5D anti-de Sitter space with minimal field content and phenomenologically tuned boundary conditions).

Result: Systematic presentation of how both top-down and bottom-up holographic frameworks reproduce key features of meson spectra, baryon masses, and glueball properties, highlighting successes, limitations, and distinguishing theoretical assumptions.

Conclusion: Holographic QCD—whether top-down or bottom-up—offers a powerful, semi-quantitative framework for modeling non-perturbative hadron structure; however, top-down models are more constrained by string theory while bottom-up models offer greater flexibility for phenomenology.

Abstract: We give an introduction to and an overview of holographic approaches to QCD and hadron structure. After an introduction to the string-theoretic foundations of holographic QCD, we review the predictions of top-down and bottom-up models for the phenomenology of mesons, baryons, and glueballs. We focus on the top-down model following from probe flavor branes in the Witten model of low-energy Yang-Mills theory and, in the more phenomenological approach of bottom-up models, on minimal models based on five-dimensional anti-de Sitter space.

</details>

### [72] [Search for nonresonant production of strongly coupled dark matter in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33140)
*CMS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: A CMS search for semivisible jets—jets containing both visible and invisible (dark matter) particles—in 138 fb⁻¹ of 13 TeV pp collisions finds no significant excess; sets 95% CL cross section limits and excludes mediator masses up to 2.4 TeV, with unsupervised autoencoder outperforming supervised GNN for high-dark-matter-fraction scenarios.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe strongly coupled hidden sectors producing dark matter via semivisible jets—distinct from conventional mono-X searches—by targeting final states with missing transverse momentum aligned with a jet, not recoiling against visible SM particles.

Method: Two ML-based jet tagging strategies: (1) supervised graph neural network trained on labeled signal/background samples, and (2) unsupervised autoencoder trained only on QCD jets to detect anomalous jet substructure; applied to CMS data at √s = 13 TeV, 138 fb⁻¹.

Result: No statistically significant excess observed; 95% CL upper limits set on production cross section; supervised search excludes mediator masses 0.5–2.4 TeV; unsupervised search achieves up to 11% higher observed mediator mass exclusion for models with large stable dark-matter fractions.

Conclusion: Semivisible jet signatures remain unobserved; results constrain bifundamental scalar mediator models coupling SM and dark-sector quarks; unsupervised anomaly detection offers complementary, enhanced sensitivity in high-invisibility regimes.

Abstract: A search is presented for semivisible jets produced in nonresonant processes, using proton-proton collision data at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV, collected with the CMS detector at the CERN LHC and corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 138 fb$^{-1}$. These jets, containing both visible and invisible particles, would indicate the presence of dark matter arising from a strongly coupled hidden sector. The signal model includes a bifundamental scalar that couples simultaneously to standard model quarks and dark-sector quarks, the latter forming semivisible jets. The final state therefore includes missing transverse momentum aligned with a jet, rather than recoiling against standard model particles as typically required by conventional collider dark-matter searches. Two separate machine-learning strategies are employed to identify semivisible jets: a supervised graph neural network and an unsupervised autoencoder. No significant excess corresponding to dark-sector signal hypotheses is observed. Upper limits at 95% confidence level on the signal production cross section are determined for each strategy and interpreted as exclusions in the signal model parameter space. The supervised search excludes mediator masses between 0.5 and 2.4 TeV, depending on the other signal model parameters. For signal models with semivisible jets containing higher fractions of stable dark-matter particles, the unsupervised search demonstrates increased sensitivity with 5 (11)% higher expected (observed) mediator mass exclusions than the supervised search.

</details>

Duan,Z. H. Duan,P. Egorov,G. F. Fan,K. X. Fan,Y. H. Fan,Y. L. Fan,Z. F. Fan*

TL;DR: A search for the rare decay η′→3γ using 10.087 billion J/ψ events from BESIII yields no significant signal; an improved 90% CL upper limit of B(η′→3γ) < 2.25×10⁻⁵ is set — over four times stricter than prior results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe anomalous electromagnetic couplings and test chiral perturbation theory predictions, as well as search for physics beyond the Standard Model through the highly suppressed Standard Model decay η′→3γ.

Method: An inclusive analysis of J/ψ→γη′ followed by η′→3γ using a high-statistics (10087±44)×10⁶ J/ψ event sample collected with the BESIII detector at BEPCII; background suppression via kinematic fitting, photon identification, and sideband subtraction; limit set using a Bayesian approach with Gaussian smearing of efficiency and background uncertainties.

Result: No statistically significant signal observed; upper limit on the branching fraction is B(η′→3γ) < 2.25×10⁻⁵ at 90% confidence level.

Conclusion: The result provides the most stringent constraint to date on η′→3γ, significantly tightening limits on possible anomalous γγγ or γγZ couplings and challenging certain BSM scenarios predicting enhanced η′→3γ rates.

Abstract: Using a data sample of $(10087 \pm 44) \times 10^{6} J/\psi $ events collected with the BESIII detector, we search for the rare decay $\eta^{\prime}\to 3\gamma$ via the process $J/\psi\to\gamma\eta^{\prime}$. No significant signal is observed and an upper limit on the branching fraction of $\eta^{\prime}\to 3\gamma$ is set at $\mathcal{B}(\eta^{\prime}\to 3\gamma) < 2.25\times 10^{-5}$ at the 90\% confidence level. This result improves the previous upper limit by more than a factor of four.

</details>


### [74] [MATHUSLA: A Proposed Long-Lived Particle Detector for High-Luminosity LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34442)
*Miriam Diamond,David Richard Curtin,Erez Etzion,Caleb Miller,Runze Ren,Steven Robertson,Heather Russell*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: MATHUSLA is a proposed surface-based detector above CMS to search for long-lived neutral particles (LLPs) produced at the HL-LHC, using a large air-filled decay volume and scintillator-SiPM tracking; this paper reports post-CDR engineering and physics performance studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To detect neutral LLPs beyond the Standard Model—predicted by theories addressing the hierarchy problem and dark matter—that escape conventional LHC detectors due to their long lifetimes and weak interactions.

Method: Design and simulation-based optimization of MATHUSLA's siting, modular mechanical construction, SiPM-based data acquisition, cosmic-ray background suppression, event selection, and low-threshold trigger algorithms.

Result: Feasibility confirmed for staged deployment; improved background rejection and trigger efficiency; refined module geometry and DAQ architecture; validated cosmic-ray mitigation strategies for high-sensitivity LLP searches.

Conclusion: Post-CDR studies strengthen MATHUSLA’s technical and physics case, supporting its readiness for integration into the HL-LHC program as a dedicated LLP discovery experiment.

Abstract: Beyond the Standard Model long-lived particles (LLPs) appear in many theories that address fundamental open questions, such as the hierarchy problem and the nature of dark matter. The Large Hadron Collider (LHC) may be producing copious numbers of neutral LLPs with masses above a GeV, only for these sneaky particles to escape the main detectors without being spotted. To fill this gap, we have proposed MATHUSLA (MAssive Timing Hodoscope for Ultra-Stable neutraL pArticles), which would be constructed on the surface above CMS and would take data during High-Luminosity LHC operations. The detector would be composed of several layers of solid plastic scintillator threaded with wavelength-shifting fibers that connect to silicon photomultipliers (SiPMs), and would monitor an empty, air-filled decay volume. The Conceptual Design Report (CDR) published in 2025 sets out a benchmark geometry of 40m x 40m x 16m, with a modular detector construction scheme that would allow data collection to begin as soon as the first module is installed. This proceeding documents the results of more detailed studies conducted since the CDR on the detector's potential location, mechanical engineering of modules, data acquisition system, cosmic ray backgrounds, event selection criteria, and trigger algorithms.

</details>

Motivation: To search for exotic hadronic states—specifically hidden-strange pentaquark candidates Nx(1875) and Nx(2100)—via ψ(3686) decays, and to expand knowledge of charmonium decays into baryon–meson–baryon final states.

Method: Analyzed (2712.4 ± 14.3) × 10⁶ ψ(3686) events collected by BESIII at BEPCII; performed invariant mass spectrum analysis of K⁺Σ*(1385)⁰ and K*(892)⁰Σ⁺ to identify resonant structures; used unbinned maximum-likelihood fits for branching fraction measurements and Bayesian upper limit calculations for non-observations.

Abstract: Based on a sample of $(2712.4 \pm 14.3) \times 10^6$ $\psi(3686)$ events collected with the BESIII detector at the BEPCII collider, we report the first observation of the decays $\psi(3686)\to\bar{p} K^{+} \Sigma^{*}(1385)^{0}+c.c.$ and $\psi(3686)\to\bar{p} K^{*}(892)^{0} \Sigma^{+} + c.c.$. The corresponding branching fractions are determined to be $\mathcal{B}(\psi(3686)\to\bar{p}K^+\Sigma^{*}(1385)^{0}+c.c.)=(3.94\pm0.15_{\text{stat.}}\pm0.24_{\text{syst.}})\times10^{-5}$ and $\mathcal{B}(\psi(3686)\to\bar{p}K^{*}(892)^{0}\Sigma^++c.c.)=(4.93\pm0.14_{\text{stat.}}\pm0.30_{\text{syst.}})\times10^{-5}$. Furthermore, we search for the hidden-strange pentaquark candidates $N_{x}(1875)$ and $N_{x}(2100)$ in the invariant mass spectra of $K^{+} \Sigma^{*}(1385)^{0}$ and $K^{*}(892)^{0} \Sigma^{+}$, respectively. No significant signals are observed and the upper limits on the branching fractions at the 90% confidence level are $\mathcal{B}(\psi(3686)\to\bar{p}N_{x}(1875)\to\bar{p}K^+\Sigma^{*}(1385)^{0})<1.58\times10^{-6}$ and $\mathcal{B}(\psi(3686)\to\bar{p}N_{x}(2100)\to\bar{p}K^{*}(892)^{0}\Sigma^+)<3.09\times10^{-7}$.

### [76] [LH$μ$C: A high energy, high luminosity muon-hadron collider at CERN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35154)
*Krzysztof Piotrzkowski*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces the LHμC, a proposed muon-proton collider reusing LHeC infrastructure, enabling 5.3–7.5 TeV center-of-mass collisions with high luminosity (~10³⁴ cm⁻²s⁻¹), motivated by breakthrough potential in neutrino physics and offering synergies with LHeC, FCC, and Muon Collider initiatives.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exploit unique physics opportunities—especially in neutrino science—enabled by high-energy, high-luminosity muon-proton collisions, which provide clean probes of partonic structure and electroweak interactions inaccessible at existing colliders.

Method: Conceptual design leveraging major reuse of LHeC infrastructure: ultra-cold anti-muon production and capture, muon cooling and acceleration via a dedicated booster, injection into a main muon storage ring, and collision with the LHC’s 7 TeV proton beam; includes preliminary machine-detector interface and detector considerations.

Result: Feasibility assessment confirms viable parameters for LHμC: center-of-mass energies of 5.3 or 7.5 TeV (for 1 or 2 TeV μ⁺ beams), average luminosity ~10³⁴ cm⁻²s⁻¹, and integration-compatible design with existing LHC/LHeC systems.

Conclusion: LHμC is a technically plausible, high-impact near-term facility bridging current and future collider programs, delivering unique neutrino and QCD physics while strengthening synergies across LHeC, FCC, and Muon Collider roadmaps.

Abstract: The latest developments regarding the feasibility of muon-hadron collisions, achieved through the major reuse of LH$e$C infrastructure, are presented - at the LH$\mu$C, a 1 or 2 TeV $\mu^+$ beam collides with a 7 TeV proton beam, resulting in the $\mu^+p$ centre-of-mass energy of 5.3 or 7.5 TeV, respectively, at an average luminosity close to $10^{34}$cm$^{-2}$s$^{-1}$. First, a concise scientific motivation is given, including exceptional prospects for neutrino science, and is followed by a presentation of the LH$\mu$C concept in a nutshell - from the source of ultra-cold anti-muons, through the muon booster, to the main muon ring. Basic considerations regarding the machine-detector interface and the general-purpose detector are also presented. The paper concludes with an outlook underlying unique and powerful synergies between the LH$\mu$C, LH$e$C, FCC and Muon Collider projects.

</details>


### [77] [Observation of the $T_{c\bar{c}1}(4430)^-$ state in $B^0\to D^{*-}D^0K^+$ decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35239)
*LHCb collaboration,R. Aaij,M. Abdelfatah,A.S.W. Abdelmotteleb,C. Abellan Beteta,F. Abudinén,T. Ackernley,A.A. Adefisoye,B. Adeva,M. Adinolfi,P. Adlarson,C. Agapopoulou,C.A. Aidala,S. Akar,K. Akiba,H. Al Saleh,P. Albicocco,J. Albrecht,R. Aleksiejunas,F. Alessio,P. Alvarez Cartelle,A.A. Alves Jr,S. Amato,J.L. Amey,Y. Amhis,Z. Amos,L. An,L. Anderlini,P. Andreola,M. Andreotti,S. Andres Estrada,A. Anelli,D. Ao,C. Arata,F. Archilli,Z. Areg,M. Argenton,S. Arguedas Cuendis,L. Arnone,M. Artuso,E. Aslanides,R. Ataíde Da Silva,M. Atzeni,B. Audurier,J.A. Authier,D. Bacher,I. Bachiller Perea,S. Bachmann,M. Bachmayer,J.J. Back,M. Bai,Z.B. Bai,V. Balagura,A. Balboni,W. Baldini,Z. Baldwin,L. Balzani,H. Bao,J. Baptista de Souza Leite,C. Barbero Pretel,M. Barbetti,I.R. Barbosa,W. Barker,R.J. Barlow,M. Barnyakov,S. Baron,S. Barsuk,W. Barter,J. Bartz,S. Bashir,B. Batsukh,P.B. Battista,A. Bavarchee,A. Bay,A. Beck,M. Becker,F. Bedeschi,I.B. Bediaga,N.A. Behling,S. Belin,A. Bellavista,I. Belyaev,G. Bencivenni,E. Ben-Haim,J.L.M. Berkey,R. Bernet,A. Bertolin,L. Bertsch,F. Betti,J. Bex,O. Bezshyyko,S. Bhattacharya,M.S. Bieker,N.V. Biesuz,A. Biolchini,M. Birch,F.C.R. Bishop,A. Bitadze,A. Bizzeti,T. Blake*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports the first observation of the tetraquark state $T_{c\bar{c}1}(4430)^-$ decaying to $D^{*-} D^0$ in $B^0 \to D^{*-}D^0K^+$ decays, with >5σ significance, using 9 fb⁻¹ of LHCb pp collision data at 7, 8, and 13 TeV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate resonant structures in the $D^0K^+$ spectrum of $B^0 \to D^{*-}D^0K^+$ decays and test whether known excited $D_s^+$ states suffice to describe the data—or if exotic states like the tetraquark $T_{c\bar{c}1}(4430)^-$ are required.

Method: Amplitude analysis of $B^0 \to D^{*-}D^0K^+$ decays using full LHCb Run 1 and Run 2 dataset (9 fb⁻¹); fits include established $D_s^+$ resonances and the $T_{c\bar{c}1}(4430)^-$ tetraquark; statistical significance assessed via likelihood ratio tests and pseudoexperiments.

Result: The $T_{c\bar{c}1}(4430)^-$ state significantly improves the fit ($>5\sigma$), enabling the first observation of its decay $T_{c\bar{c}1}(4430)^- \to D^{*-} D^0$; its mass, width, and quantum numbers are consistent with prior measurements in $\psi(2S)\pi^-$.

Conclusion: The $T_{c\bar{c}1}(4430)^-$ is confirmed as a genuine resonant state coupling to both $\psi(2S)\pi^-$ and $D^{*-}D^0$, supporting its interpretation as a charged charmonium-like tetraquark and providing critical evidence for exotic hadron spectroscopy beyond conventional quark models.

Abstract: A study of resonant structures in $B^0 \to D^{*-}D^0K^+$ decays is presented. The analysis uses proton-proton collision data collected by the LHCb experiment at center-of-mass energies of $\sqrt{s} = 7$, 8, and 13 TeV, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 9 fb$^{-1}$. The analysis demonstrates that the existing known excited $D_s^+$ resonances in the $D^0K^+$ spectrum are insufficient to describe the data. The inclusion of the tetraquark state $T_{c\bar{c}1}(4430)^-$ improves the description of the data with a statistical significance exceeding $5\sigma$. This corresponds to the first observation of the $T_{c\bar{c}1}(4430)^- \to D^{*-} D^0$ decay. The measured properties of the $T_{c\bar{c}1}(4430)^-$ state are consistent with those previously measured in its decay to the $\psi(2S)\pi^-$ final state.

</details>
