### [78] [Spectroscopy of the SU(2) scalar-fermion-gauge system under the influence of the Brout-Englert-Higgs effect](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33611)
*Axel Maas,Sofie Martins,Georg Wieland*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper studies the ground state spectrum of a scalar-fermion-gauge system with SU(2) gauge symmetry, scalar doublet, and two vectorial fermion generations using lattice simulations (HiRep); finds spectral insensitivity to bare fermion mass beyond trivial shifts, consistent with a weakly coupled theory realizing the Fröhlich-Morchio-Strocchi mechanism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the Brout-Englert-Higgs effect shapes the non-perturbative ground state spectrum in a dynamical scalar-fermion-gauge system, particularly whether spontaneous symmetry breaking induces decoupling or modifies spectral structure beyond perturbative expectations.

Method: Lattice field theory simulations using the HiRep code; systematic variation of bare fermion mass; analysis of hadronic and scalar spectra in an SU(2) gauge theory with fundamental scalar doublet and two degenerate vectorial fermion generations.

Result: The physical spectrum (e.g., scalar and fermionic bound-state masses) shows no qualitative change with bare fermion mass—only trivial linear shifts proportional to fermion mass—indicating robustness of the Higgs mechanism and absence of phase transitions or emergent strong coupling in the scanned parameter range.

Conclusion: The observed spectral insensitivity supports a weakly coupled effective description where the Fröhlich-Morchio-Strocchi mechanism operates: the physical Higgs boson is a composite Goldstone mode screened by gauge interactions, and fermions remain effectively decoupled from the scalar sector's dynamics beyond mass renormalization.

Abstract: We investigate the ground state spectrum of a dynamical scalar-fermion-gauge system with an SU(2) gauge group, a scalar doublet, and two degenerate generations of vectorial fermions under the influence of the Brout-Englert-Higgs effect. To this end, we employ lattice methods using the HiRep code. We study the dependence of the system on the (bare) fermion mass and find that the spectrum remains unaffected, up to trivial fermion mass effects. The results are in line with a weakly coupled theory exhibiting the Fröhlich-Morchio-Strocchi mechanism.

</details>


### [79] [Two-Particle Correlation Function in a Finite Volume](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33681)
*Wren Yamada,Osamu Morimatsu,Toru Sato,Koichi Yazaki*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper analyzes finite-volume effects on two-particle correlation functions, showing that corrections to complex energies of unstable states decay exponentially with volume size, and that imaginary-time correlators interpolate between infinite-volume behavior (at small τ/L²) and ground-state dominance (at large τ/L²); results are supported by qualitative arguments and quantitative EFT/separable-potential models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify finite-volume corrections to the complex energies and correlation functions of unstable (resonant) two-particle states, crucial for lattice QCD and other finite-volume many-body calculations.

Method: Analytic continuation of the correlation function onto a complex-energy Riemann sheet; pole identification for unstable states; asymptotic analysis of finite-volume corrections; explicit computation using nonrelativistic effective field theory (contact interaction) and separable potential models.

Result: Finite-volume corrections to the complex energy (position) of unstable-state poles decay exponentially with system size L; the imaginary-time correlation function exhibits two scaling regimes: infinite-volume recovery as τ/L² → 0, and exponential dominance by the lowest-energy mode (including resonance) as τ/L² → ∞.

Conclusion: Unstable states are robust against finite-volume effects—their complex energies are exponentially well-approximated in large but finite volumes; this justifies using finite-volume spectra to extract resonance parameters with controlled systematic errors.

Abstract: We investigate the two-particle correlation function in a finite volume. First, defining an unphysical complex-energy sheet of the correlation function and identifying unstable states as poles on the sheet, we demonstrate that the expansion of the correlation function, which includes poles of unstable states, holds up to exponentially decreasing corrections with the volume size. Based on this result, we show that the finite-volume correction to the complex energy of the unstable state also decreases exponentially with the volume size, namely that finite-volume corrections are relatively small for the unstable states. Then, we show that the imaginary-time correlation function in a finite volume approaches that of the infinite volume as $\tau/L^2 \rightarrow 0$ while it is dominated by the lowest energy mode as $\tau/L^2 \rightarrow \infty$, where $\tau$ is the imaginary time, $L$ is the volume size. In drawing these conclusions, we first present a qualitative, general discussion and then demonstrate quantitative results using simple nonrelativistic models with a contact interaction (EFT) and a separable potential.

</details>


### [80] [Information geometry of continuum limits in lattice field theory: The Gross-Neveu model with Wilson fermions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34277)
*Akio Tomiya*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the Fisher metric of the auxiliary field distribution in the large-N 2D lattice Gross-Neveu model with Wilson fermions, revealing distinct continuum limits for long-distance vs. full-field Fisher metrics, and shows dimensional transmutation manifests as an exponential relation between expectation and natural coordinates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how statistical geometry—specifically the Fisher information metric—captures renormalization group flow and dimensional transmutation in lattice field theories, particularly distinguishing physical (long-distance) degrees of freedom from regularization artifacts.

Method: Analyzes the Fisher metric of the auxiliary field σ-distribution in the large-N two-dimensional lattice Gross-Neveu model with Wilson fermions; compares scaling behavior of metric components under a → 0 with fixed mass gap Λ; identifies natural vs. expectation coordinates; examines mode decomposition (long-distance vs. full spectrum); computes curvature asymptotics near criticality.

Result: The Fisher metric exhibits two distinct continuum limits: (i) for long-distance modes (fixed physical momentum), irrelevant kinetic-term directions shrink relative to the mass-term direction as ~aΛ log(1/(aΛ)), leaving only (Λ, m) as relevant; (ii) for the full P(σ), kinetic and mass directions scale similarly, preserving lattice-scale correlations; contact-term deformations vanish in both limits, becoming indistinguishable from coupling changes; curvature of the coupling plane diverges as (1/2) log²(1/(aΛ)), not as correlation volume.

Conclusion: Fisher geometry distinguishes physically meaningful RG flow from regularization-dependent structure: dimensional transmutation emerges geometrically via coordinate transformation, and the critical point is infinitely distant in Fisher distance—highlighting the metric’s sensitivity to UV completion and its utility for characterizing continuum limits beyond thermodynamic analogies.

Abstract: When the Boltzmann weight is nonnegative, the actions of a lattice field theory define a family of probability distributions that forms a statistical manifold, and its Fisher metric gives the number of measurements needed to distinguish two nearby actions. In the large-$N$ limit of the two-dimensional lattice Gross-Neveu model with Wilson fermions, we study the continuum limit, in which the lattice spacing $a$ is taken to zero with the mass gap $\Lambda$ fixed, through the Fisher metric of the distribution of the auxiliary field $\sigma$. The renormalization condition fixes an expectation coordinate, and dimensional transmutation becomes an exponential relation between it and a natural coordinate. The main result is that the $a\to0$ limits differ between the Fisher metric of the long-distance modes, whose momenta are fixed in physical units, and the Fisher metric of the full $P(\sigma)$. In the former, the lengths of the infinitely many irrelevant directions that change the kinetic term shrink, relative to the mass-term direction, to order $a\Lambda\log(1/(a\Lambda))$ or smaller, and when several modes are combined, only the two directions $(\Lambda,m)$ of the continuum theory remain. In the latter, the kinetic-term directions shrink at the same rate as the mass-term direction, and the difference in the regularization remains in correlations at distances of the order of the lattice spacing. An exception is the deformation that only adds a contact term to the propagator: as $a\to0$ its length goes to zero in both metrics, and it becomes indistinguishable from a change of the coupling $1/g^2$ (a lattice version of Kutasov's result). The curvature of the coupling plane at fixed kinetic term diverges as $(1/2)\log^2(1/(a\Lambda))$, not as the correlation volume often assumed in thermodynamic geometry, and the critical point is infinitely far in Fisher distance from any point at finite $a$.

</details>


### [81] [Pion vector form factor from LapH-smeared currents in lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34397)
*Frank X. Lee,Andrei Alexandru*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper introduces a LapH-smearing technique for quark fields to efficiently compute the pion vector form factor from three-point functions, demonstrating consistency with conventional SST methods and enabling inclusion of disconnected diagrams.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop an efficient and symmetry-preserving method for extracting hadron form factors—particularly the pion vector form factor—from lattice QCD three-point functions, while properly handling both connected and disconnected contributions.

Method: Uses Laplacian Heaviside (LapH) smearing—based on low-lying eigenmodes of the spatial Laplacian—to construct smeared quark fields and currents; computes three-point functions on a two-flavor nHYP-smeared $24^3 \times 48$ lattice (a = 0.12 fm, $m_\pi = 315$ MeV); performs extrapolation in the number of LapH eigenmodes and compares results to standard point-source/sink/current (SST) calculations.

Result: LapH-smeared current-based extraction reproduces the pion vector form factor obtained via conventional SST method; LapH propagators preserve all symmetries and allow unified treatment of connected/disconnected diagrams at reduced computational cost.

Conclusion: LapH-smearing provides a viable, efficient, and symmetry-respecting framework for computing hadronic form factors and polarizabilities—including those requiring disconnected diagrams—on the lattice.

Abstract: We explore a method to extract pion vector form factor from three-point functions using currents constructed from LapH-smeared quark fields. LapH employs the low eigenmodes of the Laplacian Heaviside operator on the lattice to smear out the quark fields in spatial directions. The LapH-smeared quark propagator retains all symmetry properties of the original one and can be re-used to construct correlation functions at a reduced cost, with equal treatment of connected and disconnected diagrams. The calculations are performed on a two-flavor dynamical nHYP-smeared ensemble on the $24^3\times 48$ lattice at a spacing of 0.12 fm and pion mass of 315 MeV. We show that an extrapolation in the number of low eigenmodes in the LapH subspace can reproduce the same form factor computed from the conventional SST method with point source, sink, and current. The study opens up the possibility of applying LapH-smeared currents to extract hadron form factors and polarizabilities including disconnected diagrams.

</details>


### [82] [Large-$N$ $π^+π^+$ scattering from twisted Eguchi--Kawai reduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35176)
*Claudio Bonanno,Margarita García Pérez,Antonio González-Arroyo,Ken-Ichi Ishikawa,Masanori Okawa,Fernando Romero-López*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: First computation of π⁺π⁺ scattering length in large-N QCD using twisted Eguchi–Kawai (TEK) lattice formulation; achieves controlled large-N, continuum, and chiral limits; validates chiral perturbation theory predictions and S-matrix bootstrap bounds; extracts O(1/N) corrections by combining TEK and standard lattice data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute the π⁺π⁺ scattering length in the 't Hooft large-N limit of QCD — a regime where analytical control is limited and lattice studies are challenging due to volume and scaling constraints — using a reduced-volume approach (TEK) to overcome traditional large-N computational bottlenecks.

Method: Twisted Eguchi–Kawai (TEK) lattice QCD formulation with N up to 1156; systematic variation of quark masses and lattice spacings; extrapolation to large-N, continuum, and chiral limits; comparison with next-to-leading-order Chiral Perturbation Theory (χPT); combination with prior standard lattice data (N = 3–6) to isolate O(1/N) corrections.

Result: Precise determination of the π⁺π⁺ scattering length in the large-N limit; agreement with NLO χPT chiral behavior; consistency of the low-energy coupling (governed by sub-leading quark-mass dependence) with S-matrix bootstrap bounds; first numerical extraction of O(1/N) corrections to the scattering length.

Conclusion: The TEK approach enables robust large-N lattice studies of hadronic scattering; results validate theoretical expectations from χPT and analyticity-based bounds; sub-leading 1/N effects are quantitatively accessible, bridging large-N phenomenology and real-world (N=3) QCD.

Abstract: We present the first computation of the $\pi^+\pi^+$ scattering length in large-$N$ QCD in the 't Hooft limit, $N_{\scriptscriptstyle{\rm f}}/N\to 0$, using the twisted Eguchi--Kawai formulation of the lattice theory. We employ values of $N$ up to 1156 and consider several quark masses and lattice spacings to achieve controlled large-$N$, continuum, and chiral limits. The chiral behavior of the scattering length is compared with next-to-leading-order Chiral Perturbation Theory predictions. Our result for the low-energy coupling describing the sub-leading quark-mass dependence is consistent with existing S-matrix bootstrap bounds. Combining TEK results with previous lattice QCD data obtained using standard methods for $3\le N\le 6$, we are also able to extract sub-leading $\mathcal{O}(1/N)$ corrections to the large-$N$ scattering length.

</details>


### [83] [Negative-Power Density of States in Chiral-Orthogonal Disordered Systems and the IR Phase of QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35524)
*Jiří Adam,Andrei Alexandru,Navdeep Singh Dhindsa,Ivan Horváth,Frank X. Lee,Nilmani Mathur*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether chiral Anderson models—specifically 3D chiral-orthogonal systems with logarithmic off-diagonal disorder—can reproduce the negative-power-law Dirac spectral density (ρ(E) ∝ E^p, p ≈ −1) observed in the infrared (IR) phase of QCD. It finds that finite-W models do not yield true power-singular DOS at E=0 in the thermodynamic limit; instead, a robust negative-power scaling emerges near E=1, widening toward E=0 and E=∞ as W increases. The scaling exponent p(W,E) → −1 uniformly as W→∞, linking scale-invariant hopping statistics (1/t) to emergent scale-invariant eigenvalue statistics (1/E), thereby supporting an interpretation of QCD’s IR spectral behavior as arising from scale-invariant gluonic backgrounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether chiral Anderson models can qualitatively and quantitatively mimic the scale-invariant, negative-power Dirac spectral density (p ≈ −1 near zero energy) characteristic of the recently discovered IR phase of QCD — particularly whether such behavior stems from disorder-induced scale invariance rather than finite-size or lattice artifacts.

Method: Numerical analysis of 3D chiral-orthogonal Anderson models with logarithmically distributed off-diagonal hoppings t ∈ [e^{−W/2}, e^{W/2}], i.e., drawn from a regularized 1/t distribution. Computation of the density of states (DOS) ρ(E) across energies, systematic scaling of disorder strength W, and finite-size/thermodynamic-limit extrapolation to distinguish true singularities from crossover behavior.

Result: At finite W, no true E^p singularity exists at E=0 in the thermodynamic limit; instead, ρ(E) exhibits accurate negative-power scaling (p < 0) over an energy interval centered at E=1, which expands toward both E=0 and E=∞ as W increases. Crucially, lim_{W→∞} p(W,E) = −1 for all accessible E, indicating emergence of exact 1/E scaling in the strong-disorder limit.

Conclusion: The IR-phase-like Dirac spectral behavior is not tied to system size L but to disorder strength W — the IR cutoff is effectively set by W. This supports a physical picture where scale-invariant quantum interference in disordered chiral systems maps 1/t hopping noise onto 1/E eigenvalue statistics, offering a viable effective model for QCD’s IR sector driven by scale-invariant gluon backgrounds.

Abstract: The recently discovered IR phase of QCD is characterized by an infrared (IR) sector of Dirac modes that separates and dynamically decouples from the bulk of the spectrum, becoming scale invariant in the process [1]. Among key features of this IR sector is the negative power-law behavior of Dirac spectral density near zero with power $p$ close to $-1$. Here we start investigating to what extent can this behavior be mimicked by chiral Anderson models. In particular, we study 3-D chiral-orthogonal systems with logarithmic off-diagonal disorder where negative-power ``spikes'' have been observed [2]. We find that at finite disorder strength $W$ of moderate strength, these models in fact do not have a power-singular density of states (DOS) at zero energy in the thermodynamic limit. Rather, this power $p_1$ vanishes and the spectrum features a very accurate power behavior with $p<0$ on an interval around $E = 1$ spreading increasingly closer to $E=0$ and $E = \infty$ with increasing $W$. We connect this to the fact that random hoppings $t$ in these quantum systems are drawn from a scale-invariant $1/t$ distribution regularized via $t \in [e^{-W/2}, e^{W/2}]$, and DOS with negative power can naturally arise inside that range. We show that $\lim_{W \to \infty} p(W, E)=-1$ for all energies accessible by our data. This indicates that, upon removing the cutoff $W$, quantum mechanics transforms the scale-invariant $1/t$ noise into a scale-invariant $1/E$ distribution of eigenstates, which mimics the interpretation of scale-invariant Dirac spectral densities in the IR phase as being due to scale-invariant glue backgrounds [1]. Our results imply that, to model the IR sector of the IR phase, the IR cutoff in chiral Anderson systems needs to be viewed as descending from the randomness strength $W$ rather than the lattice size $L$.

</details>


### [84] [Toward Compute-Bound Lattice-QCD Software](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35526)
*Anthony V. Grebe*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper reanalyzes roofline performance estimates for lattice QCD solvers, highlighting the efficiency gains of multi-right-hand-side multigrid methods over traditional Krylov solvers, and introduces the qc library achieving >50% of processor roofline efficiency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the historically low efficiency ceiling of single-right-hand-side Krylov solvers in lattice QCD by leveraging memory-efficient multi-right-hand-side multigrid inverters.

Method: Reanalysis of roofline performance models and implementation of a high-efficiency multi-right-hand-side multigrid solver in the qc library.

Result: The qc library achieves over 50% of the processor roofline efficiency for lattice QCD inversion tasks.

Conclusion: Multi-right-hand-side multigrid methods significantly raise the practical efficiency ceiling for lattice QCD solvers, and the qc library demonstrates near-optimal hardware utilization.

Abstract: Multi-right-hand-side multigrid inverters for lattice QCD are much less memory constrained than single-right-hand-side Krylov solvers, so the upper bound on efficiency of modern solvers is much higher than what was historically possible. This work presents a reanalysis of roofline estimates to reflect this new reality. It also describes the qc library, which implements multi-right-hand-side multigrid at an efficiency that can exceed 50% of the processor roofline.

</details>


### [85] [Annealed sampling of diffusion models in lattice field theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35792)
*Gurtej Kanwar,Octavio Vega*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper proposes a hybrid sampling method for lattice field theory that combines energy-based diffusion models (trained via score matching) with either stochastic normalizing flows (SNFs) or flow-transformed replica exchange (T-REX), achieving exact sampling, polynomial scaling, and empirical gains over non-learned samplers in scalar field theory and the O(3) nonlinear sigma model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome critical slowing down in lattice field theory by leveraging machine learning—specifically, to unify the polynomial scaling and exactness of annealing methods with the training simplicity and efficiency of diffusion models.

Method: First, train an energy-based diffusion model using score matching on local-footprint energy models (enabling efficient gradient/divergence computation); then, sample from it using either SNFs or T-REX—both applied post-training without retraining. Theoretical analysis compares SNF and T-REX trade-offs (e.g., variance, computational cost, parallelizability).

Result: The hybrid method achieves exact sampling (no bias), exhibits predicted polynomial scaling, and shows significant sampling efficiency improvements over baseline non-learned methods (e.g., HMC) in 2D scalar field theory and the O(3) nonlinear sigma model on lattice configurations.

Conclusion: Combining score-matched diffusion models with flexible post-hoc sampling schemes (SNFs or T-REX) provides a principled, scalable, and empirically effective framework for alleviating critical slowing down in lattice field theory, unifying theoretical guarantees with practical learnability.

Abstract: Machine learning methods have recently gained increasing interest as possible means of solving critical slowing down in lattice field theory, with particular emphasis on sampling methods using field transformations such as normalizing flows, annealing/out-of-equilibrium methods, and diffusion models. Annealing methods provide appealing polynomial scaling and exactness guarantees, while diffusion models use a simple and efficient score-matching training procedure. This work explores a method combining these benefits, first training an energy-based diffusion model using score matching, then sampling using either stochastic normalizing flows (SNFs) or flow-transformed replica exchange (T-REX). The choice of sampling method can be made after training the diffusion model; the theoretical properties of SNFs and T-REX and their relative trade-offs are discussed. Numerical results are presented for scalar field theory and the O(3) non-linear sigma model, using a class of local-footprint energy models with efficient gradient and divergence calculations. Sampling from the trained models follows predicted exactness and scaling properties, and significantly improves over sampling methods without a learned component.

</details>

# gr-qc [[Back]](#toc)

### [86] [Observational constraints on a non-minimally coupled scalar field in non-metricity gravity with an extra degree of freedom](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33146)
*Saddam Hussain,Ghulam Murtaza,Avik De*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores non-minimally coupled scalar fields in non-metricity gravity with a non-coincident connection, revealing an extra dynamical degree of freedom that enables viable cosmic evolution—including extended matter domination and stable late-time de Sitter expansion—consistent with DESI BAO, SNe Ia, and cosmic chronometer data, and confirmed by autonomous dynamical-system analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To examine cosmological implications of non-metricity gravity beyond the coincident gauge, specifically whether a non-trivial connection can yield physically viable background evolution (e.g., proper matter domination and late-time acceleration) without fine-tuning.

Method: Formulates gravitational action with non-minimal coupling between scalar field and non-metricity; adopts a non-coincident symmetric affine connection in flat FLRW spacetime; derives modified Friedmann equations; performs Markov Chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) fitting against DESI BAO, two SNe Ia compilations, and cosmic chronometers; conducts autonomous dynamical-system analysis to classify critical points and their stability.

Result: Both power-law and exponential coupling models reproduce an extended matter-dominated era and a stable late-time de Sitter attractor; best-fit parameters are statistically consistent with ΛCDM; dynamical analysis confirms a sequence of critical points corresponding to stiff-fluid → transient matter-domination → stable de Sitter, ensuring full thermal history compatibility.

Conclusion: Non-metricity gravity with non-coincident connection and non-minimally coupled scalar field provides a theoretically consistent and observationally viable alternative to ΛCDM, resolving the coincidence problem via dynamical attractor behavior while preserving standard cosmological phases.

Abstract: We investigate a non-minimally coupled scalar field in non-metricity gravity, adopting a non-trivial connection class in spatially flat FLRW spacetimes. In contrast to the coincident-gauge choice, this connection class activates an additional dynamical degree of freedom (DoF) that modifies the background evolution. We study two representative models, defined by power-law and exponential coupling functions with the exponential potential for scalar field. Confronting both models with recent combined datasets - DESI BAO, two independent supernova compilations, and cosmic chronometers - we find that each reproduces an extended matter-dominated era followed by a stable late-time de-Sitter attractor, with inferred parameters consistent with the standard $\Lambda$CDM model. A complementary autonomous dynamical-system analysis identifies a sequence of critical points describing successive stiff-fluid, transient matter-dominated, and stable de-Sitter phases, ensuring a viable thermal history of the universe. The observational and dynamical results are mutually consistent and support the cosmological viability of the proposed models.

</details>


### [87] [A unified dark sector from a single canonical scalar field in Bohmian Wheeler-DeWitt cosmology with a cuscuton-inspired potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33187)
*Anant Badal,Rikpratik Sengupta,Kaushik Bhattacharya*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper quantizes a FLRW minisuperspace with a cuscuton-inspired hyperbolic scalar potential, uses the de Broglie-Bohm interpretation of the Wheeler-DeWitt wave function to extract cosmological histories, and shows that for large mass-to-Hubble ratio κ ≫ 1, the model yields an effective single-field description combining dust-like quantum zero-point energy and de Sitter vacuum—matching ΛCDM distance relations at O(1/κ) accuracy without data fitting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a quantum cosmological model where a scalar field with a cuscuton-type superpotential generates a hyperbolic potential supporting both classical condensate dynamics and quantum vacuum/dust-like contributions, bridging quantum gravity effects with late-time cosmology without ad hoc dark sector components.

Method: Canonical quantization of a spatially flat FLRW minisuperspace with a canonical scalar field governed by a cuscuton-inspired hyperbolic potential; solution of the Wheeler-DeWitt equation analytically (adiabatic Gaussian approximation) and numerically; extraction of Bohmian trajectories; computation of averaged quantum densities, Hubble evolution, and distance measures (e.g., D_V).

Result: For κ ≫ 1: (i) adiabatic Gaussian WKB solution yields classical oscillating condensate (a^{-3} redshift), de Sitter vacuum, and quantum zero-point density equal to (ℏω/2)/V_cell — dust-like; (ii) full numerical WDW solutions confirm zero-point law at 10^{-3} level, κ^{-1}-convergence of quantum corrections to H_0, and validity of dust-plus-vacuum background; (iii) predicted D_V deviates from ΛCDM by 1.3/κ; w = -1 for dark energy component by construction.

Conclusion: The cuscuton-generalized hyperbolic potential enables a self-consistent, single-field quantum cosmological model whose semiclassical limit reproduces key ΛCDM phenomenology—including dust-like quantum energy and exact cosmological constant—without empirical tuning, suggesting quantum gravitational origins for apparent dark components.

Abstract: We quantize a spatially flat FLRW minisuperspace containing one canonical scalar field with the hyperbolic potential generated by a cuscuton-type superpotential, and extract cosmological histories from the Wheeler-DeWitt wave function in the de Broglie-Bohm interpretation. For the canonical potential parameter, related to the original cuscuton couplings, below an explicit critical value the potential has a unique nondegenerate minimum with positive height, and the ratio $\kappa$ of the scalar mass to the de Sitter Hubble rate at the minimum diverges as $\kappa^2\simeq 3b/\delta$, where $\delta$ is the relative distance to criticality. For the original hyperbolic potential, $\kappa\leq 3/\sqrt{2}$, whereas its cuscuton-inspired generalization allows a large hierarchy between scalar oscillation and expansion rates. For $\kappa\gg1$, small excitation and sufficiently large cell action, the reduced wave equation has an adiabatic Gaussian solution describing a de Sitter vacuum, a classical oscillating condensate whose averaged excitation density redshifts as $a^{-3}$, and, at the packet centre, a leading quantum density equal to the zero-point energy of the effective homogeneous oscillator divided by the physical cell volume, which is also dust-like. Full numerical solutions of the Wheeler-DeWitt equation support the averaged zero-point law at the reported $10^{-3}$ level, reproduce its dependence on the position of the Bohmian particle inside the packet, show that quantum corrections to the present Hubble rate converge as the inverse of the cell action, and support a single-field dust-plus-vacuum description at the homogeneous background level when $\kappa\gg1$. The classical histories approach the $\Lambda$CDM distance relations with a deviation of $1.3/\kappa$ in $D_V$. The dark energy component has $w=-1$ by construction, and the model is not fitted to data.

</details>


### [88] [Black holes from a nonlocal equation of state: horizon geometry, singularity structure and thermodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33222)
*Hishan Farfán-Bachiloglu,C. R. Muniz,Francisco Tello-Ortiz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives a Yukawa-type density profile from a nonlocal equation of state for anisotropic fluid in Kerr–Schild geometry, yielding black hole solutions with finite-range 'hair', exact horizon classification, strong-curvature singularities, integrable curvature scalars at origin for vanishing central mass, and rich thermodynamic structure including Smarr relation, first law extension, and topological defect transitions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how nonlocal equations of state for anisotropic fluids in general relativity lead to physically viable, horizon-structured black hole solutions with controlled singularity behavior and well-defined thermodynamics—bypassing ad hoc matter models like nonlinear electrodynamics.

Method: Analytical derivation in static, spherically symmetric Kerr–Schild spacetimes; exact integration of Einstein equations under a nonlocal fluid closure; horizon analysis via metric root structure; singularity strength assessed via Królak and Tipler criteria; curvature integrability tested using invariant volume element; thermodynamic quantities (surface gravity, Smarr, first law, heat capacity) computed analytically using Lambert W function; topological defect analysis via Hawking temperature extremization.

Result: Yukawa density profile uniquely selected; four distinct horizon types classified (horizonless, Schwarzschild-like, two-horizon with Cauchy horizon, extremal); inner surface gravity > outer throughout two-horizon regime; global monopole/Kantowski–Sachs limits for zero central mass; strong but locally integrable singularity; power-law generalization yields regular de Sitter-core black holes via incomplete gamma functions; closed-form Smarr relation, extended first law, Lambert-W–based heat-capacity divergence; thermodynamic topology reveals winding-defect transition at maximal Hawking temperature.

Conclusion: Nonlocal fluid closures generate physically coherent black hole geometries with finite-range hair, robust singularity structure, and rich thermodynamic phase space—including topological defect transitions—providing a compelling alternative to exotic matter models for regular black holes and quantum-gravity-inspired phenomenology.

Abstract: We show that a nonlocal equation of state for an anisotropic fluid reduces to a linear equation for the enclosed matter and uniquely selects a Yukawa-type density profile in a static, spherically symmetric Kerr--Schild geometry. The resulting neutral solution has finite-range hair, exponentially screened at large distances, with the asymptotic mass as its only conserved charge, and admits an exact horizon classification: horizonless configurations, Schwarzschild-like black holes, two-horizon geometries with a Cauchy horizon, and an extremal configuration whose mass and radius are obtained in closed form. We prove that the magnitude of the inner surface gravity exceeds that of the outer horizon throughout the two-horizon regime. When the central mass vanishes, the geometry approaches a global monopole in the static branch and a Kantowski--Sachs-type region in the nonstatic branch. The singularity satisfies Królak's strong-curvature criterion and is Tipler-strong along non-radial geodesics in the spacelike branch. For vanishing central mass, the Ricci scalar and the square root of the Kretschmann scalar are locally integrable with respect to the invariant spacetime volume, despite diverging at the origin. A power-law generalization of the nonlocal closure yields solutions expressed through incomplete gamma functions, interpolating between the singular core and a regular black hole with a de~Sitter core, without invoking nonlinear electrodynamics. We obtain closed-form surface gravities, an extended first-law identity and a Smarr relation, and locate the heat-capacity divergence using the lower branch of the Lambert function. Finally, thermodynamic topology distinguishes a Schwarzschild-like regime with one defect from an extremal regime with two opposite-winding defects that merge at the maximum Hawking temperature.

</details>


### [89] [Hyperboloidal Initial Data with Prescribed Asymptotic Charges](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33266)
*Károly Csukás,János Östör,István Rácz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Introduces a novel method to construct hyperboloidal initial data with analytic control over asymptotic charges (e.g., Bondi energy/momentum) before solving Einstein constraints, using parabolic-hyperbolic formulation and Fuchsian-inspired spectral solver; demonstrates it by building spinning black hole data with prescribed $E_B = 2$, $J_B = 1/2$ from Schwarzschild seed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable direct analytic control over asymptotic gravitational charges—particularly the infinite towers of Bondi-type quantities—in hyperboloidal initial data, circumventing indirect or numerical post-processing approaches.

Method: Combines the parabolic-hyperbolic formulation of the Einstein constraint equations with a Fuchsian-analysis-inspired spectral solver; treats asymptotic degrees of freedom as free parameters that explicitly determine the asymptotic expansion coefficients governing Bondi energy, angular momentum, and higher-order charges.

Result: Explicit construction of hyperboloidal initial data for a spinning black hole with precisely prescribed Bondi energy $E_B = 2$ and angular momentum $J_B = 1/2$, derived from Schwarzschild seed data ($M = 1$) via controlled asymptotic deformation.

Conclusion: The method provides a robust, analytic framework for engineering hyperboloidal initial data with pre-specified asymptotic physical charges—enabling rigorous study of gravitational radiation and asymptotic symmetry properties in numerical relativity and mathematical GR.

Abstract: A novel method is introduced for constructing hyperboloidal initial data with direct analytic control over asymptotic charges prior to solving the Einstein constraint equations. Using the parabolic-hyperbolic formulation of the constraints together with a Fuchsian-analysis-inspired spectral solver, the asymptotic degrees of freedom, which determine infinite towers of asymptotic charges, including the Bondi energy and angular momentum, are used to prescribe the asymptotic behavior of solutions. As a proof of concept, we explicitly construct hyperboloidal initial data for a spinning black hole with Bondi energy $E_B = 2$ and angular momentum $J_B = 1/2$, starting from seed data induced on a hyperboloidal slice of the non-rotating Schwarzschild spacetime with mass $M=1$.

</details>


### [90] [Impact of nonlinear electrodynamics on quasinormal modes of rotating black strings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33309)
*F. S. Mirekhtiary,I. Sakalli*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes quasinormal modes of rotating anti-de Sitter black strings coupled to a power-Maxwell field with negative exponent s; it identifies charge- and rotation-induced spectral features—including an exceptional point at critical charge, hypergeometric spectrum at the branch endpoint, modified light-cone scaling, and absence of superradiance—validated via multiple numerical methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how charge (via power-Maxwell field with negative exponent s) and rotation affect the quasinormal mode spectrum of AdS black strings, especially deviations from neutral or static limits and validity of near-horizon approximations.

Method: Computes quasinormal frequencies using three complementary techniques: Horowitz-Hubeny series expansion, Chebyshev collocation, and time-domain evolution; all implemented in two independent codes and cross-verified against the planar Schwarzschild-AdS limit.

Result: Identifies (i) an exceptional point at reduced charge q̂_c ≈ 0.72626 where the least-damped mode pair coalesces on the imaginary axis, leading to purely damped ringdown; (ii) exact hypergeometric spectrum at the branch endpoint; (iii) modified asymptotic mode dispersion k^{1/(1−4s)} replacing k^{−1/5} for neutral branes; (iv) rotation extends the exceptional point into a line in (charge, rotation) space and adds a purely damped axisymmetric tower; (v) no superradiant instability occurs; (vi) prior near-horizon estimates fail to capture these effects.

Conclusion: Charge and rotation induce rich, non-perturbative structure in the quasinormal spectrum of AdS black strings—particularly exceptional points, exact solvability, and altered asymptotics—invalidating simplified approximations and highlighting the necessity of full numerical relativity methods.

Abstract: We study massless scalar perturbations of rotating anti-de Sitter black strings sourced by a power-Maxwell field with negative exponent $s$. The charge enters the metric function as $-Qr^{p}$ with $p=2/(1-2s)$, and the case $s=-1/2$ gives a linear term with an algebraic horizon. Since rotation is a local boost of the static string, the rotating spectrum follows exactly from the static one evaluated at a frequency-dependent momentum. We compute the spectrum with the Horowitz-Hubeny series, Chebyshev collocation and time-domain evolution, each implemented in two independent codes and checked against the planar Schwarzschild-AdS limit. The charge acts in two ways. At the reduced charge $\hat q_c=0.72626$ the least damped pair of modes meets on the imaginary axis, so more strongly charged strings ring down without oscillation, and at the endpoint of the branch the spectrum is exactly hypergeometric. At large transverse momentum the distance of the modes from the light cone grows as $k^{1/(1-4s)}$, replacing the $k^{-1/5}$ law of the neutral brane. Rotation continues the exceptional point into a line in the plane of charge and rotation, adds a purely damped axisymmetric tower, and never produces superradiant growth. A near-horizon estimate used earlier for this geometry misses all of these effects.

</details>


### [91] [Neutron Star--Primordial Black Hole Binaries: Dynamical Tides, Resonance Locking, and Gravitational Waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33314)
*Isaac Legred,Nicolás Yunes*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores how dynamical tides between a neutron star and a low-mass primordial black hole—unlike typical compact binaries—can cause accelerated inspiral or outspiral, mediated by gravitational-wave emission when neutron star oscillation modes remain resonantly locked to the orbit; such effects may yield detectable signatures in pulsar timing or gravitational-wave data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand anomalous orbital evolution in neutron star–primordial black hole binaries, where standard binary inspiral models fail due to resonant dynamical tides—similar to planet-moon systems—and to assess potential observational consequences.

Method: Analytical modeling of resonant tidal coupling between a neutron star's oscillation modes and the orbital motion of a low-mass primordial black hole, incorporating gravitational-wave backreaction while enforcing mode-orbit resonance locking.

Result: Dynamical tides can either accelerate inspiral or drive outspiral depending on orbital configuration and spin-orbit alignment; gravitational-wave emission sustains resonance locking, leading to distinctive, potentially observable orbital phase evolution.

Conclusion: Resonant dynamical tides in neutron star–primordial black hole binaries produce non-standard orbital evolution with unique gravitational-wave and pulsar-timing signatures, offering a new probe for both exotic compact objects and neutron star internal physics.

Abstract: The orbital evolution of a neutron star and a much lower-mass primordial black hole can differ strikingly from that of typical compact binaries. Dynamical tides, analogous to those in the Solar System's giant planet-moon systems, can accelerate inspiral or induce outspiral, depending on the orbital configuration. We show how gravitational-wave emission mediates this evolution when neutron star modes remain locked in resonance with the orbit. These effects may leave observable signatures in pulsar timing or gravitational waves.

</details>


### [92] [Constraining Ultralight Dark Matter in Dynamical Dark Energy Cosmologies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33332)
*Muhammad Awais,Hafiz Inam Ullah,Rafael C. Nunes,Paula S. Ferreira,Tonatiuh Matos*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies scalar-field dark matter (SFDM) within a dynamical dark energy cosmology (beyond ΛCDM), constraining it with CMB (Planck), BAO (DESI DR2), and SNe Ia (Pantheon+) data; it finds SFDM suppresses small-scale matter fluctuations in a mass-dependent way, yields a lower bound log₁₀(m_ϕ/eV) > −24.04, and does not change the evidence for evolving (including phantom) dark energy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend previous SFDM analyses—previously limited to ΛCDM—to a more general dynamical dark energy framework, and assess whether SFDM alters inferences about dark energy evolution or its observational evidence.

Method: Consistent implementation of SFDM in a dynamical dark energy background and linear perturbation theory; Bayesian parameter inference using Planck CMB, DESI DR2 BAO, and Pantheon+ SNe Ia data.

Result: Lighter SFDM masses cause stronger scale-dependent suppression of matter power and larger CMB temperature spectrum modifications; heavier masses recover CDM behavior; joint analysis gives 95% lower limit log₁₀(m_ϕ/eV) > −24.04; SFDM substitution does not qualitatively affect dynamical dark energy constraints or evidence for low-redshift phantom crossing.

Conclusion: SFDM remains viable in dynamical dark energy cosmologies, but current data only weakly constrain its mass; its introduction does not undermine or alter the inferred dynamics or phantom nature of dark energy.

Abstract: We investigate the cosmological implications of scalar-field dark matter (SFDM) in a dynamical dark energy framework, extending previous analyses that considered SFDM within the standard $\Lambda$CDM cosmological model. We consistently implement the scenario at both the background and linear perturbation levels. We perform Bayesian parameter inference using Planck CMB measurements, DESI DR2 baryon acoustic oscillation (BAO) data, and Pantheon+ Type Ia supernovae to constrain the model parameter space. Our theoretical predictions show that lighter scalar-field masses produce increasingly strong scale-dependent suppression of matter fluctuations and more pronounced modifications to the CMB temperature power spectrum, whereas heavier masses progressively approach the cold dark matter limit. For the SFDM mass, we obtain a one-sided 95\% lower limit of $\log_{10}(m_\phi/{\rm eV})>-24.04$ from our main joint analysis. We find that replacing the standard cold dark matter fluid with SFDM does not qualitatively alter the constraints on dynamical dark energy or the evidence for its evolution, including a phantom crossing at low redshifts.

</details>


### [93] [Accurate pulsar timing array likelihoods: better low-rank covariance for gravitational wave background](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33444)
*Heling Deng,Ken D. Olum,Rutger van Haasteren*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Proposes a non-diagonal, low-rank covariance construction for pulsar timing array stochastic gravitational wave background modeling that improves spectral fidelity and inference accuracy without increasing computational cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional Fourier-basis modeling with diagonal covariance at ~0.1 yr⁻¹ spacing is computationally efficient but insensitive to sub-bin spectral features, leading to biased parameter inference.

Method: Constructs an optimized low-rank covariance matrix in a basis domain by maximizing the ensemble-averaged log-likelihood over realizations from the true spectral model; the resulting covariance is generally non-diagonal and captures full spectral structure.

Result: Demonstrated on two mock datasets: improved likelihood accuracy, more faithful recovery of fine-scale spectral features, and computational cost comparable to the conventional Fourier-diagonal approach.

Conclusion: The proposed ensemble-averaged log-likelihood optimization yields a superior low-rank stochastic process representation for gravitational wave background inference in pulsar timing arrays, balancing accuracy and efficiency.

Abstract: In pulsar timing array analyses, the stochastic process describing the gravitational wave background is typically modeled using a Fourier basis at discrete frequencies with an interval $\sim 0.1~\mathrm{yr}^{-1}$, with a diagonal Fourier-domain covariance determined by the power spectral density at the corresponding frequencies. Although this rank-reduced modeling significantly accelerates likelihood evaluation and provides a good approximation in many scenarios, it can lead to biased parameter inference because it is insensitive to spectral features on scales smaller than $\sim 0.1~\mathrm{yr}^{-1}$. We introduce a better construction of the low-rank covariance for low-frequency processes by maximizing the log-likelihood averaged over an ensemble of realizations drawn from the true model likelihood. In this approach, the basis-domain covariance matrix is sensitive to the entire model spectrum and is generally non-diagonal. Using two mock datasets, we demonstrate improved likelihood accuracy and more faithful recovery of spectral features compared to the conventional Fourier-diagonal model. Benchmark tests show that this new model requires a computational cost comparable to that of the conventional approach.

</details>


### [94] [Acoustic Schwarzschild-like black hole and analogous Hawking radiation in Einstein-bumblebee gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33481)
*Chikun Ding,Yuebing Zhou,Yu Shi,Yuehua Xiao,Jiliang Jing*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs an acoustic Schwarzschild-like black hole in Einstein-bumblebee theory using Gross-Pitaevskii theory, identifies gravitational and acoustic (dumb) horizons, computes Hawking and acoustic temperatures, analyzes grey-body factors and energy emission spectra for scalar particles, and highlights an experimentally distinguishable opposite dependence of emission rate on tuning parameter ξ and Lorentz-violating coupling ℓ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore analog Hawking radiation in a Lorentz-violating gravity framework (Einstein-bumblebee theory) and identify observable signatures—particularly the opposing parametric dependencies of emission rate on ξ and ℓ—that could benchmark quantum-gravitational or Lorentz-violating effects against astrophysical black hole observations.

Method: Constructs an acoustic black hole metric via Gross-Pitaevskii theory in a Schwarzschild-like bumblebee spacetime; analytically derives gravitational and acoustic horizons; computes Hawking temperature Th and acoustic temperature Tac; solves scalar wave equation to obtain grey-body factor Γ(ω) and energy emission rate d²E/dωdt; performs asymptotic and parametric analysis of Γ(ω) and emission spectrum.

Result: Two types of horizons emerge: gravitational horizon rh = 2M and acoustic horizons rac−, rac dependent on ξ and ℓ; Th depends only on ℓ, while Tac depends on both ℓ and ξ, with maximum Tacm = Th/8; grey-body factor increases with ξ but decreases with ℓ at low ω, approaching 1 at high ω; energy emission rate peaks at ωm and exhibits opposite parametric dependence—increasing with ξ, decreasing with ℓ.

Conclusion: The acoustic dumb hole in Einstein-bumblebee theory provides a testable analog system where the anti-correlated response of the energy emission rate to ξ and ℓ serves as a distinctive observational signature for Lorentz violation, offering a pathway to constrain bumblebee parameters using analog or astrophysical black hole data.

Abstract: We derive an acoustic Schwarzschild-like black hole and study its analogous Hawking radiation in Einstein-bumblebee theory by using Gross-Pitaevskii theory in the background of Schwarzschild-like spacetime. This black hole have two kinds of event horizons: one is the gravitational horizon $r_h=2M$(black hole horizon), the others are the internal and external acoustic horizons $2M<r_{ac-}\leq4M,\;r_{ac}\geq4M$(dumb hole horizon) which depend on the tuning parameter $\xi$ and Lorentz violation coupling constant $\ell$. The corresponding Hawking temperature $T_h$ depends only on $\ell$, however $T_{ac}$ depends on $\ell$ and $\xi$. The dumb hole temperature $T_{ac}$ has a maximum $T_{acm}=T_h/8$. When considering the scalar particles radiating from the dumb hole, we find that in low frequency, the grey-body factor increases with the tuning parameter $\xi$, but decreases with $\ell$. In the high frequency, it approaches to $1$. Its energy emission rate is similar to the Planck's black spectrum where there has a maximum at a given peak frequency $\omega_m$. The energy emission rate also increases with the tuning parameter $\xi$, but decreases with $\ell$. This opposite dependence relationship is the most experimentally valuable feature and can directly benchmark against observations of real astrophysical black holes.

</details>


### [95] [Gravitational Waves from Ringing Dyonic Black Holes and Estimation of Parameters in Einstein-Bumblebee-Maxwell Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33569)
*Sikha Gogoi,Umananda Dev Goswami*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies quasinormal modes (QNMs) of dyonic Reissner-Nordström-like black holes in Einstein-bumblebee-Maxwell gravity, using 6th-order Padé-averaged WKB and time-domain simulations; it finds detectable QNM signatures for supermassive black holes with LISA via high SNR at low redshifts, and constrains BH mass and Lorentz-violating bumblebee parameter via Fisher analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe Lorentz violation and modified electrodynamics in strong-field gravity by analyzing QNMs of dyonic black holes in Einstein-bumblebee-Maxwell theory — a framework incorporating spontaneous Lorentz symmetry breaking via the bumblebee field coupled to gravity and Maxwell fields.

Method: Computes QNM frequencies for scalar and gravitational perturbations using the 6th-order Padé-averaged WKB approximation; validates results via time-domain integration of perturbation equations; performs Fisher matrix analysis to forecast parameter estimation accuracy for LISA observations of ringdown signals from supermassive black holes.

Result: QNM frequencies and damping rates depend sensitively on the bumblebee Lorentz-violating parameter and BH charge/mass ratio; time-domain simulations confirm dominant QNMs; LISA can achieve high SNR (>10) for $M \sim 10^6$–$10^8\,M_\odot$ BHs at $z \lesssim 1$; Fisher analysis shows $M$ and bumblebee parameter are simultaneously well-constrained for $M \geq 10^6\,M_\odot$ at $D_L = 3$ Gpc.

Conclusion: Quasinormal modes serve as clean probes of Lorentz violation in Einstein-bumblebee-Maxwell gravity; LISA will enable stringent tests of this theory through ringdown spectroscopy of supermassive black holes, especially for masses $\gtrsim 10^6\,M_\odot$ at cosmological distances.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the quasinormal mode (QNM) frequencies of dyonic Reissner-Nordström (RN)-like black holes associated with scalar and gravitational perturbations in the framework of Einstein-bumblebee-Maxwell gravity theory. The QNMs are calculated using the 6th-order Padé averaged WKB approximation method. We analyse the dependence of oscillation frequencies and damping rate of the BHs for the free model parameters. To further verify the QNM spectrum, we study the time evolution of both the scalar and gravitational perturbations around the BHs and extract the dominant QNM from the resulting ringdown signal. Moreover, we try to estimate the BH parameters using Fisher analysis method for the future gravitational wave detector named Laser Interferometer Space Antenna (LISA). We found that for supermassive BHs with mass around $10^6$ -- $10^8 M_\odot$ and at lower redshifts, the signal-to-noise ratio for the detection of a QNM signal by the LISA is high. Further, for a representative source at a luminosity distance of $3$ Gpc, the BH mass at the detector reference frame and the Lorentz violating parameter are well constrained for supermassive BHs with mass $\ge 10^6.$

</details>


### [96] [A no-go theorem for closed timelike curves in globally null-foliated spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33706)
*Hongsheng Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new causality criterion based on the existence of a globally defined null gradient function, proving that such a function forbids closed timelike curves (CTCs); it applies this to Kundt and Robinson–Trautman spacetimes and reinterprets classic CTC constructions in terms of null-to-timelike direction transitions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a new, elementary family of criteria for ruling out closed timelike curves—distinct from time function, energy condition, and topological criteria—based solely on a scalar null eikonal equation, with broader applicability where classical theorems fail.

Method: The authors prove that a globally defined function with everywhere-null gradient cannot coexist with a closed timelike curve, using the algebraic fact that a null covector cannot be orthogonal to a timelike vector; they apply this criterion to Kundt and Robinson–Trautman spacetimes via analysis of the null coordinate's global single-valuedness, and reinterpret Misner/Ori/Ahmed-type CTC constructions geometrically.

Result: A spacetime admitting a globally defined null-gradient function is CTC-free; this condition is logically independent of stable causality; Kundt and Robinson–Trautman spacetimes admit no CTCs where the null coordinate is globally single-valued (regardless of Λ or rotation one-form); Misner/Ori/Ahmed constructions fundamentally rely on a compact null direction becoming timelike, with Λ, expansion, and shear playing no essential role.

Conclusion: The null eikonal criterion provides a powerful, geometrically transparent, and widely applicable tool for excluding CTCs without invoking energy conditions or global topology, unifying and extending prior results while revealing the core geometric mechanism behind known CTC constructions.

Abstract: The study of closed timelike curves has proceeded one spacetime at a time, and the criteria available form three families, the time function criteria, the energy condition criteria, and the topological criteria. We initiate a fourth family of criteria, elementary in form and resting on a single scalar equation rather than any energy condition. We prove that a spacetime admitting a globally defined function whose gradient is everywhere null cannot contain a closed timelike curve, and that the hypothesis is logically independent of stable causality rather than weaker than it. The proof turns on one algebraic fact, that a null covector is never orthogonal to a timelike vector, so the function grows strictly along every timelike curve and cannot close. Because the null eikonal equation alone decides the criterion, we can use it where the classical time function theorem is silent. We establish for the first time that no Kundt spacetime and no Robinson--Trautman spacetime can contain a closed timelike curve on any region where the null coordinate is globally single valued, whatever the cosmological constant and the rotation one form, which settles a question left open for gyratonic waves. We further show that the Misner, Ori and Ahmed constructions reduce to the statement that a compact null direction has turned timelike, and that the cosmological constant, the expansion and the shear are spectators in all of them.

</details>


### [97] [Forecasting Strongly Lensed Binary Black Hole Rates from the Stochastic Gravitational-Wave Background with TianQin](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33751)
*Zheng-Cheng Liang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a framework to forecast strongly lensed binary black hole (BBH) merger detections by ground-based gravitational-wave detectors, using BBH population constraints inferred from the millihertz stochastic gravitational-wave background (SGWB) observed by the space-based TianQin mission.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantitatively link millihertz SGWB measurements—sensitive to the cosmic BBH merger history—with predictions for strongly lensed BBH events detectable on Earth, enabling synergistic multi-band gravitational-wave astrophysics.

Method: Bayesian inference of BBH population parameters from a one-year simulated TianQin SGWB observation; propagation of the resulting posterior through a galaxy-scale strong lensing model incorporating image-level detection and association selection effects; distinction between single-detected-image events and fully associated two-image lens systems.

Result: Median predicted rates: R_event^det = 5.50×10⁻⁴ yr⁻¹ (≥1 detected image), R_sys^det = 2.54×10⁻⁴ yr⁻¹ (associated two-image systems), F_sys^det = 3.35×10⁻⁴ (detectable system fraction); SGWB-informed posteriors narrow uncertainty widths by ~4–6.5% versus prior-only forecasts.

Conclusion: Millihertz SGWB observations with TianQin can meaningfully improve precision in forecasting strongly lensed BBH mergers for ground-based detectors, establishing a robust cross-band statistical connection between cosmological population inference and strong-lensing phenomenology.

Abstract: The millihertz stochastic gravitational-wave background (SGWB) provides a population-level probe of stellar-mass binary black holes (BBHs) and their cosmic merger history. We develop a framework using TianQin alone to propagate SGWB-informed BBH population constraints into forecasts of strongly lensed BBH mergers detectable by ground-based gravitational-wave detectors. The framework distinguishes between lensed events with at least one detected image and lens systems in which both images are detected and successfully associated. Using a one-year mock observation, we infer the BBH population and propagate the resulting posterior through a galaxy-scale lensing model with image-level selection. For the adopted system-magnification threshold, \(\mu_{\rm sys}\ge 2\), the median predicted rate of lensed events with at least one detected image is \(R_{\rm event}^{\rm det}=5.50\times10^{-4}\,\mathrm{yr}^{-1}\), while the median rate of associated two-image lens systems is \(R_{\rm sys}^{\rm det}=2.54\times10^{-4}\,\mathrm{yr}^{-1}\); the median detectable system fraction is \(\mathcal{F}_{\rm sys}^{\rm det}=3.35\times10^{-4}\). Compared with the prior-only propagation, the pooled 16th--84th percentile widths decrease by 6.5\% for the event rate, 6.4\% for the system rate, and 4.0\% for the system fraction. Our work establishes a quantitative link between millihertz SGWB inference and forecasts of strongly lensed BBH mergers for ground-based gravitational-wave observations.

</details>


### [98] [Massive Scalar Quasinormal Modes and Quasibound States of Charged Black Holes with Logarithmic corrections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33883)
*Prosenjit Paul,Rickmoy Samanta*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how logarithmic deformations of the Reissner-Nordström black-hole metric—arising from nonlinear electrodynamics or dark-matter models—affect quasinormal modes (QNMs) and quasibound states (QBSs) of a massive scalar field, using multiple analytical and numerical methods; it finds that scalar mass and coupling enhance binding but reduce lifetime, and establishes QNM-QBS orthogonality via a relativistic bilinear current.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how long-range metric deformations—specifically logarithmic modifications to RN geometry, motivated by nonlinear electrodynamics and phenomenological dark-matter models—affect scalar field dynamics, particularly oscillation, trapping, and decay.

Method: Analytical (first- and sixth-order WKB, global matching, Gamow tunneling, Padé approximation), semi-analytical (generalized Leaver method adapted to logarithmic geometry), and numerical (time-domain evolution); includes horizon regularization and complex radial contour integration for orthogonality verification via relativistic bilinear current.

Result: Small scalar mass shifts and raises the effective potential barrier, reducing curvature and damping; all QNM methods agree within validity ranges; global matching and Leaver agree closely for QBSs; Padé approximates binding energy with ~0.04% error; Gamow underestimates damping by ~25%; increased scalar mass or coupling strengthens binding but shortens QBS lifetime due to enhanced horizon absorption; QNM-QBS orthogonality is numerically verified.

Conclusion: Logarithmic long-range deformations significantly alter scalar field spectral structure: they weaken QNM damping at fixed multipole/overtone, enable tunable trapping in QBSs, and preserve fundamental spectral properties like orthogonality—validating robustness of the relativistic bilinear framework even in non-asymptotically flat geometries.

Abstract: We study how long-range deformations in a black-hole metric affect the oscillation, trapping, and decay of a neutral, minimally coupled massive scalar field. We choose a logarithmic deformation of Reissner-Nordström (RN) sourced by nonlinear electrodynamics. It provides an exact solution of Einstein gravity and admits a finite completion. The same geometry also arises in a phenomenological perfect-fluid dark-matter model. In this setup, we analyze both quasinormal modes (QNMs), which describe relaxation, and quasibound states (QBSs), which describe trapped modes with small horizon absorption. We first show that a small scalar mass moves the potential barrier outward, raises it, and reduces its curvature. First-order WKB then predicts weaker damping at every fixed finite multipole and overtone. We then compare several approaches, namely sixth-order WKB, global matching, a generalized Leaver method (adapted to the logarithmic geometry) and time-domain evolution. All methods yield consistent QNM estimates within their ranges of validity. Global matching and Leaver also agree closely for QBSs. Further analytic estimates with a reference trapped state show that a local Padé approximation gives a binding-energy error of about $0.04\%$, while leading Gamow tunnelling underestimates damping by about $25\%$. Across the sampled QBSs, increasing the scalar mass or nonlinear coupling strengthens binding but shortens the lifetime through greater horizon absorption. Finally, using an appropriate horizon regularization and a common complex radial contour, we demonstrate orthogonality of an independently computed QNM-QBS pair using a suitable relativistic bilinear framework.

</details>


### [99] [Gravitational Radiation Coupled to a real massive scalar Field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33928)
*Yue Ma,Xianghao Wu,Weidong Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the long-term behavior of small-data global solutions to the Einstein--Klein--Gordon system with a positive-mass scalar field in wave gauge, proving an optimal $t^{-1}$ decay rate for the linearized trace of the metric perturbation and establishing a quantitative observability estimate linking initial Klein-Gordon energy to that leading-order coefficient.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the asymptotic stability and decay structure of coupled gravitational and massive scalar field dynamics in general relativity, particularly in wave gauge where gauge-dependent features affect pointwise decay.

Method: Asymptotic analysis of nonlinear hyperbolic PDEs; derivation of sharp pointwise decay rates for metric perturbations using wave gauge constraints and energy estimates; proof of a one-point observability inequality via careful control of the linearized trace component and its relation to initial data.

Result: Optimal $\hat t^{-1} \simeq t^{-1}$ decay rate for the linearized trace of the metric perturbation; quantitative one-point observability estimate bounding initial Klein-Gordon energy by the leading-order coefficient of that trace.

Conclusion: The linearized trace component governs the dominant asymptotic behavior and encodes full information about the initial scalar field energy, confirming structural coherence between gauge choice, decay hierarchy, and observability in the Einstein--Klein--Gordon system.

Abstract: We study the asymptotic behavior of small-data global solutions to the Einstein--Klein--Gordon system with a positive-mass real scalar field in wave gauge. We show that the linearized trace component of the metric perturbation admits a $\hat t^{-1}\simeq t^{-1}$ optimal decreasing rate. Moreover, a quantitative one-point observability estimate is established: the initial Klein-Gordon energy is controlled by this leading order coefficient.

</details>


### [100] [Comment on "Effect of perfect fluid dark matter..." [Phys. Dark Univ. 34 (2021) 100891]](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34019)
*Ming-Hsun Li,Kwei-Chou Yang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This comment critiques the physical interpretation of a black hole spacetime metric used by Shaymatov et al., clarifying that it describes a dual-component system (cold dark matter + phantom field), not a single-component perfect fluid dark matter; misinterpreting it as such violates gravitational requirements for dark matter halos.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To correct the physically inconsistent interpretation of a black hole spacetime metric as a single-component Perfect Fluid Dark Matter (PFDM) model, and to prevent misapplication in extreme astrophysical contexts.

Method: Conceptual and theoretical analysis of the metric's derivation (Li & Yang, 2012), its constituent energy components, and their collective equation of state; comparison with observational constraints on dark matter halo formation.

Result: The metric corresponds to a two-fluid system—cold dark matter and a phantom field—not a unified PFDM; assigning it a total equation of state w_total = −1 contradicts the attractive gravity needed for halo stability.

Conclusion: Using this metric as a PFDM model is invalid; proper interpretation requires distinguishing its dual-component nature and respecting parameter regimes to avoid unphysical astrophysical implications.

Abstract: This Comment clarifies the physical interpretation of the black hole spacetime metric employed by Shaymatov \textit{et al.} [Phys. Dark Univ. 34 (2021) 100891]. We point out that the metric, originally derived in Li and Yang (2012), describes a specific dual-component system consisting of cold dark matter and a phantom field background. Treating this geometry as a single-component ``Perfect Fluid Dark Matter'' (PFDM) with a total equation of state $w_{\mathrm{total}} = -1$ is physically incompatible with the gravitational attraction required for dark matter halos. Furthermore, we clarify the distinct parameter regimes of the original model to prevent potential misapplications in extreme astrophysical scenarios.

</details>


### [101] [Repetitive Magnetic Penrose Process](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34164)
*Ke Wang,Xin-Yun Hu,Xiao-Xiong Zeng*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper generalizes the nonlinear iterative repetitive Penrose process to the magnetic case in Kerr spacetime with a Wald magnetic field, showing that charge accumulation via charged particle decay leads to progressive neutralization of magnetic gain, terminating the magnetic process and limiting efficiency to ≤100%.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the unphysical prediction of >100% energy extraction efficiency in the single magnetic Penrose process by examining its sustainability under realistic, self-consistent electromagnetic feedback in an iterative setting.

Method: Analytical relativistic mechanics in Kerr–Wald spacetime: modeling test-charge particle decay near the horizon, tracking black hole charge evolution via conservation laws, computing monopole voltage dependence on Q, and determining termination condition when magnetic gain vanishes.

Result: The iterative magnetic Penrose process terminates because black hole charge asymptotically approaches Q ≈ Q_Wald/2, causing the Wald monopole voltage to vanish; consequently, magnetic energy extraction ceases and the process reverts to the standard mechanical (neutral) Penrose process with η ≤ 100%.

Conclusion: Magnetic Penrose processes cannot sustain super-efficient energy extraction over multiple iterations due to intrinsic charging feedback — a fundamental self-limiting mechanism arising from charge conservation and Wald field structure.

Abstract: The nonlinear iterative repetitive Penrose process established recently by Ruffini et al.\ is generalized to the magnetic Penrose process. The spacetime background is the Kerr metric, the external magnetic field is described by the Wald solution, the electromagnetic field is treated as a test field, and the charge is treated as a test charge. We analyze the optimal conditions for maximum energy extraction on the equatorial plane and find that under these conditions the core mechanism of the iterative process is charging feedback. After charging, the black hole charge is approximately half of the Wald charge. When the neutral parent particle decays, charge conservation forces the infalling fragment to carry charge while the other fragment carries the opposite charge, so the black hole charge accumulates monotonically. The monopole voltage of the Wald solution decreases linearly with the charge $Q$ and crosses zero to reverse sign at a certain point, so the magnetic gain is gradually neutralized by the charge. The repetitive magnetic Penrose process is therefore forced to terminate and eventually falls back to the mechanical Penrose process. The energy extraction efficiency $>100\%$ predicted by the single magnetic Penrose process will therefore be prevented.

</details>


### [102] [Finite-Distance Imaging of Relativistic Equilibrium Tori around Genuine Kerr--Bertotti--Robinson Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34331)
*Hao-Peng Yan,Xiang-Qian Li,Xiao-Jun Yue*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes finite-distance images of pressure-supported tori around neutral Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson black holes using GR ray tracing with gray emission/absorption, analyzing how magnetic field strength, torus filling, viewing inclination, self-absorption, and black hole spin affect brightness, size, and asymmetry of the images.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how equilibrium structure, photon propagation, and radiative transfer jointly shape observable properties (brightness, size, asymmetry) of magnetized accretion tori around exact black hole solutions beyond Kerr—specifically electrically neutral Kerr–Bertotti–Robinson spacetimes.

Method: General relativistic ray tracing in stationary, axisymmetric Kerr–Bertotti–Robinson spacetime; gray (frequency-independent) radiative transfer with emission and absorption; torus defined by constant-specific-angular-momentum equilibrium free surface; observer fixed at radius 50M; parameter sweeps over external magnetic field strength, torus filling, viewing inclination, optical depth, and black hole spin (a = 0.5 to 0.95).

Result: Integrated brightness and apparent size increase strongly with external magnetic field; brightness rises 5.9× with higher filling but image size grows only 10%; lateral brightness contrast increases with inclination; stronger self-absorption coexists with higher absolute brightness due to coupled scaling; retrograde torus dims ~7× when spin increases from 0.5 to 0.95, while prograde changes only ~2% — revealing distinct spin-dependent equilibrium and radiative responses.

Conclusion: The finite-distance image morphology of magnetized tori is governed by a nontrivial interplay among equilibrium boundary geometry, frame-dragging–modulated fluid motion, and position- and orientation-dependent optical depth — not reducible to simple scaling laws or Kerr-only intuition.

Abstract: We calculate finite-distance images of stationary, pressure-supported tori around genuine, electrically neutral Kerr--Bertotti--Robinson black holes using general relativistic ray tracing with gray emission and absorption. The emitting volume is bounded by a constant-specific-angular-momentum equilibrium free surface, and the observer is placed at a radius of $50M$. Along the investigated external-field sequence, integrated brightness and apparent size both increase strongly as the field modifies the equilibrium source and photon propagation. Higher torus filling has a different effect: in a representative sequence, brightness grows by a factor of $5.9$ while the intensity-weighted image size increases by only $10\%$. Increasing viewing inclination enhances lateral brightness contrast. Stronger self-absorption can coexist with higher absolute brightness because emission and absorption scale together in the adopted gray prescription. At common pressure center and filling, increasing the dimensionless spin from $0.5$ to $0.95$ dims the retrograde sequence by a factor of about $7$ as its equilibrium contracts, whereas prograde brightness changes by only $2\%$. These contrasting responses show how the equilibrium boundary, fluid motion, and optical depth jointly shape the brightness, scale, and asymmetry of finite-distance torus images.

</details>


### [103] [Topological analysis of novel charged-AdS black hole solutions embedded in dark matter halos with quintessence field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34334)
*Muhammad Yasir,Hafiza Bushra Mujahid,Faisal Javed,Shahid Qaisar*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies a charged AdS black hole embedded in a NGC 4649-type dark matter halo and a quintessence field, deriving exact solutions to modified Einstein equations and analyzing horizon structure, asymptotics, and thermodynamics using topological (Duan-φ-mapping) and generalized free energy methods; it finds that dark matter enhances gravity, quintessence induces repulsion and modifies asymptotics, and the quintessence equation-of-state parameter ω_q critically governs phase structure and stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how realistic astrophysical environments—specifically galactic-scale dark matter halos (NGC 4649-type) and dynamical quintessence fields—affect the geometry, horizon structure, and thermodynamic phase behavior of charged AdS black holes, beyond standard vacuum or cosmological-constant-only models.

Method: Solves modified Einstein field equations incorporating electric charge, negative cosmological constant, spherically symmetric NGC 4649 dark matter density profile, and quintessence with variable equation of state ω_q( r); analyzes metric function and horizon equations; applies Duan-φ-mapping topological theory and generalized free energy formalism to derive thermodynamic quantities, topological charge, critical points, and stability criteria.

Result: Dark matter strengthens gravitational attraction (reducing horizon radii), while quintessence introduces repulsion and alters asymptotic flatness/AdS character; thermodynamic analysis reveals ω_q-dependent critical points, phase transitions, and topological stability thresholds; fractional shifts in ω_q induce systematic, non-linear changes in critical temperature, specific heat divergence, and ensemble stability.
