Conclusion: The interplay between dark matter halos and quintessence fundamentally reshapes both the geometric and thermodynamic landscape of charged AdS black holes; quintessence—not just its presence but its equation-of-state parameterization—is a decisive control parameter for critical phenomena and topological stability, suggesting that cosmological environment must be incorporated in black hole thermodynamics for physical realism.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the properties of a charged anti-de Sitter (AdS) black hole in the presence of a NGC 4649-type dark matter halo and a surrounding quintessence field. Starting from the modified Einstein field equations (EFEs), we construct the corresponding black hole solutions by incorporating the effects of electric charge, cosmological constant, dark matter distribution, and quintessence characterized by a variable equation of state. We analyze the resulting spacetime geometry through the metric function and horizon structure, showing that the dark matter halo strengthens the gravitational attraction, whereas the quintessence field provides a repulsive contribution that substantially alters the asymptotic behavior of the spacetime. Furthermore, the thermodynamic properties are investigated by applying the Duan-\(\phi\)-mapping topological theory together with the generalized free energy method. This framework allows us to examine the topological structure, critical behavior, and stability conditions of the black hole system in a consistent manner. We further study the fractional shifts relative to the reference value of \(\omega_q\) and observe that variations in \(\omega_q\) produce systematic changes which highlight the important role of the quintessence field in determining the phase structure and critical behavior of the black hole system.

</details>


### [104] [Relativistic diffusing particles, entropy and varying cosmological constants](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34341)
*Z. Haba*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores Einstein's equations with a non-conserved energy-momentum tensor, incorporating spacetime-dependent Newton constant G and cosmological term Λ; it links energy-momentum non-conservation to thermodynamic quantities (temperature, entropy) via diffusion of particles, and analyzes barotropic fluids of diffusing massive/massless particles in FRW cosmology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize general relativity by allowing non-conservation of the effective energy-momentum tensor $\tilde{T}^{\mu\nu}$, motivated by physical processes like particle diffusion, and to explore thermodynamic interpretations and cosmological implications of varying $G$ and $\Lambda$.

Method: Covariant analysis of modified Einstein equations with $\tilde{T}^{\mu\nu} = 8\pi G T^{\mu\nu} - g^{\mu\nu}\Lambda + \Sigma^{\mu\nu}$; derivation of consistency condition from $\nabla_\mu \tilde{T}^{\mu\nu} = 0$; identification of $\nabla_\mu T^{\mu\nu}$ with first-law thermodynamics; modeling of diffusing particle gases (massive → massless limit) in spatially flat FLRW spacetime; exact solution for relativistic massless gas.

Result: A constraint linking spacetime variation of $G$, $\Lambda$, and $\Sigma^{\mu\nu}$ to energy-momentum non-conservation; expression of $\nabla_\mu T^{\mu\nu}$ in terms of temperature and entropy production; time-dependent barotropic equation of state $P_t = w_t \rho_t$ with $0 < w_t \leq 1/3$ for diffusing massive particles; asymptotic transition to massless gas; explicit solutions for energy density, entropy, and phase-space distribution in the massless relativistic case.

Conclusion: Energy-momentum non-conservation arises naturally from diffusion in curved spacetime and is thermodynamically consistent; varying $G$ and $\Lambda$ are dynamically coupled to matter non-conservation; the model provides a physically motivated extension of standard cosmological fluid descriptions, with concrete predictions for evolution of entropy and equation of state in expanding universes.

Abstract: We discuss Einstein equations with a non-conserved energy-momentum $T^{\mu\nu}$. The energy-momentum tensor $\tilde{T}^{\mu\nu}$ on the right hand side of Einstein equations is expressed in the form $8\pi GT^{\mu\nu}-g^{\mu\nu}\Lambda+\Sigma^{\mu\nu}$ where $G$ is the Newton constant, $\Lambda$ is the cosmological term and $T^{\mu\nu}$ is the energy-momentum of an ideal fluid. The remaining part $\Sigma^{\mu\nu}$ describes the viscosity and a heat flow. We discuss space-time-dependent Newton ``constant" $G$ and the cosmological term $\Lambda$. The covariant conservation law for $\tilde{T}^{\mu\nu}$ imposes a relation between the energy-momentum (non)conservation and the variation of $G$ and $\Lambda$. The covariant derivative $\nabla_{\mu}T^{\mu\nu}$ can be related to the first law of thermodynamics and expressed by the temperature $T$ and the entropy $S$. The energy-momentum non-conservation in our models is a consequence of a diffusion of its constituting particles. We describe a gas of diffusing massive particles in a homogeneous isotropic space-time as a system with a time-dependent linear barotropic equation of state $P_{t}=w_{t}\rho_{t}$ with $0<w_{t}\leq \frac{1}{3}$. The gas of massive diffusing particles in an expanding universe tends in a limit of infinite time to the gas of diffusing massless particles. We obtain results on the energy density, entropy and phase space dstribution in a soluble model of massless relativistic particles in a spatially flat expanding universe.

</details>


### [105] [Geometric criticality and regular quasibound spectra of axial Proca fields in Schwarzschild spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34528)
*Antonio Sousa Ribeiro,Francisco Artur Pinheiro Alves Junior*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the relationship between a geometric critical point in the odd-parity Proca potential and the global quasibound spectrum in Schwarzschild spacetime, deriving exact fold-scaling laws near critical coupling and demonstrating that spectral branches remain non-degenerate despite geometric criticality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how local geometric features (e.g., merging extrema) of the axial Proca potential influence the global quasibound mode spectrum in Schwarzschild black hole spacetimes, particularly near critical mass-coupling where the potential undergoes a fold bifurcation.

Method: Analytical derivation of fold-scaling relations (separation, depth, curvature) near the critical coupling $\mu_c$; continued-fraction computation of axial quasibound frequencies for $\ell=1,2,3$; cross-verification via Riccati shooting; analysis of radial operator’s affine dependence on the reduced parameter $\delta$.

Result: Exact $\delta^{1/2}$, $\delta^{3/2}$, and $\delta^{1/2}$ scalings for stationary-point separation, barrier–well depth, and curvature magnitudes, respectively; regular frequency continuation across $\mu_c$; approximately linear near-critical behavior; confirmation that quasibound branches do not coalesce — i.e., no spectral degeneracy — despite geometric potential criticality.

Conclusion: Geometric criticality of the Proca potential (fold bifurcation at $r=6M$) does not induce spectral degeneracy in the axial quasibound modes; the spectrum remains analytically well-behaved and branch-wise distinct, supporting a conditional simple-root structure in $\delta$.

Abstract: We investigate how a local geometric critical point of the odd-parity Proca potential relates to the global quasibound spectrum in Schwarzschild spacetime. After normalization by $\mu^2$, the axial potential $V_{\rm ax}=(1-2M/r)[\ell(\ell+1)/r^2+\mu^2]$ has the same radial form as the timelike-geodesic effective potential. Its outer minimum and inner maximum merge at $r=6M$, the Schwarzschild innermost stable circular orbit, when $(M\mu_c)^2=\ell(\ell+1)/12$. With $\delta=1-(M\mu)^2/(M\mu_c)^2$, we derive exact fold scalings: stationary-point separation $\propto\delta^{1/2}$, barrier--well depth $\propto\delta^{3/2}$, and curvature magnitudes $\propto\delta^{1/2}$. Continued-fraction calculations for the first three axial quasibound branches with $\ell=1,2,3$, cross-checked for selected modes by independent Riccati shooting, show regular frequency continuation across the threshold and approximately linear near-critical behavior. At fixed radius the radial operator is affine in $\delta$, supporting a conditional simple-root analytic interpretation. The main result is the explicit distinction between geometric potential criticality and spectral degeneracy for the branches examined.

</details>


### [106] [Existence of static axisymmetric solutions to the Einstein equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34555)
*Philippe G. LeFloch,Luc Rozoy*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Constructs smooth static Einstein metrics with positive cosmological constant on a 3-ball minus two disjoint open balls, modeling two small, comparable black holes with controlled horizon areas and separation; solves the reduced Einstein equation via weighted Hölder estimates and obstruction removal by axial position variation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct explicit, geometrically controlled static solutions of the Einstein equations with positive cosmological constant modeling multiple black holes in a compact setting, addressing the analytical challenges posed by shrinking horizons and nonlinear coupling.

Method: Uses weighted Hölder space analysis to control the linearized static Einstein operator near degenerating horizons; constructs an approximate solution by gluing Kottler (Schwarzschild–de Sitter) collars to de Sitter space; removes obstructions via variation of two axial positions; enforces the contracted Bianchi identity and uses an energy estimate for the Killing (axial) vector field to ensure gauge consistency and uniqueness.

Result: Existence of a smooth, axisymmetric static Einstein metric with positive cosmological constant on a compact 3-manifold with three-component boundary (two inner black hole horizons and one outer cosmological horizon); horizon areas scale linearly with individual masses, inter-horizon separation scales with cube root of total mass; solution is unique up to isometry and rescaling of the lapse.

Conclusion: A family of multi-black-hole static solutions in de Sitter space is rigorously constructed under small-mass and scale-separation assumptions; the geometry is fully determined—intrinsic boundary metric of one horizon suffices to fix the solution uniquely up to isometry and lapse scaling.

Abstract: We construct smooth Riemannian metrics and scalar potentials that are invariant under a circle action and satisfy the static Einstein equations with positive cosmological constant. The underlying compact three-manifold is a ball with two disjoint open balls removed. The potential (or lapse function) is positive in the interior and vanishes at the boundary, consisting of three non-degenerate horizons. The areas of the two inner boundary components are controlled by two arbitrary parameters, namely the masses of the black holes, which are taken to be sufficiently small and comparable. Each inner horizon radius scales linearly with the individual mass parameters, while the separation of their reference centers scales as the cube root of the total mass. These two scales are assumed to be sufficiently small at different rates, whereas the cosmological length remains fixed. The main analytical challenge is to control the linearized equation and the nonlinear remainder simultaneously near the shrinking horizons, between the centers and in the outer region. Weighted Hölder estimates quantify the growth of the inverse and show that the error of an approximation joining Kottler collars to de Sitter space is small enough to close a contraction argument. Namely, we solve a reduced elliptic Einstein equation that is endowed with two obstruction tensors; varying the two axial positions removes their coefficients. The contracted Bianchi identity and an energy estimate for an auxiliary vector field (Killing field) introduced by the projection to vanish. Finally, for a fixed cosmological constant, the intrinsic metric of one of the two boundary components is sufficient to determine a static solution with simply connected interior and non-vanishing boundary gradient, up to isometry and multiplication of the potential by a positive constant.

</details>


### [107] [Exact spinning wormholes in five dimensions from Toda integrability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34672)
*Masato Nozawa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a new exact rotating wormhole solution in 5D Einstein-phantom-scalar gravity, generalizing the Ellis-Bronnikov wormhole; it leverages an integrable A₁⊕A₂ Toda structure to obtain closed-form solutions, enforce regularity and asymptotic flatness, and prove key geometric and causal properties—including geodesic completeness, absence of singularities/horizons, uniqueness of the throat, ergosurface existence/uniqueness, and an angular momentum bound—while recovering extremal Myers-Perry black hole geometry in a scalar-vanishing limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct and rigorously characterize a physically viable rotating wormhole solution in higher-dimensional gravity coupled to a phantom scalar field, addressing open questions on regularity, causality, uniqueness, and parameter constraints—beyond static or lower-dimensional analogs.

Method: Imposes cohomogeneity-one symmetry on the 5D Einstein-phantom-scalar system; reduces radial equations to an integrable A₁⊕A₂ Toda system; solves analytically under asymptotically flat and regularity boundary conditions; analyzes causal structure (stable causality, ergosurfaces), geodesic completeness, throat geometry, and limiting behavior via scaling analysis.

Result: Derives a three-parameter family of exact, horizonless, geodesically complete, singularity-free, stably causal rotating wormholes with equal angular momenta; proves uniqueness of the throat and existence/uniqueness of an ergosurface; obtains a sharp upper bound on angular momentum for fixed throat area; shows smooth limit to extremal Myers-Perry black hole exterior when scalar field vanishes.

Conclusion: The integrable Toda structure provides a powerful analytical framework for constructing and characterizing exact rotating wormhole geometries in higher dimensions; the resulting solutions are fully regular and physically well-behaved, offering a concrete realization of traversable wormholes in classical gravity beyond static cases.

Abstract: We construct a new exact solution to the five-dimensional Einstein-phantom-scalar system that describes a rotating wormhole connecting two asymptotically flat regions. The solution represents a rotating generalization of the Ellis-Bronnikov wormhole with equal angular momenta. Under the cohomogeneity-one ansatz, we demonstrate that the radial equations reduce to an integrable $A_1\oplus A_2$ Toda system, from which the general local solution can be obtained in closed form. This integrable structure enables us to implement the regularity and asymptotic-flatness conditions analytically, thereby singling out a three-parameter family of solutions characterized by the mass, the equal angular momenta, and the scalar charge. The resulting spacetimes are geodesically complete, horizonless and stably causal, and contain no curvature singularities. The Toda structure further allows us to prove the uniqueness of the wormhole throat and to derive a sharp upper bound on the angular momentum for a fixed throat area. We also establish the existence and uniqueness of an ergosurface with respect to the stationary frame that is nonrotating at one asymptotic end, while the opposite end rotates relative to this frame. In a suitable scaling limit, the scalar field vanishes and the solution approaches the exterior region of the extremal Myers-Perry black hole with equal angular momenta.

</details>


### [108] [The $C^0$-inextendibility of Kerr spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35012)
*Bobby Eka Gunara*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proves C^0-inextendibility of full Kerr spacetimes (sub-, ex-, and over-extremal, positive mass, nonzero spin) and their coverings, with the obstruction localized at the ring singularity via unbounded proper time in a fixed-endpoint causal homotopy class; the result is conformally and causally robust, and includes sharp perturbation bounds and explicit geodesic behavior near the singularity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish the strongest possible form of causal boundary incompleteness for Kerr spacetimes—specifically, C^0-inextendibility—without relying on global causality assumptions or fine geometric control, thereby confirming that the ring singularity represents an essential, non-removable boundary to spacetime.

Method: Uses causal homotopy theory to define and analyze fixed-endpoint causal curves approaching the ring singularity; establishes unboundedness of proper time along such curves as a C^0-inextendibility criterion; proves stability under arbitrary positive continuous conformal rescalings and causal cone widening; derives coordinate-wise sharp estimates for stationary-axisymmetric perturbations; constructs complete timelike geodesics exhibiting curvature blow-up near the ring.

Result: Full Kerr spacetimes (all mass–angular momentum regimes with M > 0, a ≠ 0) and all connected covering spacetimes are C^0-inextendible; the obstruction is encoded in unbounded proper time within a fixed-endpoint causal homotopy class at the ring singularity; this obstruction is conformally invariant and persists under arbitrary continuous causal cone enlargement; sharp L^∞-type bounds are obtained for perturbations; explicit complete timelike geodesics with curvature divergence near the ring are constructed.

Conclusion: The ring singularity in Kerr spacetime is not merely a coordinate artifact but a genuine C^0-boundary: no continuous extension of the spacetime metric across it is possible, even topologically, and this conclusion holds robustly without global causality conditions or smoothness beyond continuity.

Abstract: We prove the $C^0$-inextendibility of the full Kerr realizations considered in this paper and all their connected covering spacetimes. The result holds for positive mass and nonzero rotation in the subextremal, extremal, and overextremal regimes. The obstruction at the singular ring is expressed by unbounded proper time in a fixed-endpoint causal homotopy class. It persists under every positive continuous conformal rescaling and under continuous widening of the Kerr causal cones, without any global causality assumption. We give sharp bounds in a fixed coordinate norm for stationary and axisymmetric perturbations and describe complete timelike geodesics that encounter arbitrarily large curvature near the ring.

</details>


### [109] [Quasinormal modes of thick branes in $f(R,T)$ gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35096)
*Yu-Peng E,Hai-Long Jia,Chun-Chun Zhu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates tensor perturbations and quasinormal modes of thick branes in $f(R,T)=R+\alpha T$ gravity, deriving a Schrödinger-like equation with a double-barrier effective potential; resonance peaks match real parts of QNMs; parameters $b$ and $\alpha$ control barrier width and height; QNMs are computed via time-domain and direct integration, showing consistency for low-lying modes but increasing numerical difficulty for higher overtones; results indicate $f(R,T)$ modifications leave distinct imprints on braneworld stability and spectra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how $f(R,T)$ gravity modifications—specifically the linear coupling $\alpha T$—affect gravitational stability and dynamics of thick branes via tensor perturbations and quasinormal modes, potentially yielding observational signatures of modified gravity in braneworld scenarios.

Method: Derives the Schrödinger-like equation for tensor (gravitational) perturbations around static thick brane solutions in $f(R,T)=R+\alpha T$ gravity; constructs and analyzes the effective potential (showing double-barrier structure); computes transmission spectra; extracts quasinormal modes using both time-domain evolution and direct integration of the perturbation equation; examines dependence on model parameters $b$ (brane thickness scale) and $\alpha$ (matter–geometry coupling strength).

Result: The effective potential exhibits a tunable double-barrier structure: $b$ controls barrier width, $\alpha$ controls barrier height; resonance peaks in transmission spectra align precisely with real parts of low-lying QNMs; QNM spectra show significant deviation from general relativity predictions; low-lying modes are robustly extracted by both methods, while higher overtones suffer from strong damping and numerical contamination.

Conclusion: Tensor perturbations in $f(R,T)=R+\alpha T$ thick branes yield a parameter-sensitive double-barrier potential and altered QNM spectra, demonstrating that $f(R,T)$ corrections produce physically distinguishable imprints on braneworld stability and oscillation behavior—offering a theoretical pathway to constrain such modified gravity models via gravitational-wave or stability analyses.

Abstract: We study tensor perturbations and quasinormal modes (QNMs) of thick branes in $f(R,T)=R+\alpha T$ gravity. Starting from the background thick brane solutions, we derive the Schrödinger-like equation for gravitational perturbations and construct the corresponding effective potential. The effective potential exhibits a double-barrier structure whose properties depend on the model parameters. We analyze transmission spectra and find good agreement between resonance peaks and the real parts of the quasinormal frequencies. The parameters $b$ and $\alpha$ mainly control the width and height of the potential barriers, respectively. QNMs are extracted using both time-domain evolution and direct integration methods, showing consistent results for low-lying modes. Higher overtones become increasingly difficult to extract due to stronger damping and numerical contamination. Our results indicate that $f(R,T)$ corrections significantly affect both the effective potential and the quasinormal spectrum, providing a possible imprint of modified gravity in braneworld perturbations.

</details>


### [110] [An asymptotically conical regular black hole in four-dimensional nonpolynomial quasitopological gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35248)
*Jia-Zhou Liu,Shao-Wen Wei,Yu-Xiao Liu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs an asymptotically conical regular black hole in 4D nonpolynomial quasitopological gravity, using a nonlinear deformation of a linear reduced function to obtain an exact solution with a de Sitter core and finite curvature invariants; thermodynamics—including entropy with rational and logarithmic contributions—is consistently derived via Hamiltonian and Wald formalisms, and the first law holds for both the deformed and original solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a physically viable, singularity-free black hole solution that is asymptotically conical (rather than asymptotically flat or (A)dS) within a higher-curvature gravity theory—specifically, nonpolynomial quasitopological gravity—while preserving well-defined thermodynamics.

Method: Starts from a linear reduced function yielding an asymptotically conical Schwarzschild-like background; introduces a controlled nonlinear deformation preserving the leading asymptotic cone structure; solves the field equations exactly to obtain a regular black hole with de Sitter core; computes conserved charges, entropy, and thermodynamic potentials using both the Hamiltonian (canonical) and Wald Noether-charge formalisms.

Result: An exact, asymptotically conical, regular black hole solution with finite curvature invariants and a de Sitter core; explicit expressions for mass, temperature, and entropy—including rational and logarithmic corrections—in both models; verification of the first law of black hole thermodynamics in both frameworks.

Conclusion: Asymptotically conical regular black holes are realizable in nonpolynomial quasitopological gravity; their thermodynamics is robust across complementary formalisms, supporting the physical consistency of such exotic asymptotics and singularity resolution mechanisms.

Abstract: In this work, we construct an asymptotically conical regular black hole in four-dimensional nonpolynomial quasitopological gravity. Starting from a linear choice of the reduced function, which yields an asymptotically conical Schwarzschild-like solution, we introduce a nonlinear deformation that preserves the leading asymptotic cone while producing an exact black hole solution with a de Sitter core and finite curvature invariants. We further establish the corresponding black hole thermodynamics for both solutions within the Hamiltonian and Wald frameworks. The entropy contains rational and logarithmic terms in the regular model, and both solutions satisfy the first law of black hole thermodynamics.

</details>


### [111] [Bargmann-invariant witness of the graviton commutator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35253)
*Hiroki Matsui*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes using the phase of a Bargmann invariant in matter-wave interferometry as a signature of quantum gravity, distinguishing it from classical gravitational fields; it shows that while decoherence is insufficient to certify gravity's quantization, the Bargmann phase isolates a gravity-specific commutator term—unaffected by graviton state or Newtonian physics—and though undetectable with current gravity experiments, it is testable in trapped-ion analogs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To find an experimentally accessible, unambiguous signature of quantum gravity that cannot be mimicked by any classical (branchwise-definite) gravitational field, overcoming the limitation that decoherence alone fails to certify gravity's quantization.

Method: Analyzes the interferometric coherence for matter linearly coupled to linearized gravity; derives exact factorization into Newtonian phase, graviton characteristic functional, and a commutator phase; identifies the Bargmann invariant’s phase as isolating the commutator term; evaluates bounds and experimental feasibility via radiation-reaction scaling and trapped-ion analog mapping.

Result: The Bargmann phase isolates a gravity-specific commutator term that is state-independent, Newtonian-free, and bounded by which-path decoherence; it is 60 orders of magnitude too small for direct gravitational detection but feasible at order unity in trapped-ion analog experiments.

Conclusion: The Bargmann invariant phase provides a rigorous, operationally testable quantum gravity signature—distinct from decoherence—that is fundamentally nonclassical and experimentally accessible via quantum analog simulators.

Abstract: We identify the phase of a Bargmann invariant of two matter-wave interferometers as a quantum gravity signature that cannot be reproduced by any branchwise-definite classical gravitational field. For matter coupled linearly to linearized gravity, the interferometric coherence factorizes exactly into a conservative Newtonian phase, the characteristic functional of the graviton state, and a commutator phase. For Wigner-positive graviton states, the first two are classically reproducible; therefore, decoherence alone cannot certify that gravity is quantized. The Bargmann phase isolates the commutator, which is independent of the graviton state and of all Newtonian interactions and is bounded by which-path decoherence. For gravity, this radiation-reaction effect is sixty orders of magnitude below experimental reach, but trapped-ion analogs can test it at order unity.

</details>


### [112] [From Specific Heat Bounds to Penrose-like Inequalities: A Unified Thermodynamic Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35264)
*Kai-Peng Lu,H. Lu,Run-Qiu Yang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper identifies a local-to-global thermodynamic mechanism unifying various Penrose-like inequalities for black holes under physically motivated assumptions, focusing on asymptotically flat and anti-de Sitter static, spherically or planar symmetric black holes/branes; it links local NEC-based thermodynamic constraints to global geometric bounds, with implications for holographic dual field theories, but does not claim full generality beyond the considered classes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify previously disconnected black hole inequalities—particularly Penrose-like ones—under a coherent local-to-global thermodynamic framework grounded in physical principles such as the Null Energy Condition (NEC) and holography.

Method: Analytical derivation of thermodynamic inequalities for static, spherically symmetric asymptotically flat black holes and generalizations to asymptotically Schwarzschild anti-de Sitter black branes; application of the Null Energy Condition (NEC) to establish a central integrated inequality; interpretation via holographic duality to infer monotonicity of normalized thermodynamic quantities in the boundary field theory.

Result: A unified thermodynamic perspective connecting local NEC-constrained thermodynamics to global geometric inequalities (e.g., Penrose-like bounds); explicit derivation of a central integrated inequality valid for static planar-symmetric asymptotically Schwarzschild AdS black branes; holographic implication of monotonic behavior of normalized thermodynamic quantities in the dual field theory.

Conclusion: Local thermodynamic constraints—especially those implied by the NEC—can structurally underpin global geometric inequalities for black holes; while proven rigorously only for restricted symmetry classes (static, spherical/planar), this reveals a deeper structural link between local physics and global geometry, motivating further investigation beyond these cases.

Abstract: Under explicit and physically motivated assumptions, we identify a local-to-global thermodynamic mechanism that organizes several previously disconnected inequalities on black holes. We first focus on asymptotically flat, static, and spherically-symmetric black holes, and provide a unified thermodynamic perspective on various Penrose-like inequalities. We also discuss the generalizations in asymptotically Schwarzschild anti-de Sitter black holes/branes. In holographic settings, these bounds imply monotonic behavior of normalized thermodynamic quantities in the dual field theory. Notably, one central integrated inequality can be established directly under the NEC in general static planar symmetric asymptotically Schwarzschild anti-de Sitter black branes. Our purpose is not to claim a fully general proof of the proposed local bounds, but to expose a unified structural perspective connecting local thermodynamic constraints to global geometric inequalities. Establishing the local bounds beyond the classes considered here remains an open problem.

</details>


### [113] [Gravitational-Wave Signatures and Periodic Orbits of Charged Particles Around a Five-Dimensional Rotating Black Hole: Comparison with the Kerr Black Hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35386)
*Bahromjon Shokirov,Tursunali Xamidov,Chen Zhou,Sanjar Shaymatov,Chengxun Yuan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates periodic orbits and gravitational wave emission from charged particles orbiting a 5D rotating Myers–Perry black hole in a uniform magnetic field, comparing results with the 4D Kerr case; it finds weaker binding, shallower potential wells, altered orbital frequencies, lower-amplitude and lower-frequency GW signals, and reduced LISA SNR for the 5D case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how higher-dimensional gravity (specifically D=5 Myers–Perry BHs) modifies charged particle dynamics and gravitational-wave signatures in external magnetic fields—relevant for testing extra dimensions via extreme mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) observable by LISA.

Method: Analytical derivation of equatorial circular and periodic orbits for charged particles in the D=5 Myers–Perry metric with uniform magnetic field; numerical kludge method to compute gravitational waveforms; comparison with Kerr BH in identical magnetic field; radiation reaction lifetime estimation and LISA signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) calculation.

Result: Charged particles are less bound in D=5 (ISCO energy ~0.95–0.99 vs. 0.78–0.94 in Kerr); potential well is several times shallower; orbits lie closer to separatrix; magnetic field induces frequency inversions (Ω_φ < Ω_r) and distant loops; GW period longer, frequencies lower (e.g., strongest line at 7.9 mHz vs. 9.9 mHz), amplitude ~18% smaller; EMRI lifetimes range from <1 hr to few days; LISA SNR reduced (7–31 vs. 15–54 for Kerr).

Conclusion: The D=5 Myers–Perry black hole in a magnetic field yields qualitatively and quantitatively distinct orbital dynamics and gravitational-wave signatures compared to Kerr, implying that LISA observations of EMRIs could constrain or detect higher-dimensional gravity—if astrophysical magnetic fields and charge-to-mass ratios are favorable.

Abstract: We study periodic orbits of charged particles in the equatorial plane of a five-dimensional ($D=5$) rotating Myers--Perry black hole (BH) immersed in a uniform magnetic field, and the gravitational waves emitted by these orbits in an extreme mass-ratio inspiral. All results are compared with the Kerr BH in the uniform magnetic field. We find that a charged particle is much more weakly bound to the $D=5$ dimensional BH, i.e. its energy at the innermost stable circular orbit is close to unity, $0.95$--$0.99$, compared with $0.78$--$0.94$ for the Kerr BH, and the depth of the potential well is several times smaller. As a result, the periodic orbits of the $D=5$ dimensional BH lie much closer to the separatrix. The magnetic field can make the azimuthal frequency of the orbit smaller than the radial one and produces loops in the orbits far from the BH. Using the numerical kludge method, we compute the gravitational waveforms for a compact object of $10M_\odot$ orbiting a BH of $10^6M_\odot$. At the same magnetic field, the $D=5$ dimensional signal has a longer period, lower frequencies, and an amplitude smaller by about $18\%$. We show that for the magnetic parameter $\beta M=0.02$ the strongest spectral line is at $7.9$\,mHz, compared with $9.9$\,mHz for the Kerr BH. An estimate of the radiation reaction shows that the periodic orbits survive from less than an hour to a few days in the presence of an external magnetic field. Over these lifetimes, the signal-to-noise ratio for LISA is $7$--$31$ for the rotating BH in $D=5$ dimensions, compared to the Kerr BH where it is about $15$--$54$.

</details>


### [114] [Phase-Space Constraints on a General Class of Eckart-like Viscous Cosmologies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35405)
*David R. Moreno,J. Bayron Orjuela-Quintana,Yeinzon Rodriguez*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes viscous dark matter cosmologies using dynamical systems methods, showing that viable background evolution closely mimics ΛCDM—requiring negligible viscosity in early times and converging to a cosmological constant-dominated attractor late-time; however, bulk viscosity significantly suppresses sub-horizon structure growth via scale-dependent dissipative drag, though it cannot resolve the H₀ tension.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether viscous dark matter models can provide viable cosmological backgrounds and potentially alleviate observational tensions (e.g., H₀) while remaining consistent with standard cosmological epochs (radiation-, matter-, and Λ-domination).

Method: Dynamical system analysis of viscous cosmologies in the Eckart formalism, parameterizing bulk viscous pressure as Ω_Π = −ξ̂₀ Ωₘˢ; examining fixed points, stability, physical viability (e.g., positivity of Ωₘ, reality of Ω_Π), and linear perturbations on sub-horizon scales.

Result: All physically viable models require Ω_Π ≈ 0 during radiation and matter eras and converge uniquely to the Λ-dominated fixed point; power-law models with s < 1 lead to unphysical (negative/complex) quantities at finite past time; sub-horizon perturbations exhibit suppressed growth due to viscosity-induced Hubble friction.

Conclusion: Viscous dark matter models cannot alter the background expansion meaningfully away from ΛCDM and thus fail to resolve the H₀ tension, but they introduce testable suppression of structure formation via scale-dependent dissipation.

Abstract: We study the cosmological dynamics of a general class of viscous dark matter models using a dynamical system analysis. In the Eckart formalism, these viscous cosmologies are characterized by a dimensionless bulk viscous pressure $\Omega_\Pi \equiv \Omega_\Pi(\Omega_m)$, where $\Omega_m$ is the matter density parameter. Our analysis demonstrates that a viable early radiation era requires the complete absence of viscosity, a proper matter-dominated epoch demands a small viscous pressure, and the only physically admissible late-time attractor is the cosmological constant-dominated fixed point; consequently, the background expansion must closely track standard $\Lambda$CDM evolution. We validate these general results using the power-law parameterization $\Omega_\Pi = -\hat{\xi}_0 \Omega_m^s$, where $\hat{\xi}_0$ and $s$ are real parameters; for $s \geq 1$ and $\hat{\xi}_0 \ll 1$, the model reproduces the $\Lambda$CDM background, while for $s < 1$ the system encounters a past-time integration boundary where the dark matter density transitions into negative values and its viscous pressure evaluates to nonphysical complex numbers. Since the background expansion essentially duplicates $\Lambda$CDM, the model is unlikely to resolve the $H_0$ tension. However, large differences arise at the linear perturbative level; indeed, on sub-horizon scales, bulk viscosity causes a scale-dependent dissipative drag on the matter density fluctuations; this increases the Hubble friction, offering a channel to suppress structure growth.

</details>


### [115] [Decoupled asymmetric thin-shell wormholes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35407)
*A. López-Hormazábal,Hishan Farfán-Bachiloglu,Kazuharu Bamba,Francisco Tello-Ortiz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs reflection-asymmetric thin-shell wormholes by gluing a Schwarzschild exterior to a geometrically deformed interior (a scalar–nonlinear-electrodynamics black hole), analyzing energy conditions, stability, and extending the model to rotating (Kerr) spacetimes with exact junction solutions and gravitational-wave estimates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore stable, physically viable thin-shell wormholes beyond symmetric configurations by leveraging gravitational decoupling to introduce controlled asymmetry while preserving key physical features (e.g., mass, redshift function) on one side.

Method: Uses minimal geometric deformation via gravitational decoupling to generate an asymmetric junction; applies Israel junction conditions; derives exact identities for radial null energy and momentum flux; analyzes linear stability with shell equation-of-state; extends to Kerr geometry with exact spin-dependent junction solution; models throat dynamics via free precession for GW estimation.

Result: 1) Total radial null energy (shell + bulk) is positive under sufficient deformation; 2) Momentum flux across shell equals bulk’s radial null-energy density; 3) Linear stability condition obtained in closed form; 4) Conventional shell stability requires bulk violation of radial null energy condition at throat—deformation alleviates this by shifting transition radius inside photon sphere; 5) Exact Kerr extension fixes angular momentum partition and reveals rotating-fluid behavior at shell; 6) Free-precession model yields qualitative GW signal estimate.

Conclusion: Gravitational decoupling enables construction of stable, asymmetric thin-shell wormholes without fine-tuned exotic matter at the throat; the deformation improves stability properties and allows consistent incorporation of rotation, offering a concrete framework for testing wormhole phenomenology—including gravitational-wave signatures.

Abstract: We construct reflection-asymmetric thin-shell wormholes by joining a Schwarzschild exterior to its minimal geometric deformation, which shares the same mass and redshift function, so that the asymmetry is carried entirely by the gravitational-decoupling sector. The deformed side is a black hole sourced by a scalar field coupled to nonlinear electrodynamics, in its branch with a real scalar. We show that the total radial null energy of shell and bulk obeys an exact identity and becomes positive for a sufficiently strong deformation. The momentum flux across the shell equals the radial null-energy density of the bulk, and retaining it yields the linear stability condition in closed form. We prove that stability with a conventional shell response requires the bulk to violate the radial null energy condition at the throat; the deformation instead enlarges the stable domain and moves the transition radius inside the photon sphere. For a stationary extension built on Kerr, the junction is solved exactly in the spin, which fixes how the angular momentum is shared between shell and bulk and where the shell behaves as a rotating fluid. A free-precession model provides an estimate of the gravitational-wave signal of the throat.

</details>


### [116] [Quasibound modes of rotating acoustic black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35493)
*H. S. Vieira,V. B. Bezerra*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a spherical-coordinate metric for rotating acoustic black holes and derives exact analytical solutions for scalar perturbations via the massless Klein–Gordon equation; it obtains closed-form quasibound state frequencies, enabling analysis of resonant, long-lived modes relevant to analog gravity experiments and gravitational black hole physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable exact analytical study of quasibound states—long-lived resonant modes—in rotating acoustic black holes, bridging theoretical analog gravity models with experimentally testable predictions and offering insights into wave dynamics, stability, and energy confinement analogous to gravitational black holes.

Method: Constructed a rotating acoustic black hole metric in spherical coordinates; solved the massless Klein–Gordon equation exactly in that background; imposed physically appropriate boundary conditions (ingoing at effective horizon, exponentially decaying at infinity) to isolate quasibound state modes; derived an exact analytical expression for their complex frequencies.

Result: An exact analytical formula for the quasibound state frequencies of scalar perturbations in rotating acoustic black holes in spherical coordinates, confirming existence of discrete, long-lived trapped modes with negative imaginary parts indicating exponential decay timescales.

Conclusion: Exact solvability in this geometric formulation establishes a rigorous foundation for probing horizon-related wave phenomena in laboratory-based analog systems; quasibound states serve as observable signatures linking analog experiments to fundamental aspects of black hole physics, including mode stability and spectral structure.

Abstract: In this paper, we introduce a metric describing rotating acoustic black holes in spherical coordinates. This formulation allows us to obtain exact analytical solutions of the massless Klein--Gordon equation governing scalar perturbations in the corresponding background spacetime. By imposing the appropriate boundary conditions, we derive an exact analytical expression for the quasibound state frequencies of the system. Quasibound states play a central role in the dynamics of black hole analog systems, as they characterize long-lived resonant modes trapped in the vicinity of the effective horizon. Their investigation provides valuable insights into wave propagation, mode stability, and energy confinement in analog spacetimes. Moreover, because acoustic black holes offer experimentally accessible realizations of horizon physics, the study of quasibound states may establish a direct connection between theoretical predictions and laboratory observations, thereby contributing to a deeper understanding of phenomena that also arise in gravitational black hole environments.

</details>


### [117] [Gravitationally Induced Polarization-Angle Rotation in Pulsar-Black Hole Binaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35589)
*Jyotijwal Debnath,Amodio Carleo,Delphine Perrodin,Pratik Tarafdar*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper models how gravitational light bending and frame-dragging from a black hole companion distort the pulsar polarization position angle (PPA) in a binary system, using the Rotating Vector Model combined with null geodesic integration; it finds significant S-curve distortion and PPA sense reversal near superior conjunction—especially for supermassive BHs—and demonstrates that relativistic spin-orbit coupling further modulates orbital-phase dependence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether relativistic photon propagation effects—gravitational light bending, frame dragging, and parallel transport—in pulsar–black hole binaries induce observable distortions in polarization position angle (PPA) profiles, potentially enabling geometric probes of such systems once discovered.

Method: Combines the Rotating Vector Model (RVM) with numerical integration of null geodesics in Kerr spacetime; computes polarization direction evolution along photon trajectories including parallel transport; models orbital-phase-dependent observed PPA for both Schwarzschild and Kerr black hole companions; incorporates relativistic spin-orbit coupling to evolve orbital geometry.

Result: Gravitational light bending strongly distorts the characteristic S-shaped PPA curve—especially near superior conjunction—and can reverse its overall sense of variation for supermassive BH systems; frame dragging adds an extra geometric shift; parallel transport induces an additional reference-frame-dependent polarization rotation; spin-orbit coupling modifies orbital-phase dependence of the PPA profile.

Conclusion: Relativistic photon propagation significantly alters both the shape and orbital-phase dependence of pulsar polarization profiles in BH binaries; these distortions are detectable in principle and could serve as novel geometric diagnostics for future pulsar–BH discoveries.

Abstract: Radio pulsars emit highly polarized radiation whose polarization properties are commonly described by the Rotating Vector Model (RVM). In this work, we investigate the effects of gravitational light bending and parallel transport on the polarization profile of a pulsar in a hypothetical binary system with a black hole (BH) companion. We combine the RVM with numerical integration of null geodesics to calculate the polarization direction along the photon trajectory and model the orbital-phase-dependent variation of the observed polarization position angle (PPA). We consider both non-spinning and spinning black hole companions and include the evolution of the orbital geometry due to relativistic spin-orbit coupling. For a non-spinning black hole, the change in the observed polarization direction arises from the geometric shift associated with gravitational light bending. For a spinning black hole, frame dragging introduces an additional geometric shift, while parallel transport produces an additional polarization rotation measured relative to our chosen reference construction. We investigate the dependence of these polarization changes on the binary parameters and the orientation of the pulsar spin. We find that gravitational light bending can significantly distort the characteristic S-shaped PPA profile, particularly near superior conjunction, and can reverse its overall sense of variation for the supermassive black hole system considered here. Relativistic spin-orbit evolution also affects the orbital-phase dependence of the polarization profile, particularly in the supermassive black hole system. These results show that relativistic photon propagation can modify both the shape and the phase dependence of pulsar polarization profiles and may provide a useful probe of the geometry of pulsar-BH binaries when such systems are discovered.

</details>


### [118] [Nonlinear Tides of Binary Neutron Stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35602)
*Guangzhou Guo,Zhen Pan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates nonlinear tidal effects in neutron-star binary inspirals, showing they significantly shift the fundamental oscillation mode frequency and induce measurable gravitational-wave phase shifts, especially in the late inspiral.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify how nonlinear tides—beyond standard linear tidal theory—affect stellar oscillations and gravitational-wave signals in neutron-star binaries, particularly for detectability with next-generation GW observatories.

Method: Combines high-resolution hydrodynamic simulations of inspiralling neutron-star binaries with a semi-analytic mode-coupling model to compute the quadrupolar fundamental-mode frequency shift up to second order in tidal strength.

Result: Nonlinear tides reduce the fundamental-mode frequency by ~10% in equal-mass binaries during late inspiral and introduce an O(1) radian GW phase shift beyond the linear tidal response; simulations and analytic model agree in weak-tide regime but diverge where perturbation theory fails.

Conclusion: Nonlinear tides produce observable imprints on GW waveforms and are a promising target for next-generation detectors, necessitating inclusion in waveform models for precision parameter estimation.

Abstract: Nonlinear tides couple stellar oscillations and reshape the gravitational wave (GW) signal from inspiralling binaries. We present hydrodynamic simulations of nonlinear tides in inspiralling neutron-star binaries and, within a mode-coupling model, derive a semi-analytic quadrupolar fundamental mode-frequency shift through second order in the tidal strength. The two approaches agree in the perturbative regime, but diverge at stronger tidal fields, where perturbation theory breaks down. In the late inspiral, nonlinear tides reduce the fundamental-mode frequency by ${\sim}10\%$ for equal-mass binaries and add a GW phase shift of $\mathcal{O}(1)$ radian beyond the linear tidal response. These effects make nonlinear tides a promising target for next-generation GW detectors.

</details>


<div id='hep-th'></div>

# hep-th [[Back]](#toc)

### [119] [Lifshitz Phase of Quantum Gravity: The Infrared Fixed Point of the (2+1)D Unimodular Hořava-Chern-Simons Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34024)
*Maxim Zelenko,Dam Thanh Son*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Proposes unimodular Hořava-Chern-Simons gravity in (2+1)D with w∞ gauge symmetry; exhibits asymptotic freedom and anisotropic scale invariance at large |κ|, with dynamical exponent z ≈ 2 − 3/(2|κ|); conjectures a confinement phase transition below a critical κ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a consistent nonrelativistic quantum gravity theory in (2+1) dimensions incorporating both Hořava-type Lifshitz scaling and topological Chern-Simons structure, while exploring its RG flow and possible phase structure.

Method: Field-theoretic analysis of a unimodular, nonrelativistic gravity model with w∞ gauge symmetry; computation of RG flow for the Chern-Simons level κ; derivation of the dynamical critical exponent z from scaling arguments and perturbative UV behavior.

Result: Identification of three dimensionless couplings; demonstration that at large |κ| the theory is asymptotically free and flows to an IR fixed point with anisotropic scale invariance and z = 2 − 3/(2|κ|); conjecture of a confinement phase transition at finite critical κ.

Conclusion: Unimodular Hořava-Chern-Simons gravity provides a novel framework for studying quantum gravity in (2+1)D with rich RG structure and potential confinement/deconfinement duality, meriting further investigation into its phase diagram and holographic interpretation.

Abstract: We consider a nonrelativistic gravity theory in (2+1) dimensions with $w_\infty$ gauge symmetry that we call unimodular Hořava-Chern-Simons gravity. The theory has three dimensionless couplings, one of which is the Chern-Simons level $\kappa$. At large $|\kappa|$, the theory flows from an asymptotically free UV fixed point to an IR fixed point with anisotropic scale invariance characterized by a dynamical critical exponent $z=2-\tfrac32|\kappa|^{-1}$. We speculate that unimodular Hořava-Chern-Simons gravity undergoes a phase transition from this scale-invariant phase to a confining phase when $\kappa$ drops below a critical value.

</details>


### [120] [Fixed Points of the Yang-Mills Gradient Flow in the Vortex Sector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33874)
*A. M. Silva*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the Yang-Mills gradient flow of center vortices in SU(2) gauge theory, showing that axially symmetric vortices evolve via linear diffusion, conserve flux, and converge toward Abelian cores with winding n mod 2; multiply wound vortices are unstable due to tachyonic modes, while the fundamental vortex is marginally stable and remains Abelian.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the stability and dynamical evolution of center vortices under Yang-Mills gradient flow — key objects in confinement scenarios — particularly how non-Abelian structure evolves and whether vortices decay to or stabilize as Abelian configurations.

Method: Analytical study of the Yang-Mills gradient flow for axially symmetric SU(2) center vortices; reduction to a linear diffusion equation for the radial profile; spectral analysis of off-diagonal perturbations via radial Schrödinger operators; variational computation of tachyonic modes; transition to self-similar variables to extract exact growth exponents; geometric interpretation via holonomy scaling and winding reduction.

Result: Axially symmetric vortices obey exact Gaussian diffusion with conserved flux; thin vortices are bounded fixed points but not attractors; all n ≥ 1 vortices possess tachyonic modes in their Abelian core, with growth exponents μ_{n,m} = n − 1 + m; only the n = 1 vortex is exactly marginal (stable); higher-winding vortices unwind transiently to n mod 2 Abelian cores; no static transverse vortex solutions exist; instability geometrically corresponds to shortening the SU(2) holonomy path, reducing winding by two.

Conclusion: The Yang-Mills gradient flow drives all center vortices toward Abelian configurations characterized by parity of winding number (n mod 2), with non-Abelian structure surviving only as a transient during topological unwinding; this supports the idea that Abelian dominance emerges dynamically in the infrared.

Abstract: We study the Yang-Mills gradient flow of center vortices in SU(2) gauge theory. For axially symmetric vortices the flow reduces exactly to a linear diffusion equation for the radial profile: a Gaussian core keeps its shape while its width grows as $\sqrt{R^2+4t}$, the flux is exactly conserved, and the thin vortex is the unique bounded fixed point, though not an attractor. Off-diagonal perturbations are governed by a pair of radial Schrödinger operators, and a variational computation shows that, frozen at any instant, the Abelian core has a tachyonic mode for every winding $n\geq 1$. Since the core spreads while the mode grows, we pass to self-similar variables, where the unstable modes are known in closed form, with growth exponents $\mu_{n,m}=n-1+m$. Multiply wound vortices are therefore unstable, while the fundamental vortex is exactly marginal and stays Abelian. A scaling argument rules out any static vortex in the transverse plane, and the instability acquires a simple geometric meaning that shortens the path traced in SU(2) by the holonomy of a growing loop, lowering the winding by two. The flow thus drives every vortex toward the Abelian core of winding $n \bmod 2$, with non-Abelian structure appearing only as the transient through which multiply wound cores unwind.

</details>


### [121] [One ring to rule them all: syzygy maximization for Argyres-Douglas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33151)
*Monica Jinwoo Kang,Craig Lawrie,Jaewon Song*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs affine schemes encoding Higgs branches and Macdonald indices for Argyres–Douglas theories D_p(ADE) with gcd(p, h∨)=1, using syzygy-maximizing invariant potentials from twisted periods on Coxeter orbit spaces; it links Jacobian rings to Zhu’s C₂-algebras of boundary admissible affine VOAs and their DS reductions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and geometrically realize the Higgs branches and Macdonald indices of Argyres–Douglas theories D_p(ADE), especially under OPE decoupling constraints, and to bridge 4d N=2 SCFT data with vertex operator algebra structures (e.g., Zhu's C₂-algebras).

Method: Constructs invariant polynomial potentials via polynomial twisted periods on Coxeter orbit spaces; analyzes their Jacobian rings, critical loci, jet scheme syzygies, and nilradical filtrations; restricts to Slodowy slices for nilpotent Higgsing; compares with Zhu's C₂-algebras of simple affine VOAs at boundary admissible levels and their quantum Drinfeld–Sokolov reductions.

Result: Obtains uniform affine scheme constructions for Higgs branches; derives closed determinant formulas for classical types and explicit potentials for E6, E7, E8; proves syzygy-maximization in first jet schemes; recovers reduced critical loci as Higgs branches; computes candidate Macdonald indices matching Schur and Hall–Littlewood limits; extends to nilpotently Higgsed theories; conjectures isomorphism between Jacobian rings and Zhu's C₂-algebras.

Conclusion: The Jacobian ring of a single syzygy-maximizing invariant potential fully encodes both the geometry of the Higgs branch and protected BPS index data (Macdonald) for D_p(ADE) theories; this provides strong evidence for deep connections between 4d N=2 SCFTs, singularity theory, and representation theory of affine VOAs.

Abstract: We propose a uniform construction of affine schemes encoding the Higgs branches and Macdonald indices of the Argyres--Douglas theories $\mathcal{D}_p(ADE)$, with $\gcd(p,h^\vee)=1$. The construction captures conjectural OPE decoupling relations via the Jacobian ring of a single invariant polynomial potential, obtained from a polynomial twisted period of the corresponding Coxeter orbit space. We show that these potentials are ``syzygy-maximizing'' in the first jet schemes and recover the Higgs branches as their reduced critical loci. We obtain closed determinant expressions for the classical types and explicit potentials for $E_6$, $E_7$, and $E_8$. Powers of the nilradical define an intrinsic filtration on the resulting Jacobian rings, from which we compute candidate Macdonald indices with the correct Schur and Hall--Littlewood limits, in the examples studied. Restricting the potentials to Slodowy slices extends the prescription to nilpotently Higgsed theories. We further conjecture that these Jacobian rings are isomorphic to Zhu's $C_2$-algebras of simple affine VOAs at boundary admissible levels and their quantum Drinfeld--Sokolov reductions.

</details>


### [122] [Exact lattice bosonization of the double-scaled $c=1$ matrix model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34376)
*Adwait Gaikwad,Gautam Mandal,Ajay Mohan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends an exact bosonization framework for matrix quantum mechanics to the double-scaled c=1 matrix model, interpreting entanglement entropy and fermion density correlators via a finite, non-uniform lattice boson with position-dependent spacing ∼ g_s(x) l_s; it yields finite results, unlike divergent collective-variable approaches, and links local observables to a dynamical string coupling scale while asymptotic S-matrix data depend on the fundamental string length l_s.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve divergences in collective variable theory for computing entanglement entropy and density correlators in the c=1 matrix model, and to clarify the physical meaning of short-distance scales—particularly whether EE and local observables are governed by the fundamental string length l_s or a dynamical, position-dependent scale g_s(x) l_s.

Method: Exact bosonization of the c=1 double-scaled matrix model into a lattice boson theory with non-uniform lattice spacing g_s(x) l_s; derivation of entanglement entropy and one/two-point functions of fermion density ρ(λ) directly from this lattice boson formalism; comparison with the standard collective variable (ρ(λ)-based) approach which is shown to produce UV divergences.

Result: Finite, explicit expressions for the c=1 entanglement entropy and ⟨ρ(λ)⟩, ⟨ρ(λ)ρ(λ′)⟩ correlators; demonstration that the collective variable theory yields divergent results for these quantities; identification of g_s(x) l_s as the effective short-distance cutoff for local observables, whereas the genus-by-genus S-matrix depends only on l_s; connection to D0-brane physics via the emergent non-uniform lattice structure.

Conclusion: The lattice boson description provides a UV-finite, physically transparent framework for local observables in the c=1 matrix model, revealing a hierarchy of scales: local physics (e.g., EE) is regulated by a dynamical string coupling g_s(x), while asymptotic scattering data probe the bare string scale l_s—suggesting a holographic or D-brane origin for the emergent lattice structure.

Abstract: In a recent paper by two of us, it has been shown that entanglement entropy (EE) of matrix quantum mechanics (or, equivalently, of a non-relativistic fermion model) has a natural interpretation in terms of an exact bosonization of the fermion theory in terms of a lattice boson with lattice spacing $\sim 1/N$ (where $N$ is the rank of the matrix, equal to the number of fermions). The theory of this lattice boson is distinct from a latticized version of the "collective variable'' theory which is formulated in terms of the fermion density $\rho(\lambda)$ ("collective variable''); it is, in fact, more closely related to the Wigner distribution of the fermions. In this paper, we develop this connection further for the $c=1$ double scaled matrix model. Even though $N$ becomes infinite, the double scaled theory is described by a boson on a finite and non-uniform lattice spacing, of order $\sim g_s(x) l_s$ (where $g_s(x) \equiv g_s {\rm cosech}^2(x/l_s)$ is a position dependent string coupling). We explicitly derive the $c=1$ EE, as well as the one and two-point functions of fermion density from the lattice boson. The results are explicitly finite, unlike the collective variable theory which is shown to yield divergent answers. Our results suggest that while local observables such as the EE and $\langle \rho(\lambda) \rangle$, are characterized by a unique short distance scale $g_s(x) l_s$, the asymptotic observables such as the S-matrix at every genus are characterized by a short distance scale $l_s$. We show how this arises and discuss a possible interpretation in terms of D0 brane physics.

</details>


### [123] [Theory and Cosmology of Biverse Volume-Normalized Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35384)
*Siqing Yu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This dissertation proposes 'biverse volume-normalized gravity', a new classical and quantum gravity framework that resolves the cosmological constant problem by embedding our (3+1)D universe as a boundary of a (4+1)D bulk, combining Tseytlin’s volume-normalized action with ghost-free massive gravity—thereby eliminating quantum divergences, justifying the second metric tensor geometrically, solving strong coupling, and predicting r = 0.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the 60-order-of-magnitude cosmological constant problem, while overcoming key shortcomings of prior approaches: Tseytlin's volume-normalized action (quantum loop divergence) and ghost-free massive gravity (ad hoc second metric, strong coupling).

Method: Extends Tseytlin's volume-normalized action by formulating gravity in a biverse setup: our (3+1)D universe is the boundary of a (4+1)D bulk; the volume normalization is generalized to the ratio of boundary-to-bulk spacetime volumes; the second metric of massive gravity emerges as the pullback of the bulk metric; ghost-free massive potential is consistently incorporated.

Result: Quantum gravity loops are suppressed in the vanishing boundary/bulk volume ratio limit; the second metric acquires a geometric origin; the strong coupling problem is resolved; consistency with established particle cosmology and cosmic evolution is maintained; nontrivial prediction: tensor-to-scalar ratio r ≡ 0.

Conclusion: Biverse volume-normalized gravity provides a self-consistent, UV-controllable framework unifying volume normalization and massive gravity, geometrically resolving foundational issues and yielding falsifiable predictions—most notably r = 0—while addressing the cosmological constant problem without fine-tuning.

Abstract: This dissertation expounds the biverse volume-normalized gravity, a novel theoretical framework of classical and quantum gravity that provides an unusual solution to the cosmological constant problem, the 60-degree-of-magnitude discrepancy between the value of the vacuum energy density in our universe predicted by particle physics and that implied by cosmological data. In the literature, there exist two interesting ideas: 1) to use the "volume-normalized" action functional, the usual action of the particle Standard Model and general relativity but divided by the spacetime volume, first proposed by Tseytlin; 2) to generate the vacuum energy density dynamically by assigning a ghost-free massive potential to the graviton. However, each has a problem: Tseytlin's attempt suffers from diverging quantum loops and the massive gravity theory has a second metric tensor with questionable origin and the quantum strong coupling problem. Inspired by these two ideas, as well as the AdS/CFT correspondence in string theory, the biverse volume-normalized gravity extends Tseytlin's idea by placing our (3+1)-dimensional universe on the boundary of a (4+1)-dimensional bulk space. This new adaptation controls the large quantum gravity loops in the limit of vanishing ratio of the spacetime volume of our universe to that of the other universe. The ghost-free massive gravity can then be seamlessly incorporated, not only that its otherwise nebulous second metric tensor appears naturally as the pullback of the bulk metric on the boundary, but its strong coupling problem also solved. The biverse volume-normalized gravity is also consistent with all tested models of particle cosmology and the evolution of the universe, and nontrivially predicts that the tensor-to-scalar ratio of our universe is exactly zero.

</details>


### [124] [Construction of holomorphic SCFTs with minimal index](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33165)
*Du Pei*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs thousands of holomorphic N=1 supersymmetric VOAs with Witten index 1 for central charge c = 288 and, more generally, constructs holomorphic N=1 VOAs of central charge c = 12k with near-minimal Witten index 24/gcd(24,k); it achieves this via ternary code extensions, transfer-matrix evaluation of indices, symmetric orbifolds, and AI-assisted computer search, while also proving structural results on ternary codes and their non-chiral doubles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the open question of existence of holomorphic N=1 VOAs with Witten index 1 for arbitrary positive central charge c, especially motivated by connections to TMF periodicity (c=288) and the Segal-Stolz-Teichner conjecture.

Method: Extension of Gaiotto–Johnson-Freyd’s ternary code approach; AI-driven computer search over ternary codes; transfer-matrix technique for efficient index computation; symmetric orbifold reduction (e.g., k=12,24 → k=6); use of Landau-Ginzburg orbifold correspondence to analyze non-chiral doubles.

Result: Thousands of holomorphic N=1 VOAs with c=288 and index 1 constructed; for every k ∈ ℤ⁺, holomorphic N=1 VOAs with c=12k and index 24/gcd(24,k) — conjectured minimal — explicitly built; new structural results on ternary code indices; proof that non-chiral doubles admit supersymmetric gapping.

Conclusion: Holomorphic N=1 VOAs with index 1 exist beyond c=288 (in abundance), and minimal-index theories exist for all c=12k; the ternary code framework, enhanced by computational and categorical techniques, provides a robust construction toolkit linking VOA theory, topology (TMF), and quantum field theory.

Abstract: It has been an open question whether there exist holomorphic VOAs with N=1 supersymmetry and Witten index 1 for any positive central charge c. The existence of such a theory with c=288 is closely related to the 576-periodicity of the TMF spectrum via the Segal-Stolz-Teichner conjecture. Here we report the construction of thousands of such theories by extending the approach initiated by Gaiotto and Johnson-Freyd via ternary codes. In addition, for every positive integer k, we also construct holomorphic N=1 VOAs of central charge c=12k with index 24/gcd(24,k) -- conjectured to be the minimum -- in a computer-aided search carried out by AI coding agents. We overcome the exponential-time barrier by using chains of smaller cells to build ternary codes, whose indices can then be evaluated via a transfer-matrix technique, and we use a symmetric orbifold construction to reduce the problem of k=12 and 24 to that of k=6. Along the way, we also establish several results on ternary codes and their indices. Using an observation relating codes and Landau-Ginzburg orbifold models, we argue that the non-chiral doubles of the ternary code VOAs can always be gapped supersymmetrically.

</details>


### [125] [The Free Scalar as a Thermal Envelope for Three-Dimensional CFTs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33179)
*Christopher P. Herzog,Andreas Stergiou*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper proposes and tests conjectures bounding the ratio of thermal entropy density to stress-tensor coefficient (c_S/C_T) in 3D unitary CFTs, with saturation by the free scalar; it generalizes this to a temperature-dependent hyperbolic heat-capacity bound on S¹×H² and links it to conformal-collider positivity near the conformal temperature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify universal thermodynamic bounds in 3D unitary CFTs rooted in fundamental principles like unitarity and causality, extending known constraints from conformal collider physics and probing connections between geometry, entropy, and operator content.

Method: Analytical derivation using conformal symmetry, modular Hamiltonian structure, and conformal-collider positivity; numerical testing across free theories, large-N interacting models, holographic duals (e.g., AdS₄ gravity), and Monte Carlo simulations of 3D Ising and O(N) critical models.

Result: The flat-space bound c_S/C_T ≤ 16πζ(3) holds across all tested theories, including numerics; the stronger hyperbolic heat-capacity bound is verified for T ≥ (2πR)⁻¹ in free, large-N, and holographic cases; near the conformal temperature, the bound follows rigorously from conformal-collider positivity under smoothness and absence of boundary terms in the modular Hamiltonian.

Conclusion: The conjectured bounds are strongly supported as universal features of 3D unitary CFTs, suggesting deep links between thermodynamics, geometry, and the conformal bootstrap; the free scalar saturates both bounds, indicating its extremal role in the CFT landscape.

Abstract: We conjecture that every unitary three-dimensional conformal field theory obeys $c_S/C_T\leq16\pi\zeta(3)$, where $s=c_ST^2$ is the thermal entropy density and $C_T$ the stress-tensor two-point coefficient, with equality for free scalars. We embed this in a stronger conjecture: on $S^1\times H^2$, the $C_T$-normalized hyperbolic heat capacity is bounded above by that of the free scalar for all $T\geq(2\pi R)^{-1}$. Near the conformal temperature, for theories smooth from the high-temperature side and without boundary terms in the modular Hamiltonian, the heat-capacity bound follows from conformal-collider positivity. We test the conjectures in free, interacting large-$N$, and holographic theories. Monte Carlo data for the three-dimensional Ising and $O(N)$ models also obey the flat-space bound.

</details>


### [126] [Spin-Induced Coordinate Noncommutativity of the Covariant Centroid](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33236)
*Daniel Guimaraes Tedesco,Luiz Paulo Colatto*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the Tulczyjew-Dixon centroid of a massive spinning particle, establishing its exact noncommutative coordinate algebra $\{X^\mu,X^\nu\} = -S^{\mu\nu}/p^2$ as foundational—not derived—and identifies the convex timelike interior $\Omega_\mu$ as the domain where the algebra is exactly closed; it introduces a two-versus-three operator criterion to determine validity of on-shell approximations (e.g., freezing $p^2$), showing such approximations preserve all two-operator commutators and key physical identities but fail at three insertions, yielding a Jacobiator residue proportional to the Pauli–Lubanski pseudovector; the analysis tracks propagation of this algebra through successive quantum-geometric constructions (positive-energy projection, Moyal fibers, holonomy, nonassociative products), confirming consistency only up to pairwise operations, with no new predictions beyond standard QED.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously define the domain of validity for the Tulczyjew-Dixon centroid's noncommutative position algebra—specifically, to resolve whether the known bracket $\{X^\mu,X^\nu\} = -S^{\mu\nu}/p^2$ can be consistently used beyond formal manipulation, and to replace qualitative 'on-shell' restrictions with a precise, algebraic criterion grounded in Jacobi identity closure.

Method: Composite realization of the centroid using canonical Poincaré variables; explicit computation of Poisson brackets and their operator analogues; analysis of Jacobi identity closure requiring $p^{-2}$ to remain operator-valued; derivation of the failure mode when $p^2$ is frozen to its mass-shell value; systematic layer-by-layer examination of quantum-geometric constructions (positive-energy projection, packet-conditioned Moyal star-products, Compton-scale holonomy, projected nonassociative products) against the two-versus-three commutator criterion.

Result: The exact coordinate algebra is valid only on the convex timelike interior $\Omega_\mu$, where single-mass-shell states are distributional; freezing $p^2$ preserves all two-operator commutators and associated physical identities (Berry cancellation, endpoint transversality), but fails at three operators, producing a nonzero Jacobiator $2\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W_\sigma/(p^2)^2$; all geometric constructions downstream remain consistent only up to pairwise operations, with no deviation from QED predictions.

Conclusion: The Tulczyjew-Dixon centroid’s noncommutativity is intrinsically tied to the operator nature of $p^2$; the two-versus-three criterion provides a sharp, algebraic demarcation for safe usage of on-shell approximations—rendering previous qualitative restrictions obsolete—and confirms that no observable beyond standard QED emerges from this geometric framework.

Abstract: The Tulczyjew-Dixon centroid of a massive spinning particle, built as a composite of canonical Poincaré variables, carries the exact coordinate algebra $\{X^\mu,X^\nu\}=-S^{\mu\nu}/p^2$, whose noncommutativity bivector is the transverse spin tensor of the particle itself rather than an external background. That bracket and its Compton-scale magnitude are already known, and we recover them here as the starting point of the analysis, not as a result. What the composite realization settles is the domain on which the algebra may be used. Closure of the Jacobi identity requires $p^{-2}$ to remain operator valued, and the exactness domain is the convex timelike interior $\Omega_\mu$, on which states supported on a single mass shell are distributional. We show that freezing $p^2$ to its shell value inside the centroid leaves every two-operator commutator unchanged on shell, so that the projected position dictionary, the Berry cancellation and the endpoint transversality identity are untouched, while the same substitution fails from three insertions onward, with residue the truncated Jacobiator $2\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W_\sigma/(p^2)^2$. This two-versus-three criterion replaces the qualitative restriction to shell-preserving matrix elements and states which constructions built on a fixed-shell bivector $\Theta_m$ remain valid. We then follow the algebra through positive-energy projection, packet-conditioned Moyal fibers, Compton routing holonomy and projected nonassociative products, recording for each layer whether it falls on the safe side of the criterion. No prediction beyond QED follows. In the geometric branch the physical amplitude reduces to the QED amplitude, and the transverse matching coefficients remain uncomputed.

</details>


### [127] [Berends-Giele currents for higher derivative gauge theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33693)
*Pratik Chattopadhyay*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives Berends-Giele currents for higher-derivative scalar QED and Yang-Mills theories, verifies Ward identities, confirms consistency between Feynman diagram and perturbiner methods, shows HDYM currents reduce to standard YM currents for massless gluons, and reveals novel higher-derivative effects via Merlin modes; it also discusses extensions to gravity and off-shell color-kinematics duality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct off-shell multi-particle currents (BG currents) in higher-derivative gauge theories—where standard on-shell amplitude methods fail—and to understand how higher-derivative operators affect gauge invariance, recursion, and physical amplitudes, especially with novel massive 'Merlin' states.

Method: Derives BG currents via Feynman diagrams from higher-derivative Lagrangians; verifies Ward identities at 2- and 3-point level; re-computes currents using perturbiner formalism for cross-check; applies BG recursion to HDYM and proves equivalence to pure YM for massless external legs; analyzes amplitudes involving Merlin modes using multi-particle currents.
