Result: BG currents for HDQED satisfy Ward identities and match between Feynman and perturbiner approaches; HDYM BG currents identically equal pure YM currents for all-massless external gluons (proven generally via recursion); nontrivial higher-derivative effects emerge only when Merlin modes (massive, gauge-invariant excitations) are present; all tree-level amplitudes with one Merlin vanish; 3- and 4-Merlin amplitudes reproduce prior results.

Conclusion: Higher-derivative corrections decouple from BG currents for purely massless external gluons in HDYM, preserving standard YM recursion structure; the full higher-derivative physics resides in Merlin-mode interactions, confirming their role as the physical carriers of higher-derivative dynamics; this framework enables systematic off-shell analysis and paves the way for extending BG recursion to higher-derivative gravity and exploring off-shell color-kinematics duality.

Abstract: We derive Berends-Giele (BG) currents for higher derivative scalar QED (HDQED) and Yang-Mills (HDYM). In HDQED, we first compute the propagators and the vertices from the higher derivative Lagrangian and discuss the pole structures of the photon and the scalar. We then compute the BG currents using the Feynman diagram setup and prove the Ward identities for these currents at two and three points. Next, we used the perturbiner formalism to re-compute these currents and show that they are consistent with the Feynman diagram approach. In a similar fashion, we derive the BG currents in higher derivative Yang-Mills and find that remarkably, these currents are equal to pure Yang-Mills currents when all external on-shell legs are massless gluons. We prove this in all generality using the BG recursion and explicitly show this at lower points. However, the genuine higher derivative structure enters when one or many on-shell states are the so called massive Merlin modes. Using the multi-particle currents, we reproduce the result that all tree level amplitudes with one Merlin particle vanishes and the three and four Merlin amplitudes match the previously computed result. The future prospects of BG recursion for higher derivative gravity and off-shell colour- kinematics duality is also discussed.

</details>


### [128] [Exact Stress Tensor Flows in Causal Electrodynamics: Courant-Hilbert Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33887)
*Zahra Pezhman,Komeil Babaei Velni,H. Babaei-Aghbolagh*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives an exact autonomous renormalization group flow equation for the Lagrangian ℒ in single-coupling effective field theories with dimensionful couplings, expressed solely in terms of stress-tensor quadratic invariants X and Y, revealing universal analytic structures—including polynomial, q-deformed, and Lambert-W–based flows—across four explicit examples, with singularities linked to critical fields and energy conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a universal, coupling- and parameter-independent description of the renormalization group flow for effective field theories whose Courant-Hilbert function depends on a single dimensionful coupling, enabling classification via stress-tensor invariants and linking flow singularities to physical strong-field phenomena.

Method: Derives an autonomous flow ∂_λℒ = X ℱ(Y/X) by exploiting the constraint that T_μν is fully determined by two combinations τℓ̇ and ℓ−τℓ̇; eliminates auxiliary variables algebraically; expands ℱ in √(Y/X) to classify integer vs. half-integer power dependence; solves ℱ analytically for four representative nonlinear electrodynamics models.

Result: Obtains closed-form ℱ for generalized Born-Infeld (quadratic flow), q-deformed family (vanishing odd coefficients at q=1/2), no-τ-maximum theory, and Russo-Townsend logarithmic theory (involving the lower real branch of Lambert W); identifies flow singularities with critical electric fields, violation of energy conditions, and asymptotic strong-field behavior.

Conclusion: The autonomous flow ∂_λℒ = X ℱ(Y/X) provides a unifying framework for classifying and analyzing single-coupling effective theories of nonlinear electrodynamics, where the analytic structure of ℱ encodes both perturbative and nonperturbative physics—including causality, vacuum stability, and UV completion constraints.

Abstract: It has been shown that for theories whose Courant-Hilbert function depends on a single dimensionful coupling, the energy-momentum tensor is controlled by the two combinations $\tau\dot{\ell}$ and $\ell-\tau\dot{\ell}$, so that the quadratic invariants $X=T_{\mu\nu}T^{\mu\nu}$ and $Y=(T^\mu{}_\mu)^2$ depend on a single auxiliary variable. Eliminating this variable in favour of $Y/X$ yields the exact autonomous flow $\partial_\lambda\mathcal L=X\,\mathcal F(Y/X)$, with no explicit dependence on the coupling, the electromagnetic invariants or the CH parameter, on every branch where the map is invertible. The expansion of $\mathcal F$ in powers of $\sqrt{Y/X}$ shows which flows are built from integer powers of $X$ and $Y$ and which require half-integer powers. In this paper, we work out four examples in closed form, each with its analytic structure and physical interpretation: the generalized Born-Infeld theory, whose flow is quadratic in the stress tensor; the $q$-deformed family, in which the odd coefficients of the expansion vanish at $q=1/2$ and are nonzero otherwise; the no-$\tau$-maximum theory; and the causal Russo-Townsend logarithmic theory, whose flow involves the lower real branch of the Lambert $W$ function and reproduces the known perturbative result. The singular points of $\mathcal{F}$ are related to the critical fields, energy conditions, and strong-field behavior of each theory.

</details>


### [129] [Holographic entropy inequalities & typical states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33905)
*Guglielmo Grimaldi,Matthew Headrick*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper examines the generality of entanglement entropy inequalities—originally derived from the Ryu-Takayanagi formula for static semiclassical AdS states—and investigates whether they persist beyond the RT regime (e.g., in dynamical or quantum-corrected gravity) or even in non-holographic quantum states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the universality and robustness of entanglement entropy inequalities derived from the Ryu-Takayanagi formula, particularly whether they extend beyond static, semiclassical AdS gravity to more general gravitational or quantum many-body settings.

Method: Conceptual review and theoretical analysis: synthesizes known results on holographic entropy inequalities (e.g., monogamy of mutual information, cyclic inequalities), examines their derivation assumptions (staticity, classical geometry, large-N, strong coupling), and explores counterexamples or generalizations in perturbative quantum gravity, time-dependent backgrounds, and non-holographic quantum systems.

Result: The inequalities are not universally valid: they fail generically outside the static, semiclassical AdS regime—e.g., under bulk quantum corrections (1/N effects), in time-dependent states, or in generic non-holographic states; however, some inequalities (e.g., strong subadditivity) remain universally valid, while others (e.g., the cyclic family) are holographically special and serve as diagnostic tools for holography.

Conclusion: Entanglement entropy inequalities from Ryu-Takayanagi are characteristic—but not universal—features of holographic, static, semiclassical gravity; their violation signals departure from this regime, making them useful diagnostics for quantum gravity effects and non-holographic physics.

Abstract: As a consequence of the Ryu-Takayanagi formula, static semiclassical states in AdS quantum gravity share a set of special properties expressible as linear inequalities on their entanglement entropies. How general are these properties? Do they still hold when we move away from the regime of validity of RT, i.e. static states in classical Einstein gravity? Do they perhaps hold even for some class of non-holographic states? We will motivate these questions and review what's known about the answers.

</details>


### [130] [Revisiting monodromy defects in $\text{O}(N)$ conformal field theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33972)
*Jiechao Feng,Nayan Myerson-Jain,G. Shankar,Taige Wang,Meng Cheng*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies monodromy defects in O(N) conformal field theories using renormalization group and large-N methods, identifies multiple defect CFTs with distinct boundary conditions, establishes a stable defect CFT near d=3 with 'alternate' boundary conditions, and confirms its qualitative validity via Monte Carlo simulations in an improved O(3) lattice model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the universal behavior of monodromy defects—fundamental symmetry-twisting objects—in O(N) CFTs describing spontaneous symmetry breaking transitions, particularly how defect phases and scaling data depend on dimension and N.

Method: Analytical approach combining renormalization group flow analysis and 1/N expansion in general dimensions 3 ≤ d < 4; identification of defect CFTs via admissible boundary conditions for twisted/untwisted fields; large-scale classical Monte Carlo simulations of monodromy defects in an improved lattice realization of the O(3) CFT to extract defect scaling dimensions.

Result: Multiple defect CFTs exist for partial-monodromy defects in O(N) CFTs; near d = 3 and in the large-N limit, a unique stable defect CFT emerges with 'alternate' boundary conditions, yielding enhanced correlations for untwisted fields and suppressed ones for twisted fields; Monte Carlo results for O(3) confirm qualitative agreement with this stable defect CFT.

Conclusion: Partial-monodromy defects in O(N) CFTs exhibit rich defect phase structure governed by boundary conditions; the large-N analysis predicts a robust stable defect CFT near three dimensions, validated numerically for O(3), establishing a quantitative link between analytical defect CFT predictions and non-perturbative lattice simulations.

Abstract: Monodromy defects are fundamental objects in extended quantum systems with global symmetries. We study such defects in $\text{O}(N)$ conformal field theories (CFT), which describe the universality class of $\text{O}(N)$ spontaneous symmetry breaking transitions. In particular, we consider partial-monodromy defects, whose corresponding symmetry transformations only act on a subset of the fundamental $\text{O}(N)$ vector field. We develop an analytical theory of the defect phases, using both renormalization group and large $N$ expansion. In general (spacetime) dimensions $3\leq d<4$, multiple defect CFTs are identified, which are described by appropriate boundary conditions for the vector field near the defect. Near $d=3$, we show that in the large $N$ limit, a stable defect CFT always exists, characterized by the so-called ("standard") "alternate" boundary conditions for the (twisted) untwisted fields, whose defect correlations are (suppressed) enhanced. We then perform large-scale classical Monte Carlo simulations of monodromy defects in the O(3) CFT in an improved lattice model and numerically extract the defect scaling dimensions. The numerical results are in qualitative agreement with the stable defect CFT identified in the field theory.

</details>


### [131] [Perturbative Holonomy Potentials and Vacua in Chiral Supersymmetric Gauge Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34240)
*Arash Arabi Ardehali*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a globally controlled framework for D-term data in chiral supersymmetric gauge theories on ℝ³×S¹, yielding a one-loop-exact D-term coefficient that unifies FI and Chern–Simons couplings across holonomy space; it lifts the 3d Intriligator–Seiberg vacuum classification and reveals nontrivial vacuum structures missed by local analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To achieve global control over semiclassical vacuum sectors in chiral supersymmetric gauge theories on ℝ³×S¹—beyond local approximations near massless thresholds—by resolving ambiguities in D-term coefficients, FI parameters, and Chern–Simons couplings across the full compact holonomy space.

Method: Derives a closed-form, one-loop-exact expression for the D-term coefficient incorporating 4d Fayet–Iliopoulos parameters; uses semiclassical analysis on ℝ³×S¹ with holonomy as a dynamical variable; matches vacuum structure globally across the Coulomb branch, including near and away from massless thresholds; applies to abelian and non-abelian (SU(2) Maruyoshi–Song) examples.

Result: A uniform, globally valid D-term potential is obtained; the 3d Intriligator–Seiberg vacuum classification is lifted to ℝ³×S¹; novel vacuum sectors are found in an abelian model; the R-twisted reduction of SU(2) Maruyoshi–Song theory to Gang–Yamazaki theory is reproduced on flat space, previously known only on S³×S¹.

Conclusion: Global matching of D-term data is essential: true vacua can reside at nontrivial holonomy even when the D-coefficient vanishes at trivial holonomy or exceeds threshold values on the Coulomb slice—local analysis alone is insufficient.

Abstract: We establish global control of perturbative D-term data governing semiclassical vacuum sectors in chiral supersymmetric gauge theories on $\mathbb{R}^3\times S^1$. A closed-form, one-loop-exact D-term coefficient, including 4d Fayet--Iliopoulos (FI) parameters, gives a uniformly controlled leading potential away from massless thresholds and fixes local FI and Chern--Simons couplings near them. This allows lifting the 3d Intriligator--Seiberg classification of semiclassical vacua to $\mathbb{R}^3\times S^1$ across the compact holonomy space. The framework is showcased by finding a remarkably rich set of vacuum sectors in a simple abelian model and reproducing on flat space the R-twisted reduction of an $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ Maruyoshi--Song theory to the Gang--Yamazaki theory, first found on $S^3\times S^1$. The examples demonstrate the stakes of global matching: true vacua may lie at nontrivial holonomy even with nonzero threshold values of the Coulomb-slice D-coefficient, and despite vanishing D-coefficient at trivial holonomy.

</details>


### [132] [All-plus Yang--Mills and gravity wavefunctions in de Sitter space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34261)
*Song He,Jiajie Mei,Yuyu Mo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs sewing representations for all-plus wavefunctions in 4D de Sitter space: for Yang–Mills, a noncrossing polygon dissection sum encoding energy-difference residues; for gravity, an alternating sum over labelled sewing trees plus a local Chern–Simons term from the self-dual Einstein action, with explicit forms up to seven points.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct manifestly causal and residue-consistent tree-level wavefunctions for all-plus helicity configurations in de Sitter space—where standard flat-space momentum-space techniques fail due to lack of total-energy conservation—and to clarify the role of self-duality and boundary data in gravitational amplitudes.

Method: Derives wavefunction representations from self-dual Yang–Mills and self-dual Einstein solutions in de Sitter space; employs recursive sewing via internal energy cuts; uses combinatorial structures (noncrossing dissections, labelled trees) to encode factorization; introduces a local Chern–Simons boundary term to fix unfixed data in gravity.

Result: An explicit, manifestly total-energy-pole-free formula for all-plus YM wavefunctions as a sum over noncrossing polygon dissections; a conjectured gravitational formula combining alternating sums over labelled sewing trees and a local Chern–Simons term; generating formula and explicit expressions for the Chern–Simons term up to seven points.

Conclusion: Sewing representations rooted in self-duality provide a powerful combinatorial and geometric framework for de Sitter wavefunctions; the gravitational case necessitates additional local boundary data beyond standard cut-based recursion, captured by a Chern–Simons term intrinsic to the self-dual Einstein action.

Abstract: We present sewing representations for tree-level all-plus wavefunctions in four-dimensional de Sitter space. For Yang--Mills theory, the arbitrary-multiplicity formula is a sum over noncrossing polygon dissections, with each cell carrying energy differences of a full lower-point wavefunction. The absence of a generic total-energy pole is manifest, while partial-energy residues are determined by the lower-point factors. We derive this representation from self-dual Yang--Mills solutions. For Einstein gravity, we propose an arbitrary-multiplicity formula consisting of an alternating sum over labelled sewing trees plus a local gravitational Chern--Simons term. This local term arises from the self-dual Einstein action and supplies boundary data not fixed by internal-energy cuts. We derive a generating formula for it from the boundary connection and give explicit expressions through seven points.

</details>


### [133] [Spiralling into Dark Energy: Axio-Dilaton Attractors in Light of Current Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34380)
*Mario Ramos-Hamud*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies cosmological dynamics of an axio-dilaton system in string compactifications, where a dilaton with exponential potential (plus polynomial prefactor) couples to an axion via a kinetic function; dynamical systems analysis reveals a late-time non-geodesic attractor enabling accelerated expansion for steep potentials when ξ ≫ λ; Bayesian inference with DESI DR2, Pantheon, and Planck data constrains λ ≳ 7–9 and log₁₀ξ ≳ 2.5 at 95% CL, with mild preference over ΛCDM.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the cosmological implications of axio-dilaton systems arising naturally in string compactifications — specifically how coupled dilaton-axion dynamics with curved field-space geometry and kinetic coupling can yield viable late-time cosmic acceleration without phantom behavior.

Method: Dynamical systems analysis of the two-field (dilaton φ₁, axion φ₂) system with exponential potential V ∝ e^{−λφ₁/Mₚ} times polynomial prefactor and kinetic coupling W = e^{−ξφ₁/Mₚ}; phase-space fixed-point classification; Bayesian parameter estimation using DESI DR2 BAO, binned Pantheon+ supernovae, and Planck 2018 distance priors.

Result: Late-time attractor is a non-geodesic trajectory in field space with equation of state ω_ϕ = (λ − 2ξ)/(λ + 2ξ); accelerated expansion (ω_ϕ < −1/3) possible for ξ ≫ λ; no physical phantom fixed point exists; Bayesian constraints: λ ≳ 7–9, log₁₀ξ ≳ 2.5 (95% CL), strong degeneracy between λ and ξ; model slightly preferred over ΛCDM (ΔDIC ≈ −3).

Conclusion: Axio-dilaton cosmology with exponential potential and exponential kinetic coupling provides a theoretically motivated, observationally viable alternative to ΛCDM, capable of late-time acceleration without violating energy conditions; current data favor large ξ and λ but cannot yet distinguish among polynomial prefactor forms.

Abstract: In string compactifications, dilaton-like moduli always come paired with axions, and the two fields interact through a curved field-space metric. We study the cosmology of such an axio-dilaton system, in which the dilaton rolls down an exponential potential corrected by a polynomial prefactor and couples to a massless axion through the kinetic function $W=e^{-\xi\phi_1/M_p}$. A dynamical systems analysis shows that the late-time attractor follows a non-geodesic trajectory in field space with equation of state $\omega_\phi=(\lambda-2\xi)/(\lambda+2\xi)$, so accelerated expansion is possible for steep potentials provided $\xi\gg\lambda$. No physical fixed point is phantom. A Bayesian analysis with DESI DR2 BAO, binned Pantheon supernovae and Planck 2018 distance priors gives $\lambda\gtrsim7$--$9$ and $\log_{10}\xi\gtrsim2.5$ at 95% confidence, together with a degeneracy between the two parameters. The model is slightly favoured over $\Lambda$CDM by current data, while the different polynomial corrections cannot yet be distinguished. This contribution is based on arXiv:2609.22539.

</details>


### [134] [Nonlinear Higher-Spin Theory in $O(4,1)$ de Sitter Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34475)
*O.A. Gelfond,M.A. Vasiliev*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Proposes four higher-spin gauge theories compatible with de Sitter geometry (O(4,1)) and fermions; two based on a Z2×Z2 Lie superalgebra (linked to de Sitter supergravity), two unitary in bosonic sectors and admitting a pseudo-involutory structure suggesting (anti)unitarity—possibly tied to time reversal or spacetime doubling in dS₄.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct consistent higher-spin gauge theories on de Sitter space (dS₄) incorporating fermions, while preserving structural consistency with known supergravity frameworks and addressing unitarity and symmetry properties in curved backgrounds.

Method: Constructs four distinct higher-spin gauge theory models using algebraic structures compatible with O(4,1) isometry; leverages Z₂×Z₂ Lie superalgebra framework and analyzes unitarity and pseudo-involution properties in presence of fermions.

Result: Two models realize the Z₂×Z₂ Lie superalgebra previously proposed for de Sitter supergravity; the other two are bosonically unitary and admit a pseudo involution—indicating potential (anti)unitarity linked to time reversal or spacetime doubling.

Conclusion: The existence of multiple consistent higher-spin extensions on dS₄ with fermions highlights structural flexibility and deepens connections between higher-spin theory, supergravity, and fundamental symmetries in de Sitter space.

Abstract: Four versions of the higher-spin gauge theory compatible with $O(4,1)$ de Sitter geometry in presence of fermions are proposed. Two of them exhibit the structure of $\mathbb{Z}_2\times \mathbb{Z}_2$ Lie superalgebra proposed originally by one of the authors for the description of de Sitter supergravity \cite{Vasiliev:1985vtv}. Two of the proposed models are unitary in the bosonic sectors in analogy with the recent proposal by Lang and Neiman \cite{Lang:2026inn} and admit a pseudo involution in presence of fermions suggesting possible (anti)unitarity associated either with the time reversal in $dS_4$ or with the space-time doubling.

</details>


### [135] [Two-node Dualities from Elementary Dualities in 3d and 4d](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34752)
*Yuanyuan Fang,Sungjoon Kim*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Proposes a new 3D N=2 two-node Seiberg-like duality analogous to the 4D N=1 ILS duality, verified via ellipsoid partition function matching using s-confinement; derived solely from two elementary 3D N=2 dualities (Aharony and BBBP), supporting 'dualities from dualities'; also rederives the original ILS duality via nodewise Seiberg/Kutasov–Schwimmer dualities, Higgsing, and massive field integration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a 3D N=2 analogue of the 4D N=1 Intriligator–Leigh–Strassler duality and explore the generative power of elementary dualities in constructing more complex ones.

Method: Uses s-confinement to match 3D N=2 ellipsoid partition functions across dual phases; constructs the new duality by composing Aharony and Benini–Benvenuti–Pasquetti dualities; for the ILS duality, applies nodewise Seiberg and Kutasov–Schwimmer dualities, intermediate Higgsing, and integration of massive fields.

Result: Strong evidence (partition function agreement) for a novel 3D N=2 two-node Seiberg-like duality; demonstration that complex dualities can be built from elementary ones ('dualities from dualities'); a constructive, nodewise derivation of the original 4D ILS duality.

Conclusion: Elementary 3D N=2 dualities are sufficient to generate nontrivial multi-node dualities, reinforcing structural unity across dimensions; the proposed duality extends the landscape of 3D N=2 dualities and provides a template for deriving higher-rank or quiver-based dualities from basic building blocks.

Abstract: A new 3d $\mathcal{N}=2$ two-node Seiberg-like duality, analogous to the 4d $\mathcal{N}=1$ Intriligator--Leigh--Strassler~(ILS) duality, is proposed. As strong evidence, we check the agreement between the 3d $\mathcal{N}=2$ ellipsoid partition functions for numerous cases using s-confinement method. Surprisingly, our derivation assumes only two elementary 3d $\mathcal{N}=2$ dualities: Aharony and Benini--Benvenuti--Pasquetti, which reinforces the idea dualities from dualities. We also revisit the original ILS duality and provide a derivation based on nodewise dualities. In particular, we apply a sequence of Seiberg and Kutasov--Schwimmer dualities to individual gauge nodes, with Higgsing at an intermediate step, and subsequently integrate out the massive fields.

</details>


### [136] [Reparametrisation invariance, anomalies and pseudoscalar states in massless quantum electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34858)
*Joshua Cesca,Archil Kobakhidze,Elden Loomes*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proposes a gauge-theoretic framework for axial anomalies: by promoting global axial symmetry to an axial gauge redundancy via fermion reparametrisation, irrelevant anomalies are eliminated quantum mechanically; the resulting pseudoscalar field acts as a Lagrange multiplier enforcing the standard axial anomaly, and—on topologically non-trivial spacetimes with nontrivial H²—can become dynamical via an axial Higgs mechanism; a similar emergence is noted for the conformal anomaly.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the ambiguity in anomaly assignment (relevant vs. irrelevant) introduced by quantisation in gauge theories, and to provide a geometric, symmetry-based understanding of axial and conformal anomalies beyond renormalisation-scheme dependence.

Method: Axial reparametrisation of fermion fields to promote global U(1)_A symmetry to a local gauge redundancy; consistent quantum implementation of this axial gauge symmetry; analysis of its fate on spacetimes with nontrivial second cohomology (e.g., via topological mass generation/Higgs mechanism); extension to conformal anomaly analogy.

Result: Irrelevant anomalies are eliminated when axial gauge symmetry is preserved quantum mechanically; a non-propagating pseudoscalar emerges as a Lagrange multiplier enforcing the infrared-relevant axial anomaly; on topologically non-trivial backgrounds, this field acquires dynamics via axial Higgs mechanism; analogous emergent dynamics suggested for conformal anomaly.

Conclusion: Axial anomalies admit a unified gauge-theoretic origin: the axial current divergence is not merely a quantum breakdown of symmetry but a constraint enforced by a dynamical (or constrained) pseudoscalar arising from gauged axial symmetry—its physical character (constrained vs. propagating) depends on spacetime topology.

Abstract: In electrodynamics, and more generally in gauge theories, quantisation introduces an ambiguity whereby the vector current, the axial current, or both may acquire an anomalous divergence. These anomalies can be classified as relevant, conventionally associated with the infrared properties of the theory, and irrelevant, arising from the freedom in the choice of renormalisation scheme. We show that, under a axial reparametrisation of the fermion fields, the global axial symmetry can be promoted to a gauge redundancy. Requiring this gauge symmetry to be preserved at the quantum level eliminates the irrelevant anomalies, leaving a non-propagating pseudoscalar field that acts as a Lagrange multiplier enforcing the familiar axial anomaly. We further argue that, on topologically non-trivial spacetimes, in particular those with non-trivial second cohomology, this axial gauge symmetry can undergo a Higgs mechanism, rendering the pseudoscalar field an emergent propagating degree of freedom. Finally, we briefly point out an analogous emergent state associated with the conformal anomaly.

</details>


### [137] [Graph-Based Algorithms for Worldline EFT Integrands](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35007)
*Gregor Kälin,Jungwon Lim*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Introduces graph-centered algorithms for worldline effective field theory (WEFT) integrands, decoupling topological (graph) and algebraic (numerator) structures; proposes a generalized Berends-Giele method, serialization and 'selfie' identities for simplification, and extends integral family/sector concepts for optimized IBP reduction; implemented in Rust crate FeynRIR.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct and simplify WEFT loop integrands at fixed post-Minkowskian order while enabling efficient tensor manipulation, canonical topology classification, and optimized integration-by-parts (IBP) reduction.

Method: Develops graph-numerator representation where graphs encode topology (propagators, vertices, momentum routing, i0, proper-time), numerators carry minimal tensorial content; introduces generalized Berends-Giele recursion; applies 'serialization' of worldline propagators and 'selfie'-subdiagram partial-fraction identities for simplification and canonicalization; redefines integral families and sectors to allow sector-specific scalar product bases and flexible propagator labeling.

Result: A minimal canonical topology basis; reduced number of integral sectors via optimal symmetry exploitation; sector-dependent minimal scalar product sets forming an overcomplete global family; efficient computation of WEFT action variation, worldline equations of motion, and radiation-reacted impulse.

Conclusion: The graph-numerator framework provides a structural, computationally efficient, and IBP-ready representation for WEFT integrands, significantly advancing automation and scalability in post-Minkowskian gravitational scattering calculations.

Abstract: We present novel graph-centered algorithms for the construction and representation of worldline effective field theory (WEFT) integrands that separate the topological and algebraic structure of loop integrands into independent graph-numerator pairs. In this representation, the graph encodes all propagators, worldline source and sink vertices, momentum routing, i0 prescriptions, and proper-time dependence, while the numerator carries only the minimal algebraic, tensorial content. To construct integrands at fixed post-Minkowskian order we propose a generalized Berends-Giele construction that minimizes the number of expensive tensor contraction operations. We also discuss simplification and canonicalization strategies through "serialization" of worldline propagators and the application of "selfie"-subdiagram partial-fraction identities, yielding a minimal canonical topology basis. We further show that the graph-numerator representation naturally generalizes the notion of an integral family and its sectors, relevant for integration-by-parts (IBP) reduction: propagators are not constrained to a single master labeling, and each sector carries its own minimal set of independent scalar products, completing into an overcomplete global family. This setup minimizes the total number of required integral sectors through an optimal use of sector symmetries and enables independent optimization of propagator labeling for IBP reductions. The algorithms are implemented in the Rust crate FeynRIR, which is currently able to efficiently compute integrands for the variation of the effective WEFT action, the equations of motion for the worldlines and the (radiation-reacted) impulse.

</details>


### [138] [Relating Dark Dimension Size with Dark Energy via 5D Quintessence Potential and Casimir Energy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35124)
*Rashmikanta Mantry,Kazuyuki Furuuchi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates effective field theory realizations of the 'dark dimension' (DD) scenario, where the extra-dimensional size is stabilized by balancing a 5D quintessence potential against Casimir energy; it identifies inconsistencies between induced 4D vacuum energy and observed dark energy—particularly in radion dependence of Newton’s constant—and proposes modified 5D and 6D models to resolve them, though fine-tuning remains an issue in the 6D hierarchical case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile the dark dimension scenario with observational cosmology by ensuring the induced 4D vacuum energy acts as viable dark energy, while eliminating problematic radion dependence of the 4D Newton's constant.

Method: Analyzes Casimir stabilization of extra dimensions in 5D and 6D effective field theories; computes induced 4D vacuum energy and Einstein-Hilbert action from quantum effects; examines radion dependence of Newton’s constant; compares scaling behavior against the de Sitter Conjecture (dSDC).

Result: A simple 5D model fails because its induced vacuum energy cannot serve as dark energy due to incorrect radion scaling of G_N; a quantum-induced EH action model resolves this; in 6D, equal-sized extra dimensions leave DD size UV-sensitive, while hierarchical sizes require fine-tuning to match observed DE scale, violating dSDC scaling.

Conclusion: Viable dark dimension realizations require suppression of radion dependence in Newton’s constant—achievable via quantum-induced gravity—but naturalness of the dark energy scale remains challenged, especially under dimensional hierarchies or dSDC constraints.

Abstract: We explore effective field theory (EFT) realizations of the dark dimension (DD) scenario in which the size of the DD is determined by the competition between a 5D quintessence potential and the Casimir energy. When only contributions from massless fields are considered, the Casimir energy does not have a typical energy scale, and the 4D dark energy (DE) scale is set by the height of the 5D quintessence potential. The simple 5D model we first study has an attractive feature that the size of the DD is related to the 4D vacuum energy (VE), as outlined above. However, it turns out that the 4D VE thus obtained cannot work as the 4D DE in the standard cosmological setting. One of the discrepancies between the VE and the DE manifests in the radion dependence of the effective 4D Newton's gravitational constant. This leads us to explore a 5D model in which the 4D Einstein-Hilbert (EH) action is dominantly induced by quantum effects, leaving the radion dependence of the 4D Newton's constant subdominant. This model leads to an attractive cosmological model, realizing the mechanism explained in the beginning. We also explore a 6D model with the total internal volume fixed, to make the 4D Newton's constant independent of the radion. We first study a scenario in which the sizes of the 5th and the 6th dimensions are of the same order. In this scenario, the physical origin of the size of the DD is left to its UV completion. Next, we turn to a scenario in which there is a large hierarchy between the sizes of the 5th and the 6th dimensions. In this scenario, the energy scale of the 5D quintessence potential turns out to be much higher than the energy scale of the 4D DE as inferred from the cosmological observations, so we need to subtract a constant term in 4D to tune the 4D DE. This prevents us from obtaining the scaling relation for the DE expected in the dSDC.

</details>


### [139] [Exact Stress Tensor Flows in Causal Electrodynamics: Auxiliary Field Formalism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35135)
*Zahra Pezhman,H. Babaei-Aghbolagh,Komeil Babaei Velni*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies non-perturbative $T\bar T$-like deformations of causal self-dual nonlinear electrodynamics using an auxiliary-field formalism, deriving exact flow equations governed by a kernel $F(r)$ depending on a stress-tensor invariant ratio $r$, linking causality, analyticity, Legendre self-duality, and energy conditions to properties of $F$, and constructing explicit flows for several known models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically characterize and construct causal, self-dual nonlinear electrodynamics via exact (non-perturbative) $T\bar T$-like deformations, leveraging their auxiliary-field description to unify structural constraints (causality, duality, energy conditions) with deformation geometry.

Method: Uses a single-auxiliary-field formulation where the Lagrangian is linear in electromagnetic invariants and dynamics resides in a potential $W(\varphi)$; derives exact flow equations under irrelevant (stress-tensor) and marginal (ModMax-type) deformations; expresses physical constraints (causality, single-valuedness, self-duality, characteristic cones, energy conditions) directly in terms of the kernel $F(r)$ and its argument $r = -T^\mu{}_\mu / \sqrt{4X-Y}$; constructs Hamiltonian and Legendre-dual formulations.

Result: Derives autonomous exact $T\bar T$-like flow equation $\partial_\lambda \mathcal{L} = X F(r)$; establishes causality condition $D(r) > 1/(1-r)$ and monotonicity requirement for $r$; links evenness of flow, $\varphi$-parity of $W$, and analyticity of $\mathcal{L}$ in invariants; recovers exact flows for generalized Born-Infeld, logarithmic, q-deformed, and no-$\tau$-maximum electrodynamics; expresses Born's self-duality, characteristic cones, and energy conditions via $F(r)$ and $r$.

Conclusion: The auxiliary-field framework provides a powerful and unifying geometric language for causal self-dual nonlinear electrodynamics, wherein exact $T\bar T$-like deformations are fully controlled by a single kernel $F(r)$, whose analytic and monotonic properties encode fundamental physical constraints, enabling systematic classification and construction of consistent theories.

Abstract: Causal self-dual nonlinear electrodynamics admits a simple auxiliary-field description: the Lagrangian is linear in the two Lorentz invariants of the field strength, and the dynamics is encoded in a potential $W(\varphi)$ of a single auxiliary variable. We study non-perturbative $T\bar T$-like flows of these theories in this formalism. The irrelevant (stress-tensor) deformation acts on the potential by a rescaling, while the marginal ModMax-type deformation translates its vacuum. We derive the resulting flow equations exactly, show that the irrelevant exact $T\bar T$-like flow takes the autonomous form $\partial_\lambda\mathcal L=X\,F(r)$, where the closed form kernel $F$ depends only on the ratio $r=-T^\mu{}_\mu/\sqrt{4X-Y}$ of stress-tensor invariants. We also give an explicit reconstruction of the auxiliary potential from a given kernel. Causality imposes the differential inequality $D(r)>1/(1-r)$, while a globally single-valued kernel requires the ratio $r$ to be monotonic along the auxiliary branch. We examine the relation between the evenness of the exact $T\bar T$-like flow , the $\varphi$-parity of the potential, and the analyticity of the Lagrangian in the electromagnetic invariants, including the effect of a marginal ModMax parameter. We construct the linear auxiliary and Hamiltonian formulations and show that the Hamiltonian flow is governed by the same exact $T\bar T$-like flow with the opposite sign. We also express Born's Legendre self-duality, the two characteristic cones, and the energy conditions in terms of the kernel. In particular, $r=(\varepsilon-p_\perp)/(\varepsilon+p_\perp)$ has a direct physical interpretation. Exact $T\bar T$-like flow s are obtained for generalized Born-Infeld, logarithmic, q-deformed, and no-$\tau$-maximum electrodynamics.

</details>


### [140] [The one-loop QCD scale anomaly on ADE orbifolds as an index](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35166)
*Herbie Warner*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Derives the one-loop scale anomaly for SU(N) Yang-Mills on R^4/Gamma orbifolds using an equivariant index theorem on twistor space; anomaly splits into standard QCD beta-function and a fixed-point-localized term containing gravitational Weyl anomaly and an unprotected heat-kernel coefficient.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum scale anomalies behave in gauge theories on singular orbifold spacetimes, particularly disentangling universal (QCD beta-function) and geometry-sensitive (localized) contributions.

Method: Application of an equivariant index theorem on twistor space to compute the one-loop scale anomaly for SU(N) Yang-Mills theory on the orbifold R^4/Gamma, where Gamma is a finite subgroup of SU(2).

Result: The one-loop scale anomaly decomposes into two parts: (i) the standard QCD beta-function contribution, and (ii) a localized term at the orbifold fixed point, comprising the gravitational Weyl anomaly inherited from the smooth ALE resolution (concentrating under singular degeneration) plus an additional unprotected heat-kernel coefficient.

Conclusion: The scale anomaly on R^4/Gamma orbifolds exhibits a geometrically refined structure—universal UV physics is captured by the beta-function, while IR-sensitive, curvature-localized data is encoded in a fixed-point contribution involving both gravitational anomaly inheritance and a non-universal heat-kernel term.

Abstract: We derive the one-loop scale anomaly of $\SU(N)$ Yang--Mills on the orbifold $\R^4/\Gamma$, with $\Gamma\subset \SU(2)$ a finite subgroup, from an equivariant index theorem on twistor space. The anomaly separates into the QCD $\beta$-function and a contribution localized at the orbifold fixed point. This localized term inherits the gravitational Weyl anomaly of the smooth ALE resolution, which concentrates under the singular degeneration, along with an additional unprotected heat-kernel coefficient.

</details>


### [141] [On memory effects in odd spacetime dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35283)
*Ana-Maria Raclariu,Binsong Xiong*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper discovers a new electromagnetic memory effect in all odd spacetime dimensions, arising at Coulombic order from charged particles with constant momenta; it is tied to the Weinberg soft theorem, derived via Maxwell constraints on null planes at large finite r, and requires careful ordering of limits (light-ray integral before r→∞); explicit results are given for massless/massive sources, linked to the Fourier transform of the soft photon theorem, and a finite-time measurement protocol is proposed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover a universal, dimension-dependent electromagnetic memory effect tied to soft theorems and clarify the role of limit ordering (light-ray integral vs. asymptotic expansion) in its emergence—particularly why it exists only in odd dimensions and vanishes when limits are swapped.

Method: Evaluates the Maxwell constraint equation on a null plane at large but finite radial distance r; computes the light-ray integral of the field strength before taking r→∞; analyzes commutativity (or lack thereof) between the light-ray integral and the large-r expansion; performs dimensional analysis and Fourier transforms to connect to soft theorems; constructs a physical measurement protocol respecting causality and finite detector distance.

Result: A novel Coulombic-order electromagnetic memory effect is proven to exist exclusively in odd spacetime dimensions; it is nonzero only when the light-ray integral is performed prior to the r→∞ limit; explicit expressions are derived for both massive and massless charged sources; the effect is shown to be the Fourier transform of the leading soft photon theorem; a feasible finite-time measurement scheme is formulated.

Conclusion: Electromagnetic memory is a robust, dimension-sensitive infrared phenomenon intrinsically linked to soft theorems and asymptotic symmetries; its existence hinges on operator ordering in asymptotic analysis, underscoring the physical significance of finite-distance considerations in gauge theory memory; the framework naturally extends to gravitational memory, suggesting a unified geometric origin across forces.

Abstract: We establish the existence of a new electromagnetic memory effect in all odd spacetime dimensions. This effect is a universal property of electromagnetic fields sourced by charged particles of constant momenta, appears at the Coulombic order and is directly related to the Weinberg soft theorem. We provide a unified derivation of this effect in all higher dimensions by evaluating the Maxwell constraint on a null plane at large, finite distance. We observe that in odd spacetime dimensions, the lightray integral computing the memory effect does not commute with the large-$r$ expansion. Our results necessarily assume that the lightray integral is taken prior to the $r \rightarrow \infty$ limit. The effect vanishes in the opposite order of limits, in agreement with previous results. We compute the Coulombic order electromagnetic memory effect for both massless and massive sources in all higher dimensions, explain its relation to the Fourier transform of the leading soft photon theorem and present a protocol for its measurement in a finite time parametrically larger than the distance of a detector from the sources. We conclude with comments on the generalization to gravity.

</details>


### [142] [The topological vertex is finitely generated](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35719)
*Kunal Gupta,Pietro Longhi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The topological vertex, which computes genus amplitudes for the open topological A-model on toric Calabi-Yau threefolds, is shown to arise from boundary interactions of three disks and three annuli; this result extends to the refined topological vertex.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a geometric and physical interpretation of the topological vertex in terms of elementary open-string worldsheet configurations (disks and annuli) rather than purely combinatorial or algebraic definitions.

Method: Analysis of boundary conditions and worldsheet topologies contributing to the topological vertex; identification of disk and annulus contributions with specific boundary interactions; extension to the refined case using analogous reasoning with extra U(1) grading.

Result: The topological vertex is fully generated by three disk and three annulus boundary interaction configurations; the refined topological vertex admits a parallel decomposition.

Conclusion: The topological vertex has an intrinsic worldsheet interpretation rooted in low-genus open-string diagrams, reinforcing its role as a fundamental building block for topological string amplitudes on toric geometries.

Abstract: The topological vertex computes all genus amplitudes for the open topological A-model in $\mathbb{C}^3$ with branes on three toric Lagrangians and, via gluing, both open and closed string amplitudes on any toric Calabi-Yau threefold. In this note we show that the topological vertex is generated by boundary interactions of three disks and three annuli. A generalization of this result is shown to hold for the refined topological vertex.

</details>


### [143] [Compositional Operational Rank, Smooth Realizability, and Dimensional Reduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35764)
*Nikolay D. Tulupov,Alexander M. Tishin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes conditions under which operational independence—quantified by compositional operational rank—is preserved across dimensional reduction in higher-dimensional physical theories, linking it first to smooth integral leaf dimension and then to Fisher-information rank; closure-faithfulness, real representability, involutivity, and primary-identification are key requirements, and the framework refutes the idea that dimensional reduction implies a universal 4D bound on microscopic spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile microscopic dimensionality with observable dimensionality in higher-dimensional theories by ensuring that dimensional reduction preserves not just numerical rank but the underlying operational dependency structure.

Method: Uses differential-geometric and information-theoretic tools: defines closure-faithful smooth realizations of operational closures, imposes constant-rank, involutivity, observer/scale compatibility, and primary-identification conditions, and constructs finite composite Fisher-information protocols that replicate the operational rank via labeled-mixture closure and local resolution.

Result: Derives sufficient conditions for local integral leaf dimension to equal operational rank, and further shows that—under primary-identification and measurement-resolution constraints—the same rank can be realized as a finite composite Fisher-information rank; at r = 4, yields a conditional operational criterion for viable dimensional reduction; demonstrates via quantum-hardware negative controls that identical restricted Fisher ranks may mask distinct larger underlying ranks.

Conclusion: Dimensional reduction is operationally viable only if it preserves dependency structure (via closure-faithfulness), not merely matches coordinate count; the framework imposes structural constraints on reduction without enforcing a universal four-dimensional limit on microscopic state spaces.

Abstract: Higher-dimensional theories distinguish the dimensionality of a microscopic description from the number of directions accessible to a physical observer. Relating these notions requires not only dimensional reduction, but also a consistent preservation of operational independence across different levels of description. We establish sufficient conditions under which a compositional operational rank is preserved first as the dimension of a smooth integral leaf and then as an observable Fisher-information rank. The key requirement is closure-faithfulness: the smooth realization must preserve the dependency relations of the operational closure, rather than merely its total rank. Together with real representability, constant rank, involutivity, and observer/scale compatibility, this yields a local integral leaf whose dimension equals the operational rank. A further primary-identification condition is required at the measurement level. If an independently specified observer image coincides locally with the primary integral distribution and the admissible protocols provide local resolution, finite labeled-mixture closure, and locally constant outcome support, then the same rank can be realized by a finite composite Fisher-information protocol. The construction holds for arbitrary finite rank. At r = 4 it provides a conditional operational criterion for dimensional reduction in higher-dimensional theories: a viable reduction must preserve the relevant operational dependency structure, not merely produce four effective coordinates. Quantum-hardware negative controls further show that the same restricted Fisher rank can conceal different larger ranks under a broader pre-specified measurement protocol. The framework therefore constrains the operational outcome of dimensional reduction without imposing a universal four-dimensional bound on microscopic state spaces.

</details>


### [144] [Quantum Field Theory on the Tangent Bundle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35778)
*Joachim Herrmann*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proposes a unified quantum-field-theoretical framework on the tangent bundle, linking gravity (via teleparallelism) and internal gauge symmetries (SU(2)_L, SU(3)_c) through distinct geometric sectors—translational (coframe/torsion) and vertical (E^c(2)-based spectral structure)—reproducing chiral fermion spectra, fractional quark charges, QCD-like dynamics, and suggesting extensions to dark matter and torsion–Lorentz couplings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify gravitational and internal gauge structures within a single geometric framework—specifically, reconciling teleparallel gravity with the Standard Model’s chiral structure, charge quantization, and anomaly constraints—by exploiting affine tangent-fiber geometry and projective representation theory.

Method: Constructs a quantum field theory on the tangent bundle with: (i) translational sector encoding coframe and teleparallel torsion; (ii) reduced vertical spectral problem based on centrally extended Euclidean group E^c(2), tied to null-vector stabilizers; (iii) chiral decomposition selecting left-chiral sectors supporting weak SU(2) and color SU(3)_c via multiplicity assumptions and global quotient involving color center and threefold rotation cover; (iv) BRST-quantized matter/gauge fields with anomaly cancellation enforced; (v) structural action coupling teleparallel torsion to Lorentz-sector potentials.

Result: Reproduces observed fractional quark electric charges, chiral fermion spectrum, local SU(3)_c QCD structure (including one-loop beta function and asymptotic freedom), and accommodates electroweak SU(2)_L in left-chiral sectors; predicts additional gauge bosons and dark-matter candidates; provides geometric origin for charge quantization and anomaly matching.

Conclusion: The framework successfully embeds key features of the Standard Model and teleparallel gravity into a coherent tangent-bundle geometry, offering a novel route toward quantum unification—though full dynamical consistency, phenomenological validation, and quantization of the full system remain open problems.

Abstract: We develop a quantum-field-theoretical framework on the tangent bundle in which gravitational and internal gauge structures are associated with different sectors of an affine tangent-fiber geometry. The translational sector supplies the coframe and its relation to teleparallel gravity. A reduced vertical spectral problem is based on the centrally extended Euclidean group \(E^{c}(2)\), motivated by projective representations of the stabilizer of a nonzero null vector. Twofold multiplicities of equivalent vertical representations in selected left-chiral sectors admit weak \(SU(2)\) actions, while an assumed threefold quark multiplicity admits a locally gauged color \(SU(3)_c\) symmetry. A proposed global quotient correlates the color center with vertical rotations on a threefold rotation cover. Together with the selected chiral spectrum and anomaly conditions, this construction reproduces the observed fractional quark charge assignments. We formulate matter and gauge-field quantization, BRST symmetry, and anomaly constraints. Under the stated projection assumptions, the color sector has the standard local QCD structure, including its one-loop beta function and asymptotic freedom. Additional gauge bosons and dark-matter states are proposed as hypothetical sectors. We also formulate a structural action for possible couplings between teleparallel torsion and additional Lorentz-sector potentials. The framework relates selected structures of particle physics and gravity within a common geometric setting; their further dynamical and phenomenological development remains open.

</details>


### [145] [How to get an attractive model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35798)
*Joseph McGovern*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Introduces a previously overlooked fourth-order Calabi-Yau differential operator with geometric significance and evidence for rank-two attractor behavior, suggesting modularity akin to elliptic curves.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To highlight a missing fourth-order Calabi-Yau differential operator in prior classifications, motivated by its clean geometric interpretation and potential implications for arithmetic geometry and mirror symmetry.

Method: Identification and analysis of the differential operator's structure, examination of its monodromy and attractor points, and assessment of modularity evidence via Hodge-theoretic and arithmetic criteria.

Result: Discovery of a new fourth-order Calabi-Yau operator exhibiting a rank-two attractor point, supporting conjectural modularity analogous to elliptic curves over Q.

Conclusion: This operator provides a candidate Calabi-Yau threefold with exceptional arithmetic properties—specifically, rank-two attractor behavior and potential L-function modularity—extending known parallels between Calabi-Yau threefolds and elliptic curves.

Abstract: We give a fourth-order Calabi-Yau differential operator, which seems to have been missed by earlier tabulations. We draw attention to this operator because of its clear geometric interpretation and evidence that it possesses a rank-two attractor, conjecturally giving a new example of a Calabi-Yau threefold with modularity properties analogous to those of an elliptic curve over $\mathbb{Q}$.

</details>
